# Unequal-middle modules: a full-cone physical obstruction

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926. Worker max4. Analytical invocation74.
Actual ended baseline73; ended ceiling74 / attempted ceiling75; cap1.
Steering: TASK.md and master_guidance d3e800bc68686d6e.
Requested transport/model regime: direct reasoning, consultOFF, Astra/ultra.

STATUS: ONE JOINT MATHEMATICAL DRAFT, ENTIRELY UNVERIFIED.
No assertion below is a new fact. No fact_submit, other formal call, mathematical
program, executed trajectory/recurrence, arithmetic replay, optimizer, consult,
installation, or subagent was used. The original minimax target remains OPEN.

This invocation opens ONE new candidate law, with unequal module levels, and
stops retuning it after deriving its declared-domain obstruction. It does not
reopen the old equal-basic F/M/E layout. The full previously shared exclusion
eea0512960806ccc was read for awareness only and is not a proof premise.

## 1. Original problem and the precise scoped output

For every integer n>=0 and h in [0,infinity)^n, W_n(h) is the supremum of

  2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L ||x_0-x_star||^2)

over every finite dimension, every convex differentiable function with globally
L-Lipschitz gradient and an attained minimum, every minimizer x_star, and every
x_0 different from x_star. The actual iteration is
x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). L>0 is arbitrary. Thus W=2R.

The original all-integer-split benchmark is

  Omega(c,d)=(c+d+sqrt((c+d)^2+4cd))/2,
  G(c,d)=2c+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8cd)/2,
  C_0=B_0=1,
  C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
  B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),       n>=1,
  U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

The project asks whether W_n^*/U_n^ZJ tends to1, uniformly in the lower-bound
direction over all nonnegative words. A negative answer would require an ACTUAL
family with a full-class upper bound below U_n^ZJ by a fixed factor at unbounded
actual horizons. No such upper bound is supplied here.

The candidate exclusion proposed in this joint draft is instead:

For EVERY integer m>=24, EVERY finite a,b>=0, and EVERY physical word described
in Section2, one explicitly selected static function in dimension1 or2, with
complete minimum set and OWN closest initial distance1, has terminal payoff V
satisfying

  B_N V > 1+1/40000,       N=2^(m+2).                       (1)

Consequently W_N(h)>(1+1/40000)U_N^ZJ for this entire scoped family.
In particular, the FULL original-coordinate objective certificate system has
no feasible solution at ANY level q_cert<=(1+1/40000)/B_N on this family.
This is a candidate physical obstruction to a negative construction, NOT a
negative resolution or an unrestricted affirmative result.

All new benchmark estimates, reductions, functions, trajectories and inequalities
are derived together below. No unverified intermediate is imported by citation.

## 2. The ONE physical law and its full block-sum scope

Put lambda=1+sqrt2 and A=lambda^m. Define the actual basic words by

  q_0=(),
  q_(j+1)=(q_j, 1+(lambda-2)lambda^j, q_j),     j>=0.

Induction gives length(q_j)=2^j-1 and sum(q_j)=lambda^j-1:
the next sum is 2(lambda^j-1)+1+(lambda-2)lambda^j=lambda^(j+1)-1.
All these entries are positive.

The explicit candidate is

  H_m(a,b)=(q_m, 1+aA, q_(m+1), 1+bA, q_m, 1).            (2)

The middle block has level m+1, whereas the outside basic blocks have level m.
This is not one of the old three-equal-basic-module F/M/E words. Expanding the
middle recursion prints four level-m lists with the bridge pattern

  (q_m,1+aA,q_m,1+(lambda-2)A,q_m,1+bA,q_m,1).

Both displays specify the same literal physical word. No child is an oracle,
and there is no restart, momentum, adaptation, averaging, or extra state.

The actual horizon and sum are exactly

  N=(2^m-1)+1+(2^(m+1)-1)+1+2^m=2^(m+2),
  S=sum H_m=(2+lambda+a+b)A.                               (3)

In fact the proof covers the following entire block-sum class, which contains
(2). Write h=(L0,1+aA,L1,1+bA,L2), with EVERY entry of the three real lists
nonnegative, retaining their literal positions, and require

  length L0=2^m-1,        sum L0=A-1;
  length L1=2^(m+1)-1,    sum L1=lambda A-1;
  length L2=2^m,          sum L2=A.                        (4)

For (2), L0=q_m, L1=q_(m+1), L2=(q_m,1).
There is no restriction on internal order, individual positive lower bounds,
or an upper bound independent of m. Zero entries, repeated query positions,
and entries as large as an entire block mass are included. The exterior a,b
may be arbitrarily large and may depend on m. They are selected before the
objective and initialization.

The original zero-based bridge queries are

  j1=2^m-1,      j2=3*2^m-1;

the final block begins at original query 3*2^m and ends at N=4*2^m.
All terminal formulas below refer to this N, not to either bridge or to N-1.

The extension from (2) to (4) is not a second candidate schedule law or an
assumed module invariance. It follows directly from every physical query
formula: only nonnegative partial sums and the three fixed total sums enter.

## 3. Accepted inputs and retrieval scope

Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the original ordered interpolation
orientation, exact full objective-certificate existence, and its selected
terminal-square parameterization, INCLUDING zero-step formal-coordinate
handling. Its full proof was read.

Accepted factb98bb807d514e80d supplies the legal global-Huber/single-cut
necessary screen with W=2R. Its full statement and proof were read. The actual
functions and all physical query formulas are reprinted here.

Accepted fact04219554e41d9750 fixes the same ORIGINAL benchmark and contains
full original-space composition identities. The relevant identities and
benchmark arguments, as well as the full fact text, were read. No conditional
improving seed is presumed, and no saved numerical endpoint or program in that
fact is executed. Section5 gives its needed benchmark bound directly from
the displayed defining recurrence; it does not use a stored finite DP table.

The former draft eea0512960806ccc was fully read in own-file bounded sections.
It is awareness, not an accepted predecessor. The frozen earlier prototype
7d434affa1888c02 was neither changed nor used as a mathematical premise.

Search-math-results was used in mutation mode for the mature unequal-module
upper-certificate program. One Matlas query returned bounded-step/self-contracted
and unrelated optimization results; none supplied the required full long-step
certificate. Primary-source web retrieval recovered the original concatenation
literature. Exact OWN extracted text
downloads/zhang-jiang-2410.12395.txt was read at Definitions4,5,7, Theorem6 and
its proof, Proposition8 and its proof, Theorems11,12, and Lemma15 INCLUDING its
proof. Its primitive/dominant assumptions require actual all-function Lyapunov
inequalities; a free oversized bridge is not licensed by the theorem. In
particular the positive denominator sum(right)+2-bridge in Lemma15 is an
explicit hypothesis, not a globally valid algebraic simplification.

No external theorem is a black-box premise of the new proof. The literature
is used for construction context; accepted facts and the derivations below
provide all mathematical support.

## 4. Complete canonical upper certificate, with no deleted directions

This is the full system attacked, not a displayed feasible multiplier array.
The physical obstruction will prove its infeasibility at the claimed improving
levels, so an improving array cannot honestly be supplied for this law.

Normalize L=1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Let I={star,0,...,N}. Retain all formal
vectors x_0,g_0,...,g_N independently, INCLUDING those at zero steps.
For each finite i define

  x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,
  Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

There is a separate variable Lambda_ij for EVERY ordered distinct pair in I.
Put Lambda_ii=0. The full sign and flow conditions are

  Lambda_ij>=0,                          every i!=j,
  sum_j Lambda_ij-sum_j Lambda_ji=2*1_(i=N),    0<=i<=N.

The star flow is then -2 by summing finite flows. All (N+2)(N+1) ordered
multipliers are retained. Define standard formal-coordinate columns
E_x,E_0,...,E_N in R^(N+2), E_star=0,

  X_i=E_x-sum_(t<i)h_t E_t,       X_star=0,
  K_ij=sym(E_i (X_j-X_i)^T)+(E_i-E_j)(E_i-E_j)^T/2,
  M(q_cert,Lambda)=q_cert E_x E_x^T+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij K_ij,       (5)

where sym(Z)=(Z+Z^T)/2. The full certificate requirement is PSD of (5)
on the ENTIRE R^(N+2), not merely on a trajectory subspace, quotient, common
gradient complement, or endpoint subspace. Initial, terminal, common and
singular directions are all present. Flow cancels the value terms in

  q_cert||x_0||^2-2f_N+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij.                  (6)

For completeness, the exact selected parameterization from accepted
fact1252b8823bb5a290 may also be used WITHOUT assuming its support of every
optimum. Set J={star,0,...,N-1}; choose alpha_j>=0 summing to1 and all ordered
nonnegative prefix multipliers lambda^pre_ij. Require finite prefix flow
out_i^pre-in_i^pre=2alpha_i. Put

  y_j=x_j-g_j, delta_j=f_j-||g_j||^2/2, y_star=delta_star=0,
  V_alpha=x_N-sum_(j in J)alpha_j y_j,
  R_alpha=q_cert||x_0||^2-2sum_(j in J)alpha_j delta_j
          -||V_alpha||^2+sum_(i!=j in J)lambda^pre_ij Q_ij.

Then Lambda_(N,j)=2alpha_j, Lambda_(j,N)=0 yields the identity

  (6)=||g_N-V_alpha||^2+R_alpha.                               (7)

Full PSD is equivalent to PSD of the ENTIRE prefix form R_alpha. Every prefix
pair remains. No two-point support or zero remainder is assumed. We make no
claim that these restrictive choices would be lossless at earlier query cuts.

Direct upper attempt1 was standard primitive/dominant gluing with all child
displacement terms retained. Its known bridge formulas alone do not certify
the arbitrary a,b here or an improvement over the original maximizing tree.
Direct upper attempt2 was the unrestricted system (5)-(7). The exact primal
screen below refutes the desired level even in that larger system. This
second failure is a physical obstruction, not a failed sparse-array guess.

## 5. A textual benchmark lower bound at the ACTUAL horizon

We derive, for every m>=24,

  B_(2^(m+2)) > (27/2)lambda^m.                               (8)

All recurrences in this section are symbolic definitions and induction
arguments. None was numerically evaluated or executed.

Balanced primitive splits give C_(2^j-1)>=lambda^j. The admissible dominant
split into two children of length 2^(j-1)-1, together with monotonicity of G,
therefore gives a scalar lower comparison. Let

  t_0=1,
  T(t)=(t+sqrt(t^2+8))/(2sqrt(lambda)),
  t_j=T(t_(j-1)),
  t_inf=sqrt(2/(lambda-sqrt(lambda))).

Then t_inf is a fixed point of T, since
(lambda-sqrt(lambda))*t_inf^2=2. It lies between1 and2.
For example lambda>12/5, sqrt(lambda)<8/5 imply
lambda-sqrt(lambda)>4/5, so t_inf^2<5/2<4;
lambda<5/2 and sqrt(lambda)>3/2 imply t_inf^2>2>1.

T is increasing, T(1)=2/sqrt(lambda)>1, and

  0<T'(t)=(1+t/sqrt(t^2+8))/(2sqrt(lambda))<1/sqrt(lambda)<2/3

for t>=0. Thus 1<=t_j<t_inf and
0<t_inf-t_j<(2/3)^j for j>=1, with the weak corresponding bound at j=0.
Induction using sqrt(G(1,t^2))=(t+sqrt(t^2+8))/2 proves

  B_(2^j-1)>=lambda^j t_j^2,
  t_j^2>t_inf^2-4(2/3)^j.                                   (9)

For exact radical bounds,
1207/500<lambda<483/200 and sqrt(lambda)>1553/1000.
Indeed 707^2<2*500^2, 283^2>2*200^2, and
1553^2/1000000=2411809/1000000<1207/500.
Using lambda^2=2lambda+1,

  lambda^2 t_inf^2=2lambda^2/(lambda-sqrt(lambda))
                   > (1457/125)/(431/500)
                   =5828/431>338/25.                       (10)

The last rational comparison is 145700>145678. Also lambda^2<6.
The original benchmark is strictly increasing:
B_(n+1)>=G(C_0,B_n)=G(1,B_n)>B_n.

Put j=m+2. Equations(9)-(10) therefore give

  B_(2^(m+2))/A
    >338/25-24(2/3)^(m+2)
    >338/25-24/10000
    >27/2.

Here (2/3)^6=64/729<1/10, so (2/3)^24<1/10000, and m+2>=26.
This proves(8), at the ACTUAL horizon. It needs neither a numerical value of
a phase constant nor a benchmark upper bound. Furthermore

  1/A<1/10000000,       every m>=24,                         (11)

because lambda>2 and 2^24=16777216>10000000.

## 6. Necessary screens and a fixed selector on the ENTIRE bridge quadrant

Define the three coefficient screens

  H0=1/[2(2+lambda+a+b)],
  H1=a^2/[2(lambda+1+b)],
  H2=b^2/[2(1+lambda+a)^2],
  q_screen=2000/26999.                                     (12)

They are used to select a function, not asserted to equal W_N.
Use the following priority rule, determined from the whole word before the run:

  if H0>=q_screen, choose the global interval law;
  else if H1>=q_screen, choose the first-bridge interval law;
  else if H2>=q_screen, choose the second-bridge interval law;
  otherwise choose the planar law of Section8.

Every equality boundary is assigned to an interval law. Zero a or b is
included. Arbitrarily large coefficients are included.

The EXACT physical scalar payoffs are

  V0=1/[2(2+lambda+a+b)A+1],
  V1=a^2/[1+2(lambda+1+b)A],
  V2=b^2/[(1+lambda+a)^2(1+2A)].                            (13)

To check the indices, the first bridge has 1+P_j1=A and
T_j1=(lambda+1+b)A; the second has
1+P_j2=(1+lambda+a)A and T_j2=A.
These formulas retain every physical entry of every block.

Multiplication by A gives, for k=0,1,2,

  A Vk >= Hk/(1+1/A).                                      (14)

The only corrections are the displayed +1 terms in(13); the relevant leading
denominators are all at least1. If an interval law is selected, (8),(11),(14)
give

  B_N Vk > (27000/26999)/(1+1/A)
          > (1+1/27000)(1-1/10000000)
          > 1+1/30000.                                    (15)

For the last comparison,
1/27000-1/30000=1/270000 exceeds
(1+1/27000)/10000000<1/5000000.
This is uniform even for unbounded a,b.

If all three scalar screens fail, then

  a+b>18999/4000-lambda>7/3,
  a^2<(4000/26999)(lambda+1+b),
  b^2<(4000/26999)(1+lambda+a)^2.                          (16)

For the sum, lambda<483/200 makes the right side exceed9339/4000>7/3.
Since 4000/26999<3/20<(39/100)^2 and lambda<29/12, (16) implies

  b<(39/100)(41/12+a),
  a>1201/1668>18/25.

Indeed substitution into a+b>7/3 gives the first lower bound on a,
and 1201*25=30025>30024=18*1668.
It also implies a<9/10: otherwise
a^2<(3/20)[(139/100)(41/12)+(39/100)a].
At a=9/10 the left side minus the right is1799/40000>0, and its
derivative 2a-117/2000 is positive for every a>=9/10.
Finally b>7/3-9/10=43/30 and
b<(39/100)(41/12+9/10)=3367/2000<17/10.

It suffices to prove the planar law on the LARGER closed domain

  18/25<=a<=9/10, 43/30<=b<=17/10, a+b>=7/3.               (17)

No scalar boundary or parameter-dependent large-m threshold is lost.

## 7. The interval law: ONE static objective and every physical step

For real ell<=0<u put I=[ell,u] and

  F_I(x)=max_(g in I) (xg-g^2/2),       x_0=1.              (18)

Its gradient is the projection onto I. Equivalently,

  F_I(x)=ell*x-ell^2/2,   x<=ell;
          x^2/2,         ell<=x<=u;
          u*x-u^2/2,     x>=u.

Thus F_I is finite, convex, C1, globally1-smooth, and nonquadratic.
The least global Lipschitz constant of its gradient is1.

For the global interval law, set ell=0, u=1/(1+2S).
Its COMPLETE minimum set is (-infinity,0], its minimum is0, and the
unique closest point to x_0=1 is0. At EVERY original query i and at N,
write c_i=sum_(t<i)h_t. Then

  x_i=1-u c_i, g_i=u,
  y_i=x_i-g_i=1-u(c_i+1)>=0,
  f_i=u*x_i-u^2/2.                                        (19)

The inequality follows from c_i<=S and u(S+1)<=1.
The literal update x_i-h_i u=1-u(c_i+h_i) proves induction through the
entire word, even at zero entries. At N, 2f_N=V0 from(13).

For either selected-cut law, let j=j1 or j2; put
P_j=sum_(i<j)h_i, T_j=sum_(i>j)h_i and

  u=1/(1+P_j),
  d_minus=u(h_j-1)/(1+2T_j), ell=-d_minus.                  (20)

The selected cut has h_j>1, so both u,d_minus are positive. The COMPLETE
minimum set is now {0}, and the actual closest initial distance is1.

For i<=j, with p_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, the exact jets are

  x_i=1-u p_i>=u, g_i=u, y_i=x_i-u>=0,
  f_i=u*x_i-u^2/2.

At j, x_j=u and the actual bridge sends it to

  x_(j+1)=-d_minus(1+2T_j).

For every suffix query and for N put w_i=sum_(j<t<i)h_t. Then

  x_i=-d_minus(1+2T_j)+d_minus w_i<=-d_minus,
  g_i=-d_minus, y_i=x_i+d_minus<=0,
  f_i=-d_minus*x_i-d_minus^2/2.                            (21)

All suffix partial sums lie in[0,T_j]. Each actual update increases w_i
by the literal nonnegative h_i and preserves the displayed projection.
The terminal is x_N=-d_minus(1+T_j), with payoff
2f_N=d_minus^2(1+2T_j)=Vj from(13).

Thus these are whole-run static objectives, not hypothetical independent
block witnesses. The supports are ell*y,u*y,0 at every sample, and the
declared endpoint wins all comparisons by the displayed sign of y.

## 8. The planar law: explicit slopes, function, and complete minimum set

For parameters in(17) define

  eta=1/10000, d=lambda+eta, k=b/2,
  D0=lambda+b-a*b^2/(2lambda), D=D0+1/1000,
  sigma=a/D, theta=a^2/(dD),
  v=(-sigma,sqrt(theta-sigma^2)),
  Pvec=e1/A, Qvec=v/A, Rvec=-k v/A,
  K=conv{0,Pvec,Qvec,Rvec},
  F_K(x)=max_(g in K) (<x,g>-||g||^2/2), x_0=e1.           (22)

The ambient space is ordinary Euclidean R^2, e1=(1,0).
All four support offsets are0 and their coefficients are1.
There is no hidden proximal metric or function-value shift.

Since ab<=153/100 and lambda>2, ab/(2lambda)<1/2.
Consequently D0>lambda+b/2>lambda+43/60>d,
theta-sigma^2=a^2(D-d)/(dD^2)>0, and
k sigma=ak/D<1. The radicand is positive throughout the closed domain.
The hull is a nondegenerate triangle;0 is retained on its Qvec--Rvec edge.

For completeness, the global regularity does not require an interpolation
existence oracle. Completing the square proves that the unique maximizer in
(22) is g=P_K(x). With y=x-g, the necessary and sufficient projection condition is

  <y,z-g><=0,                 EVERY z in K.                 (23)

Necessity follows by differentiating squared distance along each segment from
g to z. Sufficiency follows from
||x-z||^2-||x-g||^2=||z-g||^2-2<y,z-g>.
Checking the four displayed vertices suffices for their whole convex hull.

At a projected sample,

  F_K(x)=<y,g>+||g||^2/2.                                 (24)

Adding the projection inequalities for two points gives
<x-x',g-g'>>=||g-g'||^2, hence ||g-g'||<=||x-x'||.
The two defining attained maxima give
0<=F_K(x+h)-F_K(x)-<g,h><=<g'-g,h><=||h||^2.
It follows that F_K is differentiable with continuous1-Lipschitz gradient g.
It is finite because K is compact and convex as a supremum of affine functions.
Projection is the identity on the nonempty interior of K, so the least global
gradient Lipschitz constant is exactly1. Its gradient is constant on open
exterior sectors, so the function is nonquadratic.

The COMPLETE attained minimum set is the polar cone

  Cmin={x:<Pvec,x><=0,<Qvec,x><=0,<Rvec,x><=0}
      ={x:x1<=0, -sigma*x1+sqrt(theta-sigma^2)*x2=0}.        (25)

Every such x gives F_K(x)=0. Conversely, if a vertex z has positive <x,z>,
a sufficiently small positive multiple of z lies in K and makes the maximand
strictly positive. Thus(25) is exact, not just a selected minimizer set.
It is a ray, and zero is its unique closest point to e1:
for x in Cmin, ||e1-x||^2=(1-x1)^2+x2^2>=1, with equality only at0.
The OWN closest initial radius is exactly1.

## 9. All first-block and positive-height middle-block queries

FIRST BLOCK: at each L0 query and at its following bridge, let c be the
already executed L0 mass, 0<=c<=A-1. Then

  x(c)=e1-c Pvec, g(c)=Pvec,
  y(c)=[1-(c+1)/A]e1.

Put H=<Pvec,y(c)>>=0. The four support heights are

  P height H, Q height -sigma H, R height k sigma H, 0 height0.

Because k sigma<1, Pvec wins all comparisons, including the endpoint
y=0. Thus(23) proves every listed jet. Its value is
f(c)=1/A-(c+1/2)/A^2. Each literal step subtracts h_i Pvec and increments c
by h_i. At j1, x=Pvec, and the ACTUAL first bridge gives

  XQ=Pvec-(1+aA)Pvec=-a e1.                               (26)

MIDDLE BLOCK: at every L1 query and the second-bridge query, let s1 be the
already executed L1 mass, 0<=s1<=lambda A-1, and z=(s1+1)/A<=lambda.
The exact candidate jets are

  x(s1)=XQ-s1 Qvec, g(s1)=Qvec,
  y(s1)=-a e1-z v,
  f(s1)=(a sigma-z theta)/A+theta/(2A^2).                  (27)

Their four support heights are

  HQ=(a sigma-z theta)/A=theta(d-z)/A>0,
  HP=(-a+z sigma)/A,
  HR=-k HQ,     Hzero=0.

Also, using z<=d and d theta=a sigma,

  HQ-HP=[a(1+sigma)-z(theta+sigma)]/A
         >=[a-d sigma]/A
         =a(1-d/D)/A>0.                                  (28)

Thus Qvec wins against Pvec,Rvec and0 at EVERY middle query. In particular,
the positive queried height is at least theta eta/A, not silently discarded.
Every literal L1 step subtracts its own h_i Qvec, proving the full induction
even if an internal step is0 or the entire middle mass is concentrated in
one entry. Its resulting query still satisfies z<=lambda.

The actual second bridge, with value1+bA, gives

  XR=XQ-(lambda A-1+1+bA)Qvec
    =-a e1-(lambda+b)v.                                  (29)

No child was reset or evaluated on another function.

## 10. The entire final block and its actual unqueried terminal

At each final-block query and at N, let w be the already executed L2 mass,
0<=w<=A. Define

  C(w)=lambda+b-k(w+1)/A,
  x(w)=XR-w Rvec,
  g(w)=Rvec,
  y(w)=-a e1-C(w)v.                                      (30)

The exact endpoint coefficients are

  C_start=lambda+b-k/A,
  C_N=lambda+k-k/A.                                      (31)

The physical +1 in the second bridge and the +1 in x-g have both been retained.
In particular C_N is not replaced by its limit lambda+k.

Since k>=43/60, eta/k<=3/21500<1/7000. From(11),

  (C_N-d)/k=1-eta/k-1/A>1-1/5000.                        (32)

Hence C(w)>d on the ENTIRE actual suffix. The four heights are

  HQ=theta(d-C)/A<0,
  HR=k theta(C-d)/A>0,
  HP=(-a+C sigma)/A,
  Hzero=0.

The remote R--P comparison requires a separate check. Define

  Psi(C)=C+ak(1-C/d).

It has positive slope because ak<1<d, and direct expansion gives

  HR-HP=(a/A)[1-Psi(C)/D].

Since C<=lambda+b,

  Psi(C)<=Psi(lambda+b)
          =D0+ak eta(lambda+b)/(lambda d)
          <D0+1/8000 <D.                                (33)

For the strict estimate, ak<1, lambda+b<5, lambda d>4 and eta=1/10000.
It follows that D-Psi(C)>7/8000 throughout the suffix. Thus Rvec wins
against EVERY vertex, not just the previous Qvec or the optimum.

Condition(23) now proves all suffix jets in(30). Each physical L2 step
subtracts h_i Rvec and changes w to w+h_i in[0,A], so all its internal
queries and the final step are included. For the explicit law(2), this
includes every step of its last q_m and its final physical unit step.

At the ACTUAL terminal w=A,

  x_N=-a e1-(lambda+b-k)v,
  g_N=-k v/A,
  y_N=-a e1-C_N v,
  V_K=2F_K(x_N)=2k theta(C_N-d)/A+k^2 theta/A^2.            (34)

This is positive and uses the closest radius1 from(25).

## 11. Exact planar payoff bound on the entire closed parameter domain

Put

  Phi(a,b)=a^2 b^2/(2lambda D0).

It is increasing in a and b separately on(17). For the a derivative,
the numerator has the sign of 2aD0+a^2 b^2/(2lambda)>0.
For the b derivative of b^2/D0 it has the sign b(2lambda+b)>0.

Let s=7/3, a0=18/25, b0=s-a0=121/75.
For b>=b0, separate monotonicity gives Phi(a,b)>=Phi(a0,b0).
For 43/30<=b<=b0, the sum condition gives a>=s-b. Along this boundary
b(s-b) decreases because b>=43/30>s/2.
The denominator D0(s-b,b)=lambda+b-s b^2/(2lambda)+b^3/(2lambda)
increases: its derivative has minimum 1-s^2/(6lambda)>0.
Hence Phi(s-b,b) decreases as b grows to b0, proving again

  Phi(a,b)>=Phi(a0,b0)>3/40.                              (35)

Here is an exact rational verification of the final comparison.
At(a0,b0), the numerator a0^2 b0^2=(726/625)^2>33/25, since
527076>515625. The denominator satisfies

  2lambda D0=2+(542/75)lambda-263538/140625
             <2+15718/900-263538/140625
             <88/5.

The last inequality reduces to
263538*450>839*140625, namely118592100>117984375.
Thus Phi(a0,b0)>(33/25)/(88/5)=3/40.

Discarding only the positive final term in(34) gives

  A V_K >= Phi(a,b)*(lambda/d)*(D0/D)*[(C_N-d)/k].

Each factor is uniform on(17):

  lambda/d>1-1/20000,
  D0/D>1-1/2000,
  (C_N-d)/k>1-1/5000.

The first uses lambda>2 and eta=1/10000; the second uses D0>2.
Their product exceeds1-(1/20000+1/2000+1/5000)>999/1000.
Combining(8),(35) yields

  B_N V_K >(27/2)*(3/40)*(999/1000)
           =80919/80000
           >1+1/40000.                                  (36)

This pays the whole finite-A terminal correction, not just a limiting
coefficient. No numerical parameter optimization or sampling is involved.

## 12. Every ordered interpolation row and full certificate separation

For both the interval and planar laws, every original sample INCLUDING N has
g_i in its one fixed hull, y_i=x_i-g_i satisfying the full normal(23), and

  f_i=<y_i,g_i>+||g_i||^2/2.

Every minimizer in its COMPLETE minimum set has gradient0, value0, and its
normal to the same hull at0. For ANY ordered pair of these actual samples,
including all remote, terminal and optimum pairs, expansion gives

  Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
      =<y_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                                 (37)

The reverse orientation follows from the normal at i. This checks ALL rows,
not just adjacent transitions. The original actual Gram matrix G_actual of
(x_0,g_0,...,g_N) is PSD because
c^T G_actual c=||c_x x_0+sum_i c_i g_i||^2>=0.
Zero-step repeated samples are kept as actual separate indices.

For any claimed full certificate (5) at a level
q_cert<=(1+1/40000)/B_N, evaluate(6) on the selected actual transcript.
The flow identities cancel the values, so

  trace(M G_actual)
    =q_cert-2f_N+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij
    <=q_cert-V<0,                                        (38)

by(15) or(36). But PSD of both M and G_actual would imply nonnegative trace.
This is a direct contradiction on the ORIGINAL coordinates. It covers dense
multipliers, arbitrary rank and nonzero or singular PSD remainders, all
initial mixed terms, common gradients, and both terminal edge orientations.
The selected system(7) cannot repair it either.

Thus the family fails physically before any certificate support restriction.
It is not legitimate to report an improving feasible array for this law.
No claim is made that zero remainder or two-endpoint sparsity is lossless.

Finally for arbitrary advertised L>0 take f=L F_I or f=L F_K and use h_i/L.
The actual points stay the same; every gradient and function value scales byL,
the whole minimum set stays the same, smoothness is exactlyL, and the OWN
closest initial radius remains1. The payoff is precisely V, in W=2R.
Each ordered original residual with 1/(2L) is L times(37).

## 13. Joint conclusion, hostile checks and genuinely remaining interface

The selector(12) covers every a,b>=0. In an interval branch, (15) gives a
uniform physical margin. In its complement, (16) puts the parameters in the
larger closed domain(17), and (36) gives a uniform planar margin. Every
constructed objective and trajectory obeys all original quantifiers for the
scoped class(4). This completes the proposed joint argument for(1).

The following boundary issues are explicitly included:
a=0 or b=0; equality at any selector threshold; unbounded and m-dependent
bridge coefficients; zero entries anywhere in L0,L1,L2; arbitrarily reordered
or concentrated block masses; complete non-singleton minimum sets; the final
physical unit in(2); the +1/A corrections; W=2R; and the correctly oriented
benchmark LOWER bound at N, rather than at a fictitious module horizon.
The planar law is used only where its radicand is strictly positive.

This is an analytical self-check, NOT an independent audit and NOT verifier
acceptance. The statement remains UNVERIFIED and cannot serve as an accepted
premise in another proof.

The new structural information is that, for these exact block sums and counts,
changing the internal ordering or supplying a denser certificate cannot help:
the same fixed function sees only nonnegative cumulative masses in three
chronological sectors. In particular rearrangements within the middle
level-(m+1) block do not create an effective additional resource for this law.

A genuinely different remaining interface is a schedule whose intended
improvement requires re-entry into earlier geometric sectors, or several
independently tunable bridges interlaced across the three cut boundaries so
that the exact block-sum hypotheses(4) no longer hold. A proof for such a
schedule must retain the corresponding cross-block displacement and initial
mixed terms in the whole matrix(5), AND exhibit a feasible base. The present
argument does not give a lossless reduction to(4), does not exclude every
unequal layout, and does not rule out a longer coupled-state construction
encoded in a predetermined step list.

That is an interface, not a new fourth law, not an existence theorem, and
not a conditional recurrence presented as a negative solution.
No second candidate law is opened after the first law's exact obstruction.
The one authorized analytical invocation ends with this scoped handoff;
there is no automatic renewal, target completion, finalization, publication,
revocation, or swarm shutdown.

# Retained terminal history: exact minimum and scalar redundancy

Status: UNVERIFIED complete joint proof draft, max2 round28, second and
final analytical round under TASK.md and guidance77175b3daa836eea.
The formal verifier gate is closed. No numerical evaluation, parameter
scan, solver, machine algebra, replay, consult, installation or subagent
is used. No conclusion below is an accepted fact.

Accepted inputs:1252b8823bb5a290, proof paragraphs1--2, for the ordered
interpolation convention and the attaining conjugate construction;
04219554e41d9750, Section10, for the ORIGINAL primitive identity.
All extra ingredients are proved jointly here. In particular no previous
annihilation or terminal-zero draft is used as a premise.

This draft closes the complete explicit family below. It does not prove
a full PSD certificate, a general no-seed result, or the original
exact-profile Goal. The normalization of that Goal remains W=2R.

Notation: the printed a,b,c,d,beta,u,v,e,p_i,z_i,k,r,q,l,nu,eta,U,B,
D_i,d_i,D,K,H,J,L_dead,E_mix,j_min,A_i,Conj,MinHull,R_true below
are local abbreviations for the corresponding symbols with suffix28.
They do not redefine the earlier project quantities with similar names.
The schedule mass s and capacity C=1+s are defined where used.

## 1. Complete family and the comparison result

Fix an arbitrary four-step word in the CLOSED domain

    h=(a,b,c,d),  1<=b<=2, 0<=a<=b, 0<=c,d<=2.               (1)

The three other steps may vanish. The condition b>=1 is part of the
specified annihilating curvature, not a deletion of zero-step words
from the original seed problem. There is no symmetry of the word.

Choose ANY beta in[1/b,1] and amplitudes u,v>=0, including zero, and set

    e=1-a/b, p=(1,e,0,0,0),
    z_i=product_(0<=j<i)(1-h_j*beta),
    x_i=(u*p_i,v*z_i), g_i=(u*p_i/b,beta*v*z_i), 0<=i<=4.    (2)

These are samples of ONE static legal base function

    F_base(x,y)=x^2/(2b)+beta*y^2/2.

It is convex and1-smooth, has unique minimizer0, and gives the exact GD
recursion with the supplied original indices. Coordinate reflections
allow arbitrary real initial amplitudes, so u,v>=0 loses no geometry.

The first mode dies by the second update. The second need not die.
For 1<a<b<2, 1/a<beta<1, and 1/beta<c,d<2, its five signs are

    +, -, +, -, +.

Thus the interior contains FOUR genuine reversals with g_4!=0. This
is outside both the one-reversal/annihilation family and round27's
terminal-zero family. All product-zero boundaries remain in(1)--(2).

Put s=a+b+c+d, C=1+s. Keep every function value and EVERY ordered
interpolation inequality on nodes0,...,4. The energy being minimized is

    V=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f_4)
             +sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sC||g_4||^2.    (3)

In particular the terminal gradient term has not been dropped.

Define the nonnegative killed-mode quantities

    D=[a(2-a)+b(2-b)e^2]/b^2,
    K=2a(b-1)e(1-e)/b^2,  L_dead=D+K.                     (4)

Sections3--4 give explicit piecewise POLYNOMIAL formulas for J(beta),
the fully value-optimized separate scalar energy per v^2, and H(beta)>=0.
The complete joint value minimum is

    if 1-a*beta>=0: V_min=u^2 L_dead+v^2 J(beta);
    if 1-a*beta<=0: V_min=u^2 D+v^2 J(beta)
                                 +(u^2 K-v^2 H(beta))_+.   (5)

Both formulas agree at equality. They optimize all values simultaneously,
not only separable values. All twenty finite constraints are retained.
The minimum is attained by one static global smooth convex function.
Its genuine minimizers and true distance are given in Section7.

There is no residual amplitude optimization in the following formula.
If u^2+v^2=1, the first line of(5) has minimum min(L_dead,J).
For the second line, if K+H>0, the minimum is

    min{L_dead,J,(H D+K J)/(K+H)}.                         (6)

If K+H=0, it is min(D,J). The third candidate comes from
u^2=H/(K+H), and includes its endpoints when H or K vanishes.
Section6 removes the remaining curvature parameter by explicit finite
endpoint/polynomial-root rules, without a numerical calculation.

Write E_mix(h) for this exact minimum over beta, all normalized amplitudes,
and all values. Write j_min(h)=min_(beta in[1/b,1]) J(beta). Then

    min(0,E_mix(h))=min(0,j_min(h)).                       (7)

In particular ALL members of this joint family have V>=0 if and only if
ALL its separately value-optimized fast scalar tests have J(beta)>=0.
The killed scalar mode already has L_dead>=0. Any negative normalized
joint minimum equals an already available separate scalar minimum.

Consequently the full collection of optimized separate scalar tests,
even before adding ANY other current full-cone necessary conditions,
implies nonnegativity for this entire joint family. This is a complete
parameter comparison on(1), not a positive-price sample or a new
shortest-path formula without a comparison. No assertion is made that
the older necessary system is itself sufficient for a full certificate.

## 2. All values, original flow, and all directed constraints

For distinct i,j in{0,1,2,3,4}, use the accepted residual

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Let

    U=(b-1)u^2/(2b^2), B=beta(1-beta)v^2/2.

At the base values, direct substitution gives the symmetric slack

    w_ij=-Q_ij(F_base)=U(p_i-p_j)^2+B(z_i-z_j)^2.           (8)

Both weights can vanish. If f_i=F_base(x_i)+delta_i, ALL directed
inequalities are exactly delta_i-delta_j<=w_ij. Let D_i be the shortest
w cost from ORIGINAL terminal node4 to node i, with D_4=0, in the
COMPLETE five-node graph. Nonnegative cycles can be removed, even when
their cost is zero, so a shortest simple path exists.

Following a path from4 to i gives delta_i-delta_4>=-D_i. Since every
h_i>=0, the simultaneous value minimum is

    V_min=V_base-2sum_(i<4)h_i D_i.                        (9)

It is attained by delta_i=-D_i: the triangle inequality
D_j<=D_i+w_ij gives delta_i-delta_j<=w_ij for every ordered pair.
This also handles h_i=0 and coincident points; no node is discarded.

For the ORIGINAL full certificate, retain all twenty Lambda_ij>=0,
zero diagonal, and set

    ones=(1,1,1,1,1)^T, L_flow=diag(Lambda*ones)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2a,-2b,-2c,-2d,2s)^T,
    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
    D_prim=diag(a(2-a),b(2-b),c(2-c),d(2-d),-s(s+2)).

Accepted04219554e41d9750 gives

    L_flow^T ones=omega,
    M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow*T+T^T*L_flow^T)/2,
    V+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij
                           =<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_4)>.    (10)

For the auxiliary price problem, Lambda_ij is mass on i->j. Node4
is the source of2s; node i<4 is a sink of2h_i. From
w_ij>=D_j-D_i every such flow has

    sum Lambda_ij w_ij>=2sum h_i D_i.

Sending2h_i along an attaining4->i path gives equality. This verifies
the terminal-source orientation as well as(9). These price flows
are NOT full PSD certificates. The original sufficient condition still
requires M_prim PSD on all of R^5, with no imposed gradient equality,
rank restriction, support restriction, or removed multiplier sign.

## 3. Exact separate scalar distances and energy

For scalar points on a line, a shortest squared-distance path uses
their consecutive spatial gaps between the source and target. Indeed
an edge spanning gaps t_1,...,t_m>=0 costs(sum t_j)^2>=sum t_j^2.
Every source-target path crosses each intervening gap at least once.
Summing gives the lower bound, and the consecutive-gap path attains it.
Repeated points and zero cost weights cause no exception.

For the killed coordinate, the distances from node4 are

    U[e^2+(1-e)^2], U e^2, 0, 0, 0.                       (11)

Their optimized values have f_1=||g_1||^2/2 and f_2=f_3=f_4=0;
the extra amount above ||g_0||^2/2 at0 is2Ue(1-e).
Substitution in(3) gives exactly u^2(D+K). This proves(4), including
nonnegativity, rather than importing a previous draft.

For the fast coordinate put

    k=1-a*beta, r=|k|, l=b*beta-1, q=l*r,
    nu=1-c*beta, eta=1-d*beta.                             (12)

Here 0<=l<=1, 0<=r<=1, 0<=q<=r, and -1<=nu,eta<=1.
Its actual coordinates are

    z=(1,k,-l*k,-l*k*nu,-l*k*nu*eta).                      (13)

Let d_i be the UNWEIGHTED squared-gap scalar distance from z_4 to z_i
using ALL five scalar samples. The following table is exact:

    d_4=0,
    d_3=q^2*nu^2*(1-eta)^2,

    nu>=0:
       d_2=q^2[nu^2(1-eta)^2+(1-nu)^2],
       d_1=(r+q*nu*eta)^2;

    nu<=0:
       d_2=q^2(1-nu*eta)^2,
       d_1=q^2*nu^2*(1-eta)^2+(r+q*nu)^2;

    k>=0: d_0=d_1+(1-k)^2,
    k<=0: d_0=d_2+(1-q)^2.                                (14)

All formulas agree on their shared boundaries. To prove the table,
first take k<0. Node1 is-r, node0 is1, and the tail coordinates are
q,q*nu,q*nu*eta. If nu>=0 their order is4<=3<=2; if nu<=0 it is
3<=4<=2. They all lie in[-r,1], so node1 is a minimum and node0
a maximum. The gap rule gives(14). If k>0, reflect nodes1,2,3,4
through zero. The same d_1,d_2,d_3 result; node1=k now lies between
every tail point and node0=1, which gives the other d_0 formula.
At k=0 all tail points and node1 coincide, proving the boundary too.

The FULL separate scalar energy minimum per squared initial amplitude is

    J(beta)=beta*sum_(i<4)h_i[1+(1-h_i)beta]z_i^2
       -s*beta[1+C*beta]z_4^2
       -beta(1-beta)*sum_(i<4)h_i d_i.                     (15)

The first two terms are the exact base quadratic energy, including the
nonzero terminal term; the last subtracts the complete original-flow
price. Formula(9) and the scalar conjugate construction in Section7
show that it is attained by an actual ONE-dimensional smooth convex
function with a minimizer. It is therefore a necessary scalar test
for any full primitive certificate, not merely a relaxed test.

As a check on conventions, beta=1 has zero slack and(15) reduces by
telescoping to1-C^2*z_4^2. No divisions by z_i, r, q, nu or eta occur.

## 4. Exact joint distances: only two competing paths

Suppose first k>=0. Every fast tail point is<=k<=1. A shortest fast
path from4 to1 uses only tail points before1, and a shortest path to0
continues1->0. Those paths simultaneously attain the killed-coordinate
distances(11). Shortest paths to2 and3 use only tail points, and also
attain(11). Consequently every joint distance is the sum of its separate
scalar distances. Equations(9), (11), and(15) give the first line of(5).

Now suppose k<=0, and put r=-k. Node1 is the minimum-r, node0 the
maximum1, and every tail point lies in[-r,1]. For targets1,2,3,4,
the same argument gives

    D_1=U e^2+B d_1, D_2=B d_2, D_3=B d_3, D_4=0.        (16)

For target0, take a shortest simple path. It either avoids node1,
ends1->0, or uses node1 between two tail nodes i,j. In the last case,
removing that visit decreases the cost by

    2U e^2+2B(z_i+r)(z_j+r)>=0.                           (17)

Thus a shortest path of one of the first two types always exists.
No edge was removed from the original constraints: (17) proves a
permitted simplification of an optimal path, including equality cases.

If node1 is avoided, the best fast route goes through tail maximum
node2 before0. Its cost is U+B d_0. If the last edge is1->0,
the best preceding tail route to1 costs B d_1, and the total is
U[e^2+(1-e)^2]+B[d_1+(1+r)^2]. Hence

    D_0=min{U+B d_0,
                U[e^2+(1-e)^2]+B[d_1+(1+r)^2]}.            (18)

Both candidates are attained by actual original-node paths. With

    Delta=d_1+(1+r)^2-d_0>=0,
    H(beta)=a*beta(1-beta)*Delta,                          (19)

the extra total ORIGINAL flow price above the sum of separate prices is

    2a[D_0-U(e^2+(1-e)^2)-B d_0]
                           =min(u^2 K,v^2 H(beta)).        (20)

Nonnegativity of Delta follows because the path to1 then1->0 is an
available scalar route to0; it cannot be cheaper than d_0. Formula(17)
and the scalar gap proof establish(16)--(18) for ALL amplitudes and
weights, including zero. The first and last expressions in(20) agree
by4aUe(1-e)=u^2K and2aB Delta=v^2H.

Subtract(20) from the sum of the separate energies to obtain

    u^2(D+K)+v^2J-min(u^2K,v^2H)
                         =u^2D+v^2J+(u^2K-v^2H)_+.

This proves the second line of(5). At k=0, r=q=0, d_1=0, d_0=1,
and H=0, so it agrees with the first line. Joint prices are genuinely
positive when K>0, H>0, and both amplitudes are nonzero. Nevertheless
their ENTIRE gain is paid by the explicit killed-mode term u^2 K.

These strict inequalities occur throughout the four-reversal interior
in Section1 when both amplitudes are positive. There K>0, while the
terminal fast coordinate w=z_4 satisfies0<w<=q<1. The scalar gap rule
gives d_0<=(1-w)^2<1, whereas(1+r)^2>1. Thus Delta>0 and H>0
since beta<1. The redundancy is not caused by a vanishing joint price.

## 5. Full amplitude minimum and complete scalar comparison

Homogeneity permits u^2+v^2=1 for deciding signs. This convention is
only an amplitude parametrization; it is NOT a claim that the new
interpolant's true minimizer distance is1. Section7 computes that distance.

For k<=0 set t=u^2. The expression in(5) becomes

    tD+(1-t)J+[tK-(1-t)H]_+, 0<=t<=1.                    (21)

It is affine on either side of t=H/(K+H). A linear function on an
interval takes its minimum at an endpoint unless constant, in which
case the endpoints also attain it. Thus(6) lists EVERY required
candidate. When K+H=0 the positive part vanishes. This derivation
retains the pure scalar endpoints, ties, and flat segments.

Because D,K,H>=0, (5) implies for every beta and normalized amplitude

    V_min>=min(0,J(beta)).                                (22)

For the compatible case use L_dead>=0. For the incompatible case use
u^2D>=0 and the nonnegative positive part; v^2J>=min(0,J).
Taking all minima gives E_mix>=min(0,j_min). Conversely the admissible
choice u=0,v=1 gives E_mix<=j_min. These two bounds prove(7).

This also proves the desired comparison with the COMPLETE older system.
Let N(h) denote its other necessary conditions, whatever additional
full-cone restrictions they impose. On the explicit word domain(1),

    [N(h) and all optimized separate scalar tests pass]
        implies [N(h) and every joint test(2)--(3) passes].

The reverse inclusion after adding the joint family is tautological.
No condition in N was dropped, weakened, or asserted to follow from a
different unverified condition. The implication holds before N is used.
If J(beta)<0, its attaining scalar function already excludes the word;
the joint family cannot make the most negative normalized energy smaller.
If every J>=0, the joint family excludes nothing. No capacity s was
fixed, so this remains true on the threshold subset C>=193/25.

## 6. Exact elimination of the remaining curvature parameter

The word h is the supplied schedule, not an unknown value parameter.
For each such word the following is a finite exact formula for its
complete amplitude/curvature/value minimum. There is no unsolved LP
or search over paths in this formula.

If K=0, equations(5)--(6) give

    E_mix=min(D,j_min).                                   (23)

If K>0, define I_minus={beta in[1/b,1]: a*beta>=1} and

    R_mix(beta)=[H(beta)D+K J(beta)]/[K+H(beta)]
                                           on I_minus.

Then

    E_mix=min{L_dead,j_min, min_(beta in I_minus)R_mix(beta)}. (24)

An empty last set contributes no candidate. K+H is strictly positive,
so this rational expression has no pole. The equality endpoint a*beta=1
is harmless: H=0 and R_mix=J there.

Here is an explicit finite rule for the minima in(23)--(24). Partition
[1/b,1] at those of1/a and1/c that belong to it, omitting a reciprocal
when its denominator is zero. Include both original endpoints, with
singleton intervals permitted. On every resulting interval, choose the
applicable sign of k and nu in(12)--(15). Each z_i and d_i is then a
polynomial in beta, J is a polynomial of degree at most10, and on
I_minus the same is true of H. Their formulas agree at every breakpoint.

For j_min retain all interval endpoints and every interior real zero
of J'(beta). If J' is identically zero on a piece, its endpoints suffice.
Evaluate J at these algebraically defined candidates and take the least.

For the last minimum in(24), use the pieces in I_minus. Retain their
endpoints and the interior zeros of the explicit polynomial

    [K+H(beta)]J'(beta)+H'(beta)[D-J(beta)].                (25)

The derivative of R_mix is K times(25)/(K+H)^2. If(25) is identically
zero, that rational function is constant on the piece and its endpoints
suffice. Otherwise it has finitely many roots. The elementary derivative
condition for an interior extremum proves that this finite list is exact.
There is no division by a zero amplitude, zero step, zero product,
parallel affine slope, or zero cost. No polynomial roots are evaluated
by a computer in this draft.

Thus the formula covers ALL curvature and amplitude parameters, and
(7) gives a simpler complete decision of whether the resulting minimum
contains any information beyond the separate scalar tests.

## 7. One attaining function, actual minimizers, and true radius

For the joint optimum use f_i=F_base(x_i)-D_i. Define on R^2

    c_i=<g_i,x_i>-f_i,
    Conj_i(w)=c_i+<x_i,w-g_i>+||w-g_i||^2/2,
    Conj(w)=max_(0<=i<=4) Conj_i(w),
    F(x)=sup_w[<x,w>-Conj(w)].                            (26)

This uses ALL five finite nodes including terminal4. It does not assert
that the original base minimizer0 remains optimal after value changes.

For completeness, the construction's properties can be checked directly
as in accepted1252b8823bb5a290. Conj is finite and1-strongly convex, and
has quadratic growth. The supremum defining F has a unique maximizer
w(x). Strong convexity at two such maximizers gives

    <x-x',w(x)-w(x')> >= ||w(x)-w(x')||^2.

Hence w(x) is1-Lipschitz. The two maximizer inequalities prove that F
is differentiable with gradient w(x), convex and finite everywhere.
At g_i, expansion gives Conj_j(g_i)-c_i=Q_ij<=0, with equality for j=i.
Thus Conj(g_i)=c_i and x_i is a subgradient of Conj at g_i, proving
F(x_i)=f_i and grad F(x_i)=g_i. Repeated positions force the same jet
through their two directed inequalities, so this argument loses no
coincident-point boundary. The resulting F realizes the actual GD word.

To exhibit its minima, define

    A_i=Conj_i(0)=D_i-U p_i^2-B z_i^2,
    A_max=max_i A_i,
    Active={i:A_i=A_max},
    y_i=x_i-g_i=((1-1/b)u*p_i,(1-beta)v*z_i).               (27)

The directional derivative of the maximum of these finitely many
quadratics at0 is max_(i in Active)<y_i,w>. Its subgradient set is
therefore exactly the convex hull of the active y_i: inclusion of that
hull follows by supporting inequalities, and a point outside it is
separated by a direction for which its proposed subgradient inequality
fails. Consequently

    MinHull=argmin F=conv{y_i:i in Active}, min F=-A_max.   (28)

Indeed grad F(x)=0 exactly when x belongs to the subgradient set of
Conj at0, by the unique-maximizer condition. This proves existence of
actual minimizers without a fixed-optimum assumption.

For nonzero (u,v), x_0 lies strictly outside MinHull. For every i,
since p_i<=1,z_i<=1 and both curvatures are positive,

    <x_0,x_0-y_i>
      =u^2[1-(1-1/b)p_i]+v^2[1-(1-beta)z_i]
      >=u^2/b+beta*v^2>0.                                (29)

Thus R_true=dist(x_0,MinHull)>0. The following is an exact finite
distance formula, with no relaxation. Take the minimum of
||x_0-y_i||^2 over active i and the distances to every nondegenerate
segment between two active y_i,y_j. For such a segment put

    tau_ij=min(1,max(0,<x_0-y_i,y_j-y_i>/||y_j-y_i||^2)),
    dist_ij^2=||x_0-y_i-tau_ij(y_j-y_i)||^2.               (30)

The minimum of this list is R_true^2. A nearest point of a planar
convex hull to an exterior point lies on its boundary, which is a vertex
or an edge; all such edges occur in the listed segments. Every listed
segment also lies inside the hull, proving equality. Coincident vertices
use the vertex candidate and require no division.

In particular the actual normalized terminal objective is exactly

    2[F_base(x_4)+A_max]/R_true^2,                         (31)

because D_4=0. This is in the project's W=2R convention. Translating
a closest minimizer to0, subtracting min F, and scaling by R_true gives
initial distance1 and preserves the schedule and normalized objective.
The sign comparisons for V are unchanged by translations and scale
quadratically, so they did not need to replace true distance by ||x_0||.
The scalar tests use precisely the same construction in one dimension.

## 8. A concrete trap: forcing the base origin to remain optimal

In the genuine four-reversal interior of Section1, take v>0. Then
B>0 and there are positive numbers t,w with z_3=-t,z_4=w. Both killed
coordinates are zero at3,4. Formula(14) gives d_3=(t+w)^2, and(16)
therefore forces at the joint optimum

    delta_4-delta_3=B(t+w)^2.                             (32)

If one also imposed the base point (0,0) as an optimum, its base slack
to nodes3,4 would be B*t^2 and B*w^2. For ANY choice of its value shift
delta_star and any common shift of the finite values, the two ordered
constraints through that optimum would give

    delta_4-delta_3<=B(w^2+t^2),                          (33)

contradicting(32) by2Btw>0. Since d=h_3>0, attaining the full objective
minimum requires equality in the individual lower bound for node3 in(9).
Thus merely choosing other equally good finite values does not repair
this forced-origin problem. The exact finite minimum is legal, but its
minimizer moves. Equations(26)--(30) provide the actual function and
distance. This symbolic counterexample is why the terminal node and the
base optimum must not be silently identified in a retained-terminal case.

## 9. Boundary audit and exact remaining interface

All calculations retain a=0, c=0, d=0, b=1, a=b, beta=1/b, beta=1,
a*beta=1, c*beta=1, d*beta=1, either vanished amplitude, coincident
tail points, and all combinations of those cases. Costs remain
nonnegative and paths are justified by weak inequalities. Formulas
(14), (18), and(5) agree at sign boundaries; no gradient product was
cancelled. If both amplitudes vanish the energy is0. Every original
formal gradient and directed pair remains in the full certificate(10).

The structural reason for redundancy is now precise. The killed mode
has only two nonzero positions. Contraction places all fast tail points
between its two early scalar extrema. Therefore there is only one
incompatible sink, node0, and its entire extra price is bounded by K,
an already present positive scalar energy term. This argument survives
a nonzero terminal gradient and four fast reversals.

The following are NOT closed:

* A later-killed slow mode, with more than two nonzero sampled positions.
* Two retained signed modes, or arbitrary nonseparable base geometry.
* A killing step b>2, where the nonnegative D budget can fail, or a
  tail step beyond2, where the tail may leave the interval between the
  early extrema and the shortcut/path classification can fail.
* Words with a>b, outside the specified monotone killed-coordinate
  chronology, unless a separate argument is supplied.
* Original full h/Lambda/exact-flow/ambient-R^5 PSD feasibility at
  C>=193/25, and the unrestricted all-schedule asymptotic profile Goal.

These are limits of an explicit family, not proved obstructions to the
general problem. No full certificate is inferred from an auxiliary
price flow or from nonnegativity on these prescribed modes.

## 10. Retrieval and status

The assignment, latest shared guidance, relevant findings/dead ends,
accepted fact graph, and own previous handoff were read first. Matlas
retrieval on retained spectral/value pricing returned unrelated theorems.
Primary web retrieval recovered Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur,
arXiv1502.05666; the exact local text's Theorem4 proof, Remark2,
Corollary1, and Remark3 were read. Its constructive conjugacy viewpoint
is relevant background. No external theorem is used as a black box:
the necessary accepted convention is1252b8823bb5a290 and every new
path, budget, minimizer, parameter, and comparison step is written here.

The counterexample skill was used to attack the forced-origin premise
and the coincident/zero-product boundaries. The former is refuted in
Section8; no counterexample to the stated family formula was found.
The formula is supported by the proof, not by that unsuccessful attack.

This is the final assigned analytical round after completed baseline26.
It supplies a proof handoff at intended completion28/attempt29 including
the historical interruption. No lifecycle exit, resource renewal,
fact acceptance, project finalization, or target completion is claimed.

# Bulk binary interpolation kernels: full-space identities and exact obstructions

Status: self-contained UNVERIFIED candidate, round12. No optimization and no
fact_submit. This is an obstruction to the precisely defined multiplier
organizations below, not a legal hard function or a decision of the project.
Only accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies a mathematical predecessor: its
signed smooth-convex interpolation residual and full-certificate framework.
Every new identity and bound used in this document is proved here jointly.

## 1. Exact operator form on the original formal space

Fix n>=1, N=n+1, h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), and normalize L=1 and the
optimum sample to x_star=g_star=f_star=0. The N+1 vectors
x_0,g_0,...,g_n remain independent. Put

    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i) h_t g_t,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

For actual smooth-convex samples Q_ij<=0, by fact1252b8823bb5a290.
Let lambda_ij>=0 denote the coefficient of Q_ij. A full upper certificate
at level q has all finite value coefficients zero and is the PSD form

    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij.       (1)

Define the strictly lower triangular N by N matrix T by
T_it=h_t for t<i and T_it=0 otherwise, and put V=I+T. For a real
matrix B, Sym(B)=(B+B^T)/2. Let e_n be the last coordinate vector
and 1 the vector with every coordinate equal to1.

Here is an exact operator parameterization useful for specifying structured
arrays. Choose an N by N real matrix A satisfying

    A_ij<=0 for i!=j,
    b=A1>=0,
    a=A^T1-2e_n>=0,                                  (2)

where inequalities on vectors are coordinatewise. Set

    lambda_ij=-A_ij  (finite distinct i,j),
    lambda_(i,star)=b_i,  lambda_(star,i)=a_i.          (3)

All actual coefficients are nonnegative. With r_i=sum_(j!=i)lambda_ij,
c_i=sum_(j!=i)lambda_ji, one has A_ii=r_i+b_i and
a_i=r_i+b_i-c_i-2*1_(i=n). Thus the value coefficient at i in (1) is
r_i+b_i-c_i-a_i-2*1_(i=n)=0, including at the terminal.

Expanding every quadratic term in (1) gives the complete matrix

    M(q) = [x_0;g]^T [[q, -b^T/2],[-b/2, G]] [x_0;g],
    G=Sym(A V)-e_n e_n^T.                            (4)

The notation means sums of Euclidean inner products when the formal vectors
have dimension greater than1. To check (4), write K_ij=lambda_ij for i!=j
and K_ii=0. The finite position terms equal
g^T[diag(r)-K]Tg. The star position terms equal
-<x_0,sum b_i g_i>+g^T diag(b)Tg. The squared-gradient terms equal
g^T[diag(r+b)-Sym(K)-e_n e_n^T]g, using the just-checked value flow.
Adding them proves (4). Conversely a finite nonnegative array with the
required value flow gives (2)--(4). This identity alone is a reformulation,
not progress on unrestricted PSD; the restricted cone studied next is
specified independently of that unknown PSD inequality.

## 2. A genuine bulk cone: directed binary-coordinate operators

Take N=2^m, m>=1, n=N-1, and index the finite samples in chronological
binary order. At each bit k choose numbers

    u_k>=0, v_k>=0, 0<=u_k-v_k<=2, kappa_k>=0,
    A_k=[[u_k,-v_k],[-u_k,v_k+2]].                   (5)

The two column sums of A_k are0 and2; its two row sums are
u_k-v_k and2+v_k-u_k, both nonnegative. Embed A_k at bit k with
identity matrices on the other bits. Choose arbitrary delta_i>=0 and put

    A=sum_(k=1)^m kappa_k (I tensor ... tensor A_k tensor ... tensor I)
                   +diag(delta_0,...,delta_n).       (6)

An off-diagonal entry in (6) is nonzero only between indices differing in
one bit. For each setting of the other m-1 bits, the edge0->1 at bit k
has coefficient kappa_k v_k, and the reverse edge has coefficient
kappa_k u_k. This is a scalable bulk organization with m*2^(m-1)
interior cross-block pairs. It is not the endpoint-fan join. The same
two-node factor is used in every context at a given bit; that equality
of contexts is a real restriction.

Let s=sum kappa_k. Then b=A1>=0. At a finite index i with binary bits i_k,

    a_i=2sum_k kappa_k i_k+delta_i-2*1_(i=n).

Consequently (2), hence all actual signs and all value coefficients, hold
if and only if

    t:=2s+delta_n >=2.                              (7)

There is no prohibition on outgoing optimum edges: all these a_i are
retained. The delta_i permit arbitrary additional equal weights on
Q_(i,star) and Q_(star,i). The initial coefficient q is free. Incoming
terminal edges are allowed; no terminal or queried pair is deleted.

## 3. Full one-step calibration

The cone contains a valid one-step seed throughout 0<H<=sqrt(2). Put
M=1+H, take physical step h_0=H, and choose

    A_H=[[2,-2/M],[-2,2+2/M]], delta=0, kappa=1.

Its actual coefficients are

    lambda_01=2/M, lambda_10=2,
    lambda_(0,star)=2H/M, lambda_(1,star)=2/M,
    lambda_(star,0)=lambda_(star,1)=0.

Direct expansion proves

    (1/M)||x_0||^2-2f_1+sum lambda_ij Q_ij
      = [||x_0-Hg_0-g_1||^2
             +(2-H^2)||g_0-g_1||^2+M(M-1)||g_1||^2]/M.       (8)

The value coefficient at0 is2/M-2+2H/M=0, and that at1,
including -2f_1, is2-2/M+2/M-2=0. Expanding the three squares verifies
all gradient and initial-distance coefficients. Every square coefficient
in (8) is nonnegative. In particular H=sqrt(2) gives the full undeformed
Silver seed, and the actual critical h_0=1+a_m also lies in this interval
for m>=128, as checked below. The next obstruction therefore concerns
the proposed tensor extension, rather than an invalid local seed.

## 4. Exact constant lower barrier for the entire cone (5)--(7)

Claim. If h_0=H>=7/5, no array (5)--(7), at ANY depth m, can make the
full matrix (4) PSD with q<=1/8. All remaining steps may be arbitrary
nonnegative numbers. All u_k,v_k,kappa_k and delta_i may depend on the
horizon. The local factors need not be calibrated as in Section3.

Proof. Define the nonnegative numbers

    U=sum kappa_k u_k,
    R=sum kappa_k v_k,
    B=U-R>=0,
    z=B+delta_0>=0,
    t=2sum kappa_k+delta_n>=2.                       (9)

The symbol R in this section is only a scalar coefficient sum, not an
objective or a gradient remainder. At the all-zero binary index0,

    b_0=z,
    G_00=U+delta_0-HR=z-(H-1)R.                    (10)

For (10), every off-diagonal neighbor of index0 is later than0,
T_j0=H at every such neighbor, and the total outgoing coefficient is R.
At the all-one index n,

    b_n=t-B,
    G_nn=t+R-1.                                    (11)

For (11), column n of V has only its diagonal entry1, and
A_nn=sum kappa_k(v_k+2)+delta_n=t+R.

The source condition gives sum b_i-sum a_i=2. If q=0 and the full
matrix were PSD, every entry in its initial row would be zero, hence
b=0, contradicting this condition and a>=0. A negative q also violates
PSD. Thus suppose 0<q<=1/8.

The principal matrix in x_0,g_0 must be PSD. By (10), its determinant
and lower diagonal require

    z^2 <=4q[z-(H-1)R],
    z-(H-1)R>=0.                                   (12)

In particular z<=4q and B<=z<=4q. Rearranging (12) as

    (z-2q)^2+4q(H-1)R<=4q^2

also gives R<=q/(H-1). Since H>=7/5 and q<=1/8,

    B<=1/2,  R<=5/16.                              (13)

The other principal matrix, in x_0,g_n, has positive lower diagonal
t+R-1>=1. By (11), its determinant requires

    q >= (t-B)^2/[4(t+R-1)].                        (14)

Using (13), t>=2, and t+R-1<t gives

    q > (t-1/2)^2/(4t)
       >= (3t/4)^2/(4t)
       =9t/64 >=9/32.

The middle inequality uses t-1/2>=3t/4, which follows from t>=2.
This contradicts q<=1/8, completing the proof. No Schur complement
of an omitted subspace, no Huber equality, and no positivity restricted
to legal transcripts were used: these are necessary principal tests
on the full original formal space.

## 5. Application to undeformed and actual critical words

For undeformed Silver, h_0=sqrt(2)>7/5. Thus the local full PSD seed
(8) cannot be extended with (5)--(7) to any upper coefficient tending
to0. In particular it cannot maintain the benchmark q=1/lambda^m
once lambda^m>=8, where lambda=1+sqrt(2).

For the assigned critical word, m>=128, epsilon=1/m,
c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c, and
H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j). Its first physical step is
H=1+a. The radical bounds12/5<lambda<17/7 imply

    a >=127/(129lambda)>889/2193>2/5,
    a <1/lambda=sqrt(2)-1.

The last rational comparison follows from4445>4386. Hence
7/5<H<sqrt(2), so both the valid seed (8) and the obstruction apply.
The total shifted mass M_m=1+sum H_m is at least2^m, since every one
of its2^m-1 steps exceeds1. Thus the desired q_m=O(1/M_m) tends to0
and is excluded by q>1/8 for this cone. This conclusion does not use
the unverified C54 scalar comparison as a mathematical premise.

Scope. Independent context-dependent edge weights, connections changing
more than one bit, arbitrary dense tensor kernels, and independent changes
to the local finite coefficient arrays are NOT excluded. In particular
the full PEP certificate cone is much larger. The scalar obstruction does
not prove that the actual critical schedule is bad. The structural defect
of (6) is precise: the same coefficient totals R,U control both the first
gradient and the terminal, while the terminal value source requires t>=2.
Increasing q or diagonal optimum storage cannot remove that defect at
the required vanishing scale.

## 6. A separate exact screen for symmetric bulk multiplier arrays

This section concerns ANY array with lambda_ij=lambda_ji for all finite
distinct i,j, including arbitrary complete bipartite, dyadic hierarchical,
or Walsh-diagonal nonnegative kernels. Optimum incidence may be arbitrary
as long as the value flow holds. It is a different restriction from (6).

Identify R^2 with C, with <u,v>=Re(conj(u)v). Set zeta=(1+i)/2 and
define a formal trajectory

    p_0=1, p_(i+1)=(1-h_i zeta)p_i,
    x_i=p_i, g_i=zeta p_i, f_i=|p_i|^2/4.           (15)

It obeys the physical GD equations exactly and has ||x_0||=1. Since
Re(zeta)=|zeta|^2=1/2, direct expansion gives

    Q_(i,star)=Q_(star,i)=0,
    Q_ij=(1/2)Im(conj(p_i)p_j)=-Q_ji.              (16)

The individual finite Q need not be nonpositive. These are formal test
vectors, not asserted to be a legal smooth-convex transcript. On (15),
the quadratic form of a symmetric finite array equals

    M(q)=q-|p_n|^2/2.

Full PSD therefore requires q>=|p_n|^2/2, irrespective of every optimum
edge. For the recursive word with h-levels1+a c^k, define

    T_j=|p_(2^j-1)|^2,
    d_k=(1+a^2 c^(2k))/2.

Then T_0=1 and T_(j+1)=d_j T_j^2, since the word contains two
identical child products and the single bridge. Both undeformed Silver
(a=1/lambda,c=lambda) and the actual critical word for m>=128 satisfy
a>2/5 and c>12/5. Consequently

    d_0>4/7, d_1>19/20, d_2>3, d_3>15,
    T_4=d_0^8 d_1^4 d_2^2 d_3
         >(4/7)^8(19/20)^4*135 >6/5.                (17)

The four rational inequalities in (17) follow by evaluating
(1+(2/5)^2(12/5)^(2k))/2 at k=0,1,2,3. For the final comparison,
the left rational product reduces to900778752/720600125; its numerator
times5 minus6 times its denominator is180293010>0. For k>=4, d_k>1.
It follows inductively that for every j>=4,

    T_j>(6/5)^(2^(j-4)),
    q>(1/2)(6/5)^(2^(j-4)).                        (18)

Thus the symmetric multiplier organization fails even the undeformed
calibration, exponentially in the number of leaves. This is not a
limitation on Laplacian SLACK matrices produced from directed arrays.

For a general directed finite array, the same computation yields instead
the exact necessary directed-area inequality

    q +(1/2)sum_(i<j)(lambda_ij-lambda_ji)
                      Im(conj(p_i)p_j) >= T_m/2.    (19)

Equation (19) identifies an actual contribution that is lost on replacing
an array by its symmetric part. It is necessary only, not sufficient for
full PSD or interpolation feasibility.

## 7. Exact tensor contraction for the remaining directed dense family

For the same recursive word and any complex zeta, let P_j be its scalar
terminal GD polynomial on H_j and define

    tau_(j+1)=(1-(1+a c^j)zeta)P_j, P_0=1.

The full sample-position vector p^(j) of length2^j obeys

    p^(0)=(1), p^(j+1)=[p^(j);tau_(j+1)p^(j)],
    p^(m)=(1,tau_m) tensor ... tensor(1,tau_1).     (20)

This is proved by applying the identical child GD polynomial after the
bridge; P_(j+1)=tau_(j+1)P_j is its last entry.

If a proposed dense finite multiplier kernel (before discarding its
diagonal) is K=eta times the tensor product of real nonnegative2 by2
matrices B_m,...,B_1, then

    p^* K p=eta product_k [B_k,00+B_k,11|tau_k|^2
                             +B_k,01 tau_k+B_k,10 conj(tau_k)].       (21)

This follows by distributing the finite tensor sums. Diagonal entries
contribute real numbers and thus do not affect the imaginary part.
At zeta=(1+i)/2, (19) is equivalently

    q+(1/2)Im(p^*Kp)>=T_m/2.                       (22)

For a finite nonnegative sum of such product kernels, sum the expressions
(21). This is an analytic contraction test for a PRESCRIBED low-complexity
kernel, not a sufficient PSD invariant. It leaves the genuinely directed
dense product family open; applying (4) with arbitrary unspecified A and
calling its PSD a remaining lemma would merely restate the original PEP
and is explicitly BLOCKED as a proof route.

## 8. Literature and scope of this round

Read as method context: Bok and Altschuler, "Accelerating Proximal Gradient
Descent via Silver Stepsizes", arXiv:2412.05497, Theorem9, Definition13,
Lemma14 and its proof, with the local PDF and extracted text retained.
That work's Laplacian is a slack matrix in auxiliary proximal subgradients,
not a symmetric multiplier array. Its exact silver identities do not
supply an upper certificate for the critical deformation here. No theorem
from the paper is used as a correctness premise in Sections1--7.

This package records two distinct scoped obstructions and a directed
tensor contraction screen. It adds no verified fact and does not resolve
either the uniform lower-bound target or a strict asymptotic separation.

## 9. Dense tensor kernels and arbitrary nonnegative mixtures: the same barrier

The coordinate sum in (6) is not essential. Here is a strictly larger,
genuinely dense coefficient cone, with independent cross-block pairs
differing in arbitrarily many bits. Its assumptions are explicit; no
arbitrary unknown full matrix is being hidden inside its definition.

At every bit k choose a two by two matrix

    P_k=[[1-v_k,u_k],[v_k,c_k-u_k]],
    0<=u_k<=v_k<=1,  u_k<=c_k<=1-v_k+u_k.           (23)

All entries are nonnegative, all row and column sums are at most1, and
the first column sum is exactly1. These properties follow directly:
the row sums are1-v_k+u_k and c_k+v_k-u_k; the column sums are1,c_k.
Conversely every nonnegative two by two matrix with these row/column
properties has the representation (23).

Take any finite number of products, with factors P_(ell,k) satisfying
(23), and choose eta_ell>=0 and delta_i>=0. Define

    P_ell=P_(ell,m) tensor ... tensor P_(ell,1),
    A=sum_ell eta_ell(I-P_ell)+diag(delta).          (24)

Thus EVERY actual finite multiplier is the concrete nonnegative sum

    lambda_ij=sum_ell eta_ell product_k P_(ell,k)[i_k,j_k], i!=j.   (25)

The diagonal of P is not an interpolation edge. The definition of A
sets the star coefficients via (3), rather than treating a diagonal
as a free nonnegative interpolation constraint. Its row sums b are
nonnegative because each P_ell has row sums at most1. Every column
sum of A is nonnegative. The only additional requirement for a>=0 is

    t=sum_ell eta_ell(1-product_k c_(ell,k))+delta_n>=2.          (26)

At all other indices the subtraction2e_n in (2) is zero. Equations
(24)--(26) therefore check every actual sign and all value coefficients.
The number of mixture terms and every parameter may depend on m; no
bounded-rank or fixed-library assumption is imposed.

This cone contains the valid seed of Section3. For any0<H<=sqrt(2),
put M=1+H, eta=4, and take a single factor

    P_H=[[1/2,1/(2M)],[1/2,1/2-1/(2M)]].           (27)

It has u=1/(2M),v=c=1/2, satisfies (23), and
4(I-P_H)=A_H. Equation (8) is its full one-step calibration. More
generally the cone contains all coordinate sums (6), including their
nonnegative limits: write a local two-node A_k as eta(I-P_k) with eta
large enough to keep both diagonal entries of P_k nonnegative, and
put identity factors at every other bit. Its row/column bounds follow
from those of A_k. The delta remains unchanged.

We now prove that every full PSD certificate from (24)--(26), on any
schedule with h_0>=7/5, still requires q>1/8.

For one product, temporarily omit the mixture label and put

    D0=product_k(1-v_k), L0=product_k(1-v_k+u_k),
    Dn=product_k(c_k-u_k), Cn=product_k c_k,
    Ln=product_k(c_k+v_k-u_k).

These are respectively the first diagonal, first row sum, last diagonal,
last column sum, and last row sum of P. The first column sum is1.
All are nonnegative and the row and column sums are at most1.

Two elementary product inequalities are decisive:

    0<=Ln-Cn<=1-L0,
    0<=Cn-Dn<=L0-D0.                                (28)

To prove them, for nonnegative vectors s,d define
F_d(s)=product_k(s_k+d_k)-product_k s_k. Its expansion is the sum
over nonempty subsets J of product_(k in J)d_k times
product_(k not in J)s_k. Each coefficient is nonnegative, so F_d
is coordinatewise nondecreasing in s.
For the first inequality of (28), set d_k=v_k-u_k>=0.
Then c_k<=1-d_k by (23), and

    Ln-Cn=F_d(c)<=F_d(1-d)=1-product_k(1-d_k)=1-L0.

For the second, set d_k=u_k. Since c_k-u_k<=1-v_k,

    Cn-Dn=F_u(c-u)<=F_u(1-v)=L0-D0.

Now restore the mixture label and define

    B_L=sum_ell eta_ell(1-L0_ell),
    R_L=sum_ell eta_ell(L0_ell-D0_ell),
    B_R=sum_ell eta_ell(Ln_ell-Cn_ell),
    R_R=sum_ell eta_ell(Cn_ell-Dn_ell).

Every number is nonnegative. By (28),

    B_R<=B_L, R_R<=R_L.                             (29)

The actual first and last coefficients of the full matrix (4) are

    z=b_0=B_L+delta_0,
    G_00=z-(h_0-1)R_L,
    b_n=t-B_R,
    G_nn=t+R_R-1.                                   (30)

For the first diagonal, the finite outgoing row sum at0 is R_L and
each such target j>0 has T_j0=h_0. For the last diagonal use
A_nn=sum eta(1-Dn)+delta_n=t+R_R and column n of V as before.
These prove (30) on the original formal space.

If0<q<=1/8 and the matrix were PSD, its x_0,g_0 principal submatrix
would imply exactly as in (12)

    B_L<=z<=4q<=1/2,
    R_L<=q/(h_0-1)<=5/16.

Equations (29)--(30) and the x_0,g_n principal submatrix would then
give, using t>=2,

    q >=(t-B_R)^2/[4(t+R_R-1)]
       >(t-1/2)^2/(4t)>=9/32,

a contradiction. Levels q<=0 are excluded by the same source/initial-row
argument in Section4. This proves q>1/8 for the entire dense mixture cone.

The critical and undeformed applications in Section5 follow unchanged.
The obstruction allows all directions of optimum incidence, all actual
interior pairs, arbitrarily many mixture terms, and arbitrary diagonal
optimum storage. It rules out this normalized tensor CONE, not all
nonnegative tensor-product arrays: a factor violating its first-column
normalization or its row/column bounds can leave the cone. Nor does it
cover a correction with negative mixture weights even if the final edge
array is nonnegative. The product inequalities (28), not a mere change
of basis or an assumed Huber kernel, are the mechanism behind the bound.

## 10. General first-query budget and required context variation

For context and a possible next mutation, the first part of the preceding
test is universal. In ANY nonnegative full certificate with h_0=H>1,
write b_0=lambda_(0,star) and r_0=sum_(j=1)^n lambda_0j. The x_0,g_0
principal submatrix is

    [[q,-b_0/2],[-b_0/2,b_0-(H-1)r_0]].             (31)

This follows directly from the general expansion (4), with no restrictions
on incoming terminal edges, optimum-source edges, or the rest of the array.
Since q>0, its determinant implies

    b_0<=4q, r_0<=q/(H-1).                          (32)

The proof is the identity
(b_0-2q)^2+4q(H-1)r_0<=4q^2. The earlier arbitrary-q queried-cut
draft367cf22cf1bd54cc retains more variables and all target-position
moments; (31)--(32) are compatible with that larger interface. They are
rederived here jointly, not imported from an unverified draft.

If a contextual extension of the binary-coordinate array has first
totals B_L,R_L and terminal totals B_R,R_R as in (30), t>=2, and
obeys B_R<=K B_L and R_R<=K R_L for some K>=1, then the same proof
shows

    q>1/(8K), provided h_0>=7/5.                    (33)

Indeed q<=1/(8K) would imply B_R<=1/2,R_R<=5/16 and also q<=1/8,
which reproduces the contradiction. Thus a useful q=O(1/M_m) in this
contextual extension requires K at least a constant times M_m. A bounded
change of context weights cannot repair the cone. This quantitative
condition is necessary only; it does not assert that larger variation
will make the full matrix PSD or that every possible certificate admits
this contextual parameterization.

## DOC-001054 — archive_full_20261010/project/research/shared_evidence/27ff70f6035b8eb1.md
