# Scope audit: the common-prefix queried law fails with a free terminal gradient

Status: **complete joint scope-correction candidate, UNVERIFIED**. This
continuation audits the completed eighth max4 assignment. It does not
start a new geometry branch or renew the cumulative launch cap. No solver,
parameter sweep, installation, or fact submission is used. The only
correctness predecessors are accepted facts
`1252b8823bb5a290` (original smooth-convex interpolation) and
`b98bb807d514e80d` (the exact benchmark and its elementary bound). All other
arguments used here are supplied within this document. In particular the
earlier named-amplitude obstruction is not a predecessor.

This document reproduces every needed calculation in one joint proof.
The earlier unverified draft is awareness, not a correctness predecessor.
The amplitudes below, at queried samples only, are arbitrary positive numbers.
They may depend on the entire known chronological history, the enclosing
horizon, and the full word.
No two copies of a subword are assumed to have matching amplitude profiles.

## 1. Statement, unchanged word, and every probability atom

Fix an integer m >= 1024. Define

    sigma = 1+sqrt(2), delta = 1-1/m,
    c_chr = sigma(1+1/m), a_chr = delta/c_chr,
    N = 2^m, n = N-1, epsilon = 1/N, eta = 1/N^2,
    l = 1+a_chr, k = 1+delta.

The actual word is H_chr,0 empty and

    H_chr,j+1 = (H_chr,j, 1+a_chr*c_chr^j, H_chr,j), 0 <= j < m.

Thus h_i = 1+a_chr*c_chr^(nu_2(i+1)), 0 <= i < n, where nu_2(q)
is the exponent of 2 dividing the positive integer q. Every subword retains
the same enclosing-m parameters. There are n physical steps and N samples.

Give sample i its standard m-bit binary label (i_0,...,i_(m-1)). For
s in {0,1}^m define

    chi_s(i) = (-1)^(sum_j s_j i_j),
    B(s) = (-1)^s_0,
    C_j(s) = (-1)^(s_1+...+s_j), 1 <= j < m.

These are real signs. The map from s to (B,C_1,...,C_(m-1)) is bijective:
(-1)^s_1=C_1 and (-1)^s_j=C_(j-1)C_j for j>=2. Put

    mu_+ = -7/10, mu_- = 9/10, p_chr = 1-epsilon,

    A_b(c_1,...,c_(m-1))
      = (1+mu_b)/2 * 1_(all c_j=1)
          + (1-mu_b)/2 * 1_(all c_j=-1),
    P_b(c_1,...,c_(m-1)) = product_j (1+mu_b*c_j)/2,

    w_s = (1+p_chr*B(s))/2
             * [(1-eta) A_(B(s))(C_1(s),...,C_(m-1)(s))
                     + eta P_(B(s))(C_1(s),...,C_(m-1)(s))].       (1)

Each conditional law sums to one. Every atom is strictly positive because
epsilon,eta>0 and both conditional means lie strictly between -1 and 1.
This is exactly the assigned finite probability law.

For ANY positive amplitudes Gamma_0,...,Gamma_(n-1), prescribe only

    g_i = (Gamma_i*sqrt(w_s)*chi_s(i))_s, 0 <= i < n.               (2)

There is NO character, norm, span, or sign constraint on the actual
terminal gradient g_n=grad F(X_n).

Suppose these are the actual gradients of ONE finite-dimensional convex
differentiable 1-smooth function F with a minimizer, along its actual GD:

    X_(i+1) = X_i-h_i*g_i, 0 <= i < n,
    g_i = grad F(X_i), 0 <= i <= n.

The same assertion covers any
Euclidean realization of the Gram matrix in (2). The anchor, function, and
values are unrestricted apart from these requirements. Define

    R = dist(X_0,argmin F), F_star = min F,
    V_chr = 2(F(X_n)-F_star)/R^2,
    T_path = sum_(i<n) h_i*Gamma_i.

Then R>0, and the claimed bounds are

    T_path < 800 R,                                         (3)
    ||X_i-X_star|| <= 129 R for every i,                     (4)
    ||g_n|| <= R[2(3/5)^(N/8)+2^24/N^2],                   (5)
    V_chr <= 1024(3/5)^(N/8)+2^33/N^2.                     (6)

Here X_star is a true closest minimizer. Since the exact original benchmark
obeys B_n <= 1+4*n^p, p=log_2(3), by fact `b98bb807d514e80d`,

    B_n V_chr <= (1+4*n^p)[1024(3/5)^((n+1)/8)
                                      +2^33/(n+1)^2] -> 0.     (7)

The bounds are uniform over ALL positive amplitudes and all actual legal
realizations of (2). They concern this fixed witness class, not unrestricted
W_n(h). Failure on these original dyadic words prevents this class from
establishing the all-schedule matching lower bound. It does not decide the
project's minimax question.

For L-smooth functions, take F=f/L and g_i=grad f(X_i)/L. The same proof then
uses the required normalization 2(f(X_n)-f_star)/(L R^2).

## 2. Necessary original inequalities and short-step consequences

The set of minimizers is nonempty and closed. A bounded minimizing sequence
for distance from X_0 has a convergent subsequence, so a closest minimizer
X_star exists. Positive Gamma_0 implies X_0 is not a minimizer, hence R>0.

Write f_i=F(X_i)-F_star. Accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290` supplies all
original ordered inequalities, including the optimum:

    Q_ij = f_i-f_j + <g_i,X_j-X_i> + ||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <= 0.

Adding the two orientations gives

    ||g_i-g_j||^2 <= <g_i-g_j,X_i-X_j>.                         (8)

In particular <g_i,X_i-X_star> >= ||g_i||^2. At queried samples i<n,
the norms in (2) are exactly Gamma_i. The terminal norm is ||g_n||.
Smoothness and grad F(X_star)=0 also give Gamma_i <= ||X_i-X_star|| for
i<n. For a physical step 0<h<=2, (8) gives

    ||X_(i+1)-X_star||^2
       <= ||X_i-X_star||^2-h(2-h)||g_i||^2,
    ||g_(i+1)|| <= ||g_i||.                                  (9)

For the second assertion, put d=g_(i+1)-g_i. Then
-h<d,g_i> >= ||d||^2, and expansion of ||g_i+d||^2 gives an increment
at most (1-2/h)||d||^2 <= 0. These facts will only be applied to the
explicit short physical steps l,k,l, all strictly between 0 and 2.

We will use the following rational bounds:

    7/5 < l < 3/2, 1 < k < 2, delta*c_chr < 5/2,
    epsilon < 1/65536, eta < 1/65536,
    p_chr >= 999/1000.                                      (10)

For example sqrt(2)<99/70 by squaring, so
c_chr<(169/70)(1025/1024)<49/20. Also delta>99/100, giving
delta/c_chr>(99/100)/(49/20)>2/5; c_chr>2 gives the upper bound on l.
Finally delta*c_chr=sigma(1-1/m^2)<sigma<5/2. The bounds on epsilon,eta
follow from m>=1024. No decimal approximation is needed.

## 3. A uniform movement bound before taking any small-mixture estimate

Select in (1) the component B=1 and all prefix signs common. Its mass is

    lambda = (1-epsilon/2)(1-eta), omega = 1-lambda.

Conditional on it, the only variable sign is C_1, with mean -7/10; all
character bits except s_1 are zero. Thus its unit sample directions are
two unit vectors a_0,b_0, in the exact period a_0,a_0,b_0,b_0, with
<a_0,b_0>=-beta_0, beta_0=7/10. The remaining measure has mass omega.
In a direct-sum Euclidean realization of the SAME Gram matrix,

    g_i = Gamma_i (sqrt(lambda)*u_i, sqrt(omega)*z_i), i<n,
    u_i in {a_0,b_0}, ||z_i||=1.                             (11)

Splitting a probability mixture proves this representation directly. An
isometry of gradient spans, extended to the initial displacement and any
orthogonal component, preserves every scalar product and the initial
distance. Thus it is legitimate for every realization in the statement.

Fix a block start t=4r, 0<=r<N/4. Before t put

    P_a = sum_(j<t,u_j=a_0) h_j*Gamma_j,
    P_b = sum_(j<t,u_j=b_0) h_j*Gamma_j, T=P_a+P_b.

Translate X_star to zero. If X_0=(x^c,x^r), then

    X_t = (x^c-sqrt(lambda)(P_a*a_0+P_b*b_0),
                         x^r-sqrt(omega)*W), ||W||<=T.

Define normalized coarse coordinates

    A_x = <a_0,x^c>/sqrt(lambda)-P_a+beta_0*P_b,
    B_x = <b_0,x^c>/sqrt(lambda)-P_b+beta_0*P_a.

The first two steps in the block have sizes l,k and both coarse directions
a_0. Their total amplitude-weighted mass is

    D_a = l*Gamma_t+k*Gamma_(t+1)>0.

Their displacement is (sqrt(lambda)*D_a*a_0,sqrt(omega)*Z), ||Z||<=D_a.
Both are short steps. The two distance inequalities in (9), added or
applied successively, therefore give

    lambda*D_a^2
       <= 2 lambda*D_a*A_x
            +2 sqrt(omega)(R+sqrt(omega)*T)*D_a.

With e=omega/lambda and r_0=sqrt(omega)*R/lambda, division gives

    D_a <= 2 A_x+2(r_0+eT).                                 (12)

At sample t+2 the coarse gradient direction is b_0. Its optimum
inequality <g_(t+2),X_(t+2)> >= ||g_(t+2)||^2 > 0 implies, after
bounding the remainder component by its norm,

    B_x+(beta_0+e)D_a+r_0+eT >= 0.

Substitution of (12), with positive coefficient beta_0+e, yields

    B_x+2(beta_0+e)A_x+[1+2(beta_0+e)](r_0+eT) >= 0.          (13)

Here omega<=epsilon/2+eta<1/65536, so

    e<1/1000, 1/sqrt(lambda)<1001/1000,
    sqrt(omega)/lambda<1/255.

Expanding (13) gives a bound on the nonnegative masses:

    c_a P_a+c_b P_b
       <= <b_0+2(beta_0+e)a_0,x^c>/sqrt(lambda)
                           +[1+2(beta_0+e)]r_0,

    c_a = beta_0+(1-2beta_0)e-2e^2,
    c_b = 1-2beta_0^2-(1+4beta_0)e-2e^2.                    (14)

Both coefficients exceed 1/64: their lower bounds at 0<=e<=1/1000
are 699598/1000000 and 16198/1000000. Also

    ||b_0+2(beta_0+e)a_0||^2 = 1+4e(beta_0+e)
                                      < (1002/1000)^2,
    1+2(beta_0+e)<5/2.

The right side of (14) is less than
[(1002/1000)(1001/1000)+(5/2)/255]R < 2R. Therefore

    T < 128 R at EVERY block start.                         (15)

This bound has been derived with arbitrary amplitudes still present; no
bounded-amplitude hypothesis or conditioning assumption has been imposed.
At a block start ||X_t||<=R+T<=129R. Its three short steps l,k,l preserve
this bound by (9). These blocks cover every sample 0,...,n, proving (4).
Every queried Gamma_i<=129R as well; smoothness also bounds ||g_n||.
At the last block the mass added after its start is at most
(2l+k)129R<5*129R. Thus

    T_path < (128+645)R < 800R,
    K_path := R+T_path < 1024R.                             (16)

In particular every gradient norm and every subinterval's total mass is
bounded by K_path. This estimate precedes the perturbation argument below.

## 4. Keep the finite epsilon component exactly: four coarse directions

Now split (1) in a DIFFERENT way: keep the whole common-prefix component,
of mass 1-eta, and regard only the conditional-product component of mass
eta as the remainder. In the common component write C_j=V for every j.
The four unit directions for indices modulo 4 are

    a_e=1, a_o=B, b_e=V, b_o=B V                              (17)

in the finite real probability space of (B,V). Their correlations are

    p = E B = 1-epsilon,
    z = E V = -7/10+4epsilon/5,
    w = E B V = -7/10-epsilon/10,

    <a_e,a_o>=<b_e,b_o>=p,
    <a_o,b_o>=<a_e,b_e>=z,
    <a_e,b_o>=<a_o,b_e>=w.                                 (18)

These formulas average the stated conditional means in (1). In particular
finite epsilon has NOT been replaced by zero. Put beta=-z. We have

    69/100 <= beta <= 7/10, p>0, p-z*w>0,
    1-k*z^2 >= 1-2(7/10)^2 = 1/50.                         (19)

The positivity p-zw follows already from p>=999/1000,
|z|<=7/10 and |w|<701/1000.

Let v_i denote the coarse direction (17) at sample i, and let u_i denote
its unit direction under the FULL measure (1). Splitting the probability
mixture gives, for ALL original queried i,j<n,

    |<u_i,u_j>-<v_i,v_j>| <= 2 eta.                         (20)

For a pair with amplitudes A,B and intervening total mass T_ij, replacing
all its directions by their coarse versions changes the cocoercivity
expression <g_i-g_j,X_i-X_j>-||g_i-g_j||^2 by at most

    2 eta(A+B)T_ij +4 eta A B
           <= 4 eta K_path(A+B).                           (21)

The first term comes from the actual displacement sum, one scalar product
at a time; the second comes from the cross term in the squared gradient
difference. Individual squared norms are unchanged. The final inequality
uses (16) and 2AB<=K_path(A+B). Thus (21) accounts explicitly for the
whole amplitude-weighted trajectory, rather than merely the Gram entries.

## 5. Every consecutive odd-b return can grow only by O(eta K_path)

Let j=4r+3 and suppose j+4<n. These are consecutive odd-b samples.
Their four intervening steps and directions are

    j:     H on b_o, amplitude B_0=Gamma_j,
    j+1:   l on a_e, amplitude A_0=Gamma_(j+1),
    j+2:   k on a_o, amplitude C_0=Gamma_(j+2),
    j+3:   l on b_e, amplitude E_0=Gamma_(j+3),
    j+4:            b_o, amplitude D_0=Gamma_(j+4).

The physical H may be arbitrarily large. No amplitude ratios are imposed.
Temporarily set a=a_o, b=b_o, tau=<a,b>=z=-beta, and

    Z = -H B_0 b-l A_0 a_e,
    X = <b,Z>, Y=<a-tau*b,Z>.

By (18),(19),

    Y = -l A_0(p-z*w) <= 0.                                (22)

The ORIGINAL pair between j and j+2, together with (21), therefore gives

    C_0^2+B_0^2-2tau B_0 C_0
       <= (tau C_0-B_0)X+C_0 Y+e_0(C_0+B_0)
       <= (tau C_0-B_0)X+e_0(C_0+B_0),                     (23)

where e_0=4 eta K_path. Suppose Delta=D_0-B_0>0. The ORIGINAL pair
between j and j+4 similarly gives

    Delta[X-k C_0 tau-l E_0 p]
       >= Delta^2-e_0(D_0+B_0).

Consequently

    X >= Delta+k C_0 tau+l E_0 p-e_0(D_0+B_0)/Delta.         (24)

Substitute (24) into (23). Its coefficient tau C_0-B_0 is negative.
Putting M=B_0+beta C_0>0 gives exactly

    (1-k*tau^2)C_0^2 + tau(k-2)B_0 C_0+B_0^2
           +M Delta+M l E_0 p
       <= e_0(C_0+B_0)+M e_0(D_0+B_0)/Delta.                (25)

Every discarded term on the left is nonnegative: tau<0, k<2, p>0.
It follows from (19) that the left side is at least

    (C_0^2+B_0^2)/100 + M Delta.

On the right, D_0+B_0=Delta+2B_0, so it is at most

    e_0[(1+beta)C_0+2B_0] + (2e_0/Delta)B_0 M
       <= 4e_0 M + (3e_0/Delta)(C_0^2+B_0^2).             (26)

Here beta>=69/100 proves the first bound, and
B_0 M <= (3/2)(B_0^2+C_0^2) proves the second.
If Delta>=1024e_0, (26) is at most

    M Delta/256 + (3/1024)(C_0^2+B_0^2),

strictly less than the lower bound for (25). This is a contradiction.
Thus every such return obeys

    Gamma_(j+4) <= Gamma_j + d_amp,
    d_amp = 4096 eta K_path <= 2^22 eta R.                  (27)

This uses two pairs of ORIGINAL samples and their actual displacement
sums. The profiles on either side need not match in any way. If eta=0,
the same argument with zero error directly rules out Delta>0, so the
exact four-ray boundary has nonincreasing odd-b amplitudes.

## 6. Every other return contracts by 3/5, for the full finite measure

For any original bridge of level q>=1 and its following leaf, provided
both endpoints are queried, let the three amplitudes be A,M,D and the
two steps 1+u,l. The relative character
signs of the three samples are 1,B C_q,C_q. This follows because adding
one to an odd binary label with q trailing ones flips bits 0,...,q; the
following leaf flips bit 0 once more. Therefore the FULL law, including
its conditional-product part, gives correlations w,p,z in that order.
Both mixture components have the same individual conditional prefix mean.

The two original inequalities between the endpoints give the exact sum

    0 >= -u A^2+D^2+(u-1)z A D+l M(pD-wA).                 (28)

The following leaf is short, so (9) gives M>=D. Since p>0 and w<0,
pD-wA>0. Hence the ratio q_amp=D/A satisfies

    F_u(q_amp) <= 0,
    F_u(q) = (1+lp)q^2+[(u-1)z-lw]q-u.                    (29)

This is a necessary inequality with arbitrary adjacent slack. Its positive
leading coefficient and negative constant term give exactly one positive
root; F_u(q)<=0 for positive q places q below that root.

At level 1, u=delta<1. Bounds (10),(18) give

    1+lp >= 11993/5000,
    (u-1)z-lw >= (7/10)l >= 49/50,
    F_delta(1/2) >= 1793/20000 > 0.

Thus every low bridge followed by its leaf has gain less than 1/2.

At level 2, u=delta*c_chr lies between 1 and 5/2. Since z>=-7/10,
w<=-7/10 and l>7/5,

    (u-1)z-lw >= (7/10)(1+l-u) >= -7/100,
    F_u(6/5) >= 108748/125000 > 0.

Thus every level-2 bridge followed by its leaf has gain less than 6/5.
If the H in section 5 has level 2 and j+4<n, its return is exactly the concatenation
of this compound step and a level-1 compound step. Therefore

    Gamma_(j+4) <= (3/5) Gamma_j at every level-2 return.    (30)

No value, anchor, copy profile, or equality assumption was used in this
calculation. The even samples and their physical leaves are retained.

## 7. Stop the character argument before the free terminal

Put K=N/4, which is even. The QUERIED odd-b samples are

    B_r = Gamma_(4r+3), 0 <= r <= K-2.

The missing next sample, at r=K-1, is exactly n and has NO prescribed
character direction. All returns used here end at or before n-4.
For 0<=r<=K-3, the actual bridge at index 4r+3 has level

    nu_2(4r+4)=2+nu_2(r+1).

It has level 2 exactly when r is even. Equations (27),(30), proved above
using queried original pairs only, give

    B_(2s+1) <= theta B_(2s), theta=3/5,
    B_(2s+2) <= B_(2s+1)+d_amp,

whenever the displayed queried indices exist. Thus for E_s=B_(2s),

    E_(s+1) <= theta E_s+d_amp.

There are K/2-1 such updates up to the final QUERIED odd-b sample n-4.
Explicit induction gives, including the empty sum when s=0,

    E_s <= theta^s B_0+d_amp sum_(q=0)^(s-1) theta^q.

The formula holds at s=0. Multiplication by theta and addition of d_amp
changes the sum to sum_(q=0)^s theta^q, proving its next instance. Hence

    Gamma_(n-4) <= theta^(N/8-1) B_0+(5/2)d_amp.             (31)

The first three physical steps are l,k,l<2, so B_0=Gamma_3<=Gamma_0<=R.
The last FOUR physical steps, at indices n-4,...,n-1, are exactly

    1+delta*c_chr, l, k, l.

Indeed n-4=N-5 has nu_2((n-4)+1)=nu_2(N-4)=2;
nu_2(N-3)=0, nu_2(N-2)=1, and nu_2(N-1)=0. The first two steps form a
level-2 bridge plus leaf with queried endpoints n-4 and n-2. Applying
(29) at these queried endpoints gives

    Gamma_(n-2) <= (6/5) Gamma_(n-4).

The remaining actual steps k and l are both less than 2. Applying (9),
with no directional constraint on the actual terminal gradient g_n, gives

    ||g_n|| <= Gamma_(n-1) <= Gamma_(n-2)
             <= (6/5) Gamma_(n-4)
             <= 2 theta^(N/8) R+3 d_amp.                   (32)

Here (6/5)/theta=2. Since d_amp<=2^22 eta R, eta=N^-2 and
3*2^22<2^24, this proves (5). No extra sample or step is introduced.

The movement proof in section 3 uses the optimum inequality only at each
block's sample t+2; in the final block this is n-1, still queried. It uses
the actual short final leaf only through distance monotonicity. Therefore
||X_n-X_star||<=129R remains valid without prescribing g_n. Convexity gives

    F(X_n)-F_star <= <g_n,X_n-X_star> <= 129R ||g_n||.

After division by the TRUE distance squared,

    V_chr <= 516 theta^(N/8)+258*2^24/N^2
           <= 1024 theta^(N/8)+2^33/N^2.

The last comparison uses 516<1024 and 258<512. The original exact benchmark
is not replaced by a mass surrogate: accepted b98bb807d514e80d gives
B_n<=1+4n^p, p=log_2(3)<2. A geometric exponential in N dominates N^p,
and N^(p-2) tends to zero, proving (7) uniformly over the entire queried-
amplitude/anchor/function class. The horizon remains n=2^m-1.

The argument also covers the maximal prefix interpolant. For any legal
queried jets including an optimum, accepted 1252b8823bb5a290 constructs
a convex 1-smooth interpolant with that minimizer and with maximal terminal
value. This is a realizing function in the present class, so the argument
uses its TRUE closest-minimizer distance as R. Thus maximal terminal
completion cannot evade (6). A reduced-feasible terminal value is at most
that maximal interpolant's value by the accepted fact. If the supplied
optimum lies within distance 1 of X_0, then R<=1, so (6) also bounds twice
that reduced terminal value in the normalized finite formulation. This
observation imposes neither an incoming formal terminal inequality nor
a terminal character direction.

For clarity, every character-specific pair used in the proof has queried
endpoints: the mass argument uses the optimum and t+2<=n-1; the return
argument uses (j,j+2) and (j,j+4) with j+4<=n-4; and the final compound
pair is (n-4,n-2). The only terminal norm step is the actual short-step
inequality for the realizing function. When starting from reduced formal
jets, that function is obtained before applying this inequality; the
formal terminal gradient is never equated with its actual gradient.

The short-step hypothesis cannot be silently dropped: for F(x)=x^2/2,
a step h>2 from x!=0 produces gradient (1-h)x, whose norm exceeds |x|.
Here both remaining physical steps k,l are strictly below 2, as proved
in (10). This is a scope check, not a counterexample to the assertion.

## 8. The fixed finite class is nonempty

This is a nonvacuity construction, not a competitive lower witness. It also
shows that the obstruction does not mean the Gram itself is illegal.
For the SAME full-support weights (1) and the SAME actual word, this
example additionally prescribes the terminal character gradient. Define
Gamma_i below for every 0<=i<=n, and put

    H_max=max_i h_i,
    kappa_chr=min(1,(min_i h_i-1)/4)>0,
    q_decay=kappa_chr/[8(H_max+kappa_chr+1)],
    Gamma_i=q_decay^i, f_i=(kappa_chr+1/2)Gamma_i^2.

In this example set g_i=(Gamma_i*sqrt(w_s)*chi_s(i))_s for ALL 0<=i<=n.
All N characters are present and have positive weight, so the character
matrix is invertible. Indeed
sum_s chi_s(i)chi_s(j)=N for i=j and 0 otherwise, by pairing the sign
of a differing bit. Hence the Gram K_ij=<g_i,g_j> is positive definite.
Define

    b_i=(kappa_chr+1)Gamma_i^2+sum_(t<i)h_t K_ti,
    X_0=sum_i (K^(-1)b)_i g_i,
    X_i=X_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t.

These finite formulas fix one anchor before GD is run. They give
<X_i,g_i>=(kappa_chr+1)Gamma_i^2. For i<j direct expansion gives

    Q_ij=(kappa_chr+1-h_i)Gamma_i^2-kappa_chr Gamma_j^2
                          -sum_(i<t<j)h_t K_it-K_ij,
    Q_ji=(kappa_chr+1)Gamma_j^2-kappa_chr Gamma_i^2
                          +(h_i-1)K_ij+sum_(i<t<j)h_t K_jt.

Using |K_it|<=Gamma_i Gamma_t, q_decay<1/2 and h_i-1>=4kappa_chr,
the first residual divided by Gamma_i^2 is at most

    -3kappa_chr+H_max*q_decay/(1-q_decay)+q_decay
       <= -3kappa_chr+2(H_max+1)q_decay
       <= -11kappa_chr/4 < 0.

The second is at most

    -kappa_chr+(kappa_chr+1)q_decay^2
                             +H_max*q_decay/(1-q_decay)
       <= -kappa_chr+(2H_max+kappa_chr+1)q_decay
       <= -3kappa_chr/4 < 0.

For the optimum jet (0,0,0), Q_(i,star)=0 and
Q_(star,i)=-kappa_chr Gamma_i^2<0. Thus ALL original ordered pairs,
including the optimum and terminal, have been checked.

For an explicit one-function realization, let

    C_poly=conv(0,g_0,...,g_n),
    F_poly(X)=max_(v in C_poly) [<X,v>-||v||^2/2].

The unique maximizer is the nearest-point projection P_(C_poly)(X).
Its normal condition is <X-P_(C_poly)(X),v-P_(C_poly)(X)><=0 for every
v in C_poly. Adding this condition at X and Y proves that the projection
is 1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two maximizing expressions bounds the
first-order remainder for F_poly between 0 and ||X-Y||^2, so F_poly is
differentiable with gradient P_(C_poly), convex, finite, and 1-smooth.

At each prescribed sample the displayed residuals say
<X_i-g_i,g_j-g_i>=Q_ji<=0, and
<X_i-g_i,-g_i>=-kappa_chr Gamma_i^2<0. Thus the normal condition holds
on every vertex and their convex hull: grad F_poly(X_i)=g_i and
F_poly(X_i)=f_i. The fixed function therefore follows the stated GD.
It is nonnegative and minimized at zero. Its full minimizer set is
{Z:<Z,g_i><=0 for all i}, by the same normal condition at the zero vertex.
The true distance to this polar cone is positive and is the R used in
(3)--(7); no claim equates it with ||X_0||.

## 9. Scope and unresolved changes

The exclusion covers EVERY positive QUERIED amplitude choice for (1),(2),
including unbounded, history-dependent, nonsaturating, and nonmatching choices, all
anchors and values that actually interpolate, every terminal completion,
and every m>=1024. It keeps
the B=-1 mass exactly in the return argument and pays only the product
mixture eta=N^-2 there. The separate initial mass proof pays the full
epsilon/2+eta remainder before bounding ANY amplitudes.

The same derivation also covers the exact eta=0 boundary, and epsilon=0
when considered with the same physical word: zero perturbation yields
nonincreasing returns directly. It does not assume positive definiteness
for the upper bound. Positive definiteness is used only for the separate
full-support nonvacuity example.

Different measures and different binary labels remain outside. In
particular the coercive margins 1-2*(7/10)^2 and 1-k*z^2 are essential;
a geometry at or beyond absolute ray correlation 1/sqrt(2) is not covered
by this proof. Nor does a larger arbitrary full-support component come
with a vanishing exact-B_n comparison: the estimate retains its eta term.
The terminal gradient is completely free. All realizing functions and
the maximal prefix interpolant from accepted 1252b8823bb5a290 are covered.
No claim is made about arbitrary schedules or the accepted unrestricted
finite H6 witness.

Matlas and web searches for long-step cone/length bounds and gradient-norm
monotonicity did not supply an applicable external theorem. The returned
short-step theorems cannot be applied to this whole word. Every short-step
fact actually used is derived above from accepted interpolation. This
remains the already audited finite interpolation regime; no additional
unread body of external theory is a premise.

The result is a full scoped PREFIX OBSTRUCTION CANDIDATE, not an accepted
fact. It corrects the earlier draft's claim that a free terminal lies
outside the proof's reach; it does not change the law.
The current assignment prohibits fact_submit. The original all-h minimax
target remains unresolved and is not finalized.

This concerns a virtual proof comparison, not a candidate seed or an independent necessary test. Fix C0=193/25, U0=193/109, N0=U0+7/3=1342/327, L0=C0-N0=29561/8175, and R(D)=(L0-D)^2(D-2)/[L0-(L0-D)D(D-2)] on D in[1+sqrt(2),14/5]. The denominator is positive because c*d=(L0-D)(D-2)<25/36<1. Direct differentiation of 1/R gives R'(D)=L0*R(D)^2*[c(1+d^2)-2d]/(c^3*d^2), where c=L0-D and d=D-2. At D=14/5, L0<11/3 implies c<13/15 and d=4/5. Thus c(1+d^2)-2d < (13/15)(41/25)-8/5=-67/375<0, so R'(14/5)<0. The needed positive LOWER bound nonetheless follows from F(D)=(D-2)(L0-D)(L0-(1-rbar)D)-rbar*L0, with rbar=(383/772)^2: F''<0 on the entire interval, both endpoint values are positive, and the positive denominator identifies F>0 with R>rbar. The full joint proof derives these identities and every actual witness row; this shortcut correction does not establish any full-system survivor.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001056 -->
# A quantitative first-row gap for the full primitive certificate

Status: complete joint proof DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is max4 round28,
the final round allocated by TASK.md and guidance8211d6128deabdf3,
with the corrected accounting instructions c9ebc26382c63e1d.
No fact_submit is authorized. The accepted mathematical input is
04219554e41d9750, namely its original full five-gradient primitive
certificate identity. All other assertions used here are proved below.

The new obstruction couples a lifted middle-cut energy to the actual
first row of the full PSD matrix and its unit-quadratic energy. It keeps
all twenty nonnegative multipliers, all five formal gradients, and every
flow equation. No rank, support, order, symmetry, or quadratic equality
is assumed. It does not construct a threshold seed or prove that every
threshold seed is impossible.

## 1. Statement and notation

Write a positive-excess four-step word as

    h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d),    a,b,c,d>0,
    C=1+sum h=5+a+b+c+d.

Suppose it admits the full primitive certificate of accepted fact
04219554e41d9750. Suppose additionally that a>sqrt(2)-1. Define

    u=1+a,             delta=u^2-2,
    H=3+c+d,           eta=b/H,
    R=a+3+1/a,         k=u*delta/a,
    nu=(1+eta)*(1-a*eta)/a,
    Erow=R-k*eta-C^2*eta^2,
    Qenergy=1-C^2*a^2*b^2*c^2*d^2,
    Wrow=max(1+b,1+b*c*d),
    Krow=max(u,a*Wrow).

Full feasibility forces a<1, a*eta<1, nu>0, and
0<=Qenergy<1. The following quantitative necessary condition holds:

    u*delta <= Krow*Erow/nu + 2*sqrt(Qenergy*Erow/nu).          (A)

In particular,

    Erow >= nu * [u*delta /
          (sqrt(Qenergy+Krow*u*delta)+sqrt(Qenergy))]^2 > 0.  (B)

Thus equality in the previously used lifted middle-cut condition Erow>=0
is impossible throughout this initial-step regime. A version avoiding
square roots is

    Erow>=0,
    [max(u*delta*nu-Krow*Erow,0)]^2 <= 4*Qenergy*Erow*nu.     (C)

If a*b<=1 and c*d<=1, the simpler strict necessary inequality is

    Erow > nu*delta^2/u^2,                                 (D)

equivalently

    b < (a+3)*(C-a-2) / ((a+1)*(C+a+2)).                (E)

The last two product assumptions are explicit hypotheses of this
simplification. They are not silently declared lossless for the
original problem. Conditions(A)--(C) do not require them. The separate
assumption a,b,c,d>0 is also retained explicitly; no assertion about a
zero step or a step at most one is being imported from an unverified
compactness draft.

## 2. Original formal certificate and its constant kernel

Put P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, s=P_4=C-1, and let e be the five-entry all-ones
column. Write e_i for the i-th original standard coordinate of R^5,
with indices0 through4. Let T have diagonal1, entry h_t at(i,t) for t<i, and zero
entries above the diagonal. The original twenty multipliers satisfy

    Lambda_ij>=0 for i!=j, Lambda_ii=0,
    L=diag(Lambda*e)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2s)^T,
    D=diag(h0(2-h0),h1(2-h1),h2(2-h2),h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)),
    L^T e=omega,
    M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2 >=0 on ALL of R^5.               (1)

The identity in accepted04219554e41d9750 is

    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij
                      =sum_(i,j) M_ij <g_i,g_j>,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f4)
          +sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g4||^2,
    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i.                                (2)

Since Te=e+P and sum_(i<4)h_i P_i=(s^2-sum h_i^2)/2,

    e^TDe=-s^2-sum h_i^2,
    omega^TTe=s^2+sum h_i^2,
    e^TMe=0.

For every real v, the nonnegative quadratic(e+t*v)^T M(e+t*v)
has zero constant coefficient. Considering both signs of t forces
v^TMe=0, hence Me=0. The initial row of this identity is

    sum_(j>0)Lambda_0j P_j=2h0.                        (3)

To check(3) without any suppressed flow condition, direct expansion
gives(Me)_i=h_i(1+P_i)+(LP)_i/2 for i<4. At i=0,
P0=0 and(LP)_0=-sum_(j>0)Lambda_0j P_j.

Let Aout=sum_(j>0)Lambda_0j and m=M00. Its original diagonal is

    m=1-a^2-a*Aout.                                    (4)

Indeed the first column of T is(1,u,u,u,u)^T, so(LT)_00
=(1-u)Aout=-a*Aout. Since m,Aout>=0 and a>0, (4) gives
a<=1. Equality would give m=Aout=0, contradicting(3). Thus a<1.

For any positive semidefinite real matrix, the two-vector inequality

    |v^T M w|^2 <= (v^T M v)*(w^T M w)                 (5)

follows by applying nonnegativity to(v+t*w)^T M(v+t*w).
If w^T M w=0, the linear coefficient must vanish; otherwise minimizing
in t proves(5). This argument does not assume positive definiteness.

## 3. Two exact scalar energies

Here clamp(x,[-eta,1]) means min(1,max(-eta,x)).
The scalar function with this derivative and minimum0
has the actual trajectory

    x=(2+a,1,-b,-(2+d)*eta,-eta),
    g=(1,1,-eta,-eta,-eta).

Here b=(3+c+d)*eta. Its derivative is continuous, nondecreasing, and
1-Lipschitz. At every displayed sample its value is
f_i=x_i*g_i-g_i^2/2. The first step gives x1=1; the other three GD
updates follow directly from the displayed relation for b.

For any GD data with saturated values
f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, telescoping the squared distances gives

    V_h=||x0||^2-||x4+s*g4||^2.                        (6)

In detail, the first two sums in(2) reduce to
2sum h_i<x_i,g_i>-sum h_i^2||g_i||^2-2s*f4;
the first two terms telescope to||x0||^2-||x4||^2.
Substitution of f4 then gives(6). The scalar trajectory has x4=g4,
so its energy is(2+a)^2-C^2*eta^2. By(1)--(2) it is nonnegative.
Consequently

    C*eta<=2+a,
    a*eta<=a*(2+a)/C<3/5<1,                           (7)

because 0<a<1 and C>5. This proves nu>0.

Next define the actual unit-quadratic gradient column

    p=(1,-a,a*b,-a*b*c,a*b*c*d)^T.

It satisfies Tp=e. Since Le=0,

    p^T M p=p^T D p=1-C^2*p4^2=Qenergy.               (8)

For the telescoping equality, p_(i+1)=(1-h_i)p_i gives
h_i(2-h_i)p_i^2=p_i^2-p_(i+1)^2; the terminal diagonal
then supplies-s(s+2)p4^2. Full PSD gives Qenergy>=0.
All a,b,c,d are positive, so Qenergy<1.

## 4. Complete lifted-cut data and its exact multiplier cost

Let U=(1,0), V=(0,1) be orthonormal physical vectors. Set

    z=-eta*U+sqrt(nu)*V.

Then

    ||z||^2=[1+(1-a)*eta]/a,
    <U,z>+a||z||^2=1-a*eta.

Use the five two-dimensional data

    X=(U+u*z, U, -b*U, -(2+d)*eta*U, -eta*U),
    G=(z, U, -eta*U, -eta*U, -eta*U),
    F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.                         (9)

Every GD update has the original step length. For these saturated
values the original ordered interpolation residual is exactly

    Q_ij=<G_i-G_j,X_j-G_j>.

Here is a check of all twenty ordered finite pairs, grouped by their
target j. Targets1 and4 have zero normal. At target0 the normal is
U+a*z. The incoming pair(1,0) has residual0, whereas each incoming
pair(i,0), i=2,3,4, has residual-a*nu. At target2 the normal is
-(b-eta)U. The only nonzero finite residual there is
Q_12=-(1+eta)(b-eta). At target3 the normal is
-(1+d)*eta*U. The only nonzero finite residual there is
Q_13=-(1+eta)(1+d)*eta. Thus all twenty residuals are nonpositive.
The zero optimum jet can also be added: Q_(i,star)=0 by the chosen
values; the opposite inequalities follow from the same normals and
a*eta<1. These optimum pairs are not additional multipliers in(1).

For completeness these are actual jets of one global smooth convex
function. Let Kphys=conv{0,z,U,-eta*U} and define

    F(X)=max_(G in Kphys)(<X,G>-||G||^2/2).

The comparisons just made say that X_i-G_i is an outward normal
to Kphys at G_i. Completing the square identifies the unique maximizer
as the Euclidean projection of X onto Kphys. If projections at X,Y
are G,H, adding their normal inequalities gives
||G-H||^2<=<G-H,X-Y>, and hence ||G-H||<=||X-Y||.
To see differentiability directly, optimality at X and X+v bounds
F(X+v)-F(X)-<G,v> between0 and<G(X+v)-G,v>, which is at most||v||^2.
Thus the gradient is the projection and is1-Lipschitz. The function
is convex as a maximum of affine functions of X; it has minimum0 at0
because0 belongs to Kphys. It realizes all of(9).

By(6), its primitive energy is

    ||U+u*z||^2-C^2*eta^2
      =a+3+1/a-(u*delta/a)*eta-C^2*eta^2=Erow.            (10)

Let

    Bin=Lambda20+Lambda30+Lambda40.

In the U coordinate the gradient column is-eta*e+(1+eta)*e1;
in the V coordinate it is sqrt(nu)*e0. The constant kernel proved
in Section2 therefore gives the full matrix remainder
(1+eta)^2*M11+nu*m. Substituting every nonzero residual above into
the original identity(2) yields the exact accounting identity

    Erow=nu*(m+a*Bin)+(1+eta)^2*M11
       +(1+eta)*eta*((2+c+d)*Lambda12+(1+d)*Lambda13). (11)

Every term on the right is nonnegative. In particular

    0<=m<=m+a*Bin<=Erow/nu.                              (12)

No incoming coefficient has been set to zero. Identity(11) prices the
original incoming coefficients explicitly.

## 5. The original first row couples the two energies

Define the weighted incoming combination

    Bweight=(1+b)*Lambda20+(1-b*c)*Lambda30
                                    +(1+b*c*d)*Lambda40.

The exact first-row identity is

    2*e0^T M p = u*(m-delta)+a^2*Bweight.             (13)

Here e0 denotes the original first coordinate column of R5. A direct
derivation avoids any coordinate reduction. Since Tp=e and Le=0,
Mp=Dp+T^T L^T p/2. Also Te0=u*e-a*e0, giving

    2*e0^T M p=2(1-a^2)-a*p^T L e0.

The flow at node0 gives Lambda10=Aout+2u-Bin. Hence

    p^T L e0
      =Aout+a*Lambda10-a*b*Lambda20
                        +a*b*c*Lambda30-a*b*c*d*Lambda40
      =u*Aout+2a*u-a*Bweight.

Finally substitute a*Aout=1-a^2-m from(4). The constant term becomes

    (2-u)(1-a^2)-2a^2*u
      =u*((1-a)^2-2a^2)=-u*delta,

which proves(13), with all row and flow contributions retained.

All three incoming multipliers are nonnegative. Since b,c,d>0,
each coefficient in Bweight is at most Wrow. This remains true even
if 1-b*c is negative. Thus Bweight<=Wrow*Bin. Applying(5) with e0,p,
and using(8), gives from(13)

    u*delta <= u*m+a^2*Wrow*Bin+2*sqrt(m*Qenergy)
             <= Krow*(m+a*Bin)+2*sqrt(m*Qenergy)
             <= Krow*Erow/nu+2*sqrt(Qenergy*Erow/nu).        (14)

This is(A). Put t=sqrt(Erow/nu). Because Krow,u,delta are positive,
(14) says

    Krow*t^2+2*sqrt(Qenergy)*t-u*delta>=0.

Solving this increasing quadratic for t>=0 and rationalizing its
positive root gives(B). Its numerator is strictly positive, so Erow>0.
Multiplying(A) by nu and squaring only when its left side after
subtraction is positive gives exactly(C). There is no sign-losing
squaring in this formulation.

## 6. A rational strengthened middle-cut boundary

If c*d<=1, then Wrow=1+b. If also a*b<=1, then
a*Wrow=a+a*b<=1+a=u, so Krow=u. Since Qenergy<1 and Erow>0,
(14) implies the strict inequality

    u*delta < u*t^2+2t,       t=sqrt(Erow/nu)>0.

The right side is strictly increasing for t>=0. Its value at
t=delta/u is

    delta^2/u+2delta/u=delta*(delta+2)/u=u*delta,

because delta+2=u^2. It follows that t>delta/u, which is(D).
The exact algebraic identity

    Erow-nu*delta^2/u^2
      =[(a+3-delta*eta)^2-u^2*C^2*eta^2]/u^2          (15)

can be checked by expanding the constant, linear, and quadratic
coefficients in eta. Those coefficients in the numerator are
(a+3)^2, -2(a+3)*delta, and delta^2-u^2*C^2.

The first factor a+3-delta*eta is positive: by a*eta<1,

    a+3-delta*eta > a+3-delta/a = 1+1/a >0.

Thus(D) and(15) give

    a+3 > (u*C+delta)*eta.

Substitute eta=b/(C-2-a-b), whose denominator is H>0. Since
delta+a+3=(a+1)(a+2), the result is exactly(E). Every denominator
in(E) is positive.

In particular this is a necessary bound on an entire step region,
not a condition on a sparse coefficient family. The exact stronger
condition(A) still retains the actual Qenergy rather than discarding
it in favor of the upper bound1.

## 7. Adversarial scope checks

The initial-step hypothesis is substantive. At a=sqrt(2)-1 the
quantity delta is zero, and(A) supplies no positive gap. This agrees
with the possibility of saturated basic certificates at that endpoint.
We do not extend the strict conclusion by continuity across delta=0.

If b*c>1, the coefficient of Lambda30 in(13) is negative. The proof
does not replace this coefficient by its absolute value or infer a
false lower bound. It uses only the valid upper bound Bweight<=Wrow*Bin.
Thus(A)--(C) still apply in that regime. The simplified statement(E)
requires only its explicitly stated a*b and c*d hypotheses.

If Qenergy=0, formula(B) reduces to Erow>=nu*u*delta/Krow;
no division by Qenergy occurs. If a multiplier vanishes or M has
additional kernels, the semidefinite form of(5) still applies. No
strict complementarity, full rank, or strictly positive support was used.

Here is a purely symbolic test family showing why the separate tests
Erow>=0 and Qenergy>=0 cannot justify the omitted coupling. It is an
analytical counterexample to that shortcut, not a numerical seed proposal
or a claim to survive the other existing necessary inequalities. Take

    a=1/2, eta=7/24, C=39*sqrt(2)/7,
    H=24*(C-5/2)/31, b=7*(C-5/2)/31, Ktail=H-3,
    0<d<min(Ktail/2,1/Ktail,1/(C*a*b*Ktail)), c=Ktail-d.

The elementary bounds 7/5<sqrt(2)<10/7 give
193/25<39/5<C<8. Thus Ktail>0, all four excesses are positive,
a*b<1, c*d<1, and 0<C*a*b*c*d<1. Also C=5+a+b+c+d
and eta=b/(3+c+d). Direct exact substitution gives

    R-k*eta=169/32=C^2*eta^2,
    Erow=0, Qenergy>0,
    nu=1271/576, delta=1/4, u=3/2.

Yet(D) requires Erow>1271/20736. Hence none of these words has the
full certificate. This illustrates the active original-row obstruction;
it is not asserted to be a newly uncovered region beyond every prior
obstruction. No finite numerical values were evaluated for this family.

## 8. Discovery provenance and remaining problem

The first half of the allocated two-round batch was already recorded
in339cfa9a90f403ec and the corrected accounting handoff b94ad81b2b227c34.
At the start of this round the owner ledger had37 charged cases,
32 discovery cases, and30.953246246000163 accounted seconds.

This round made24 additional pre-registered discovery requests in
public batch3 and batch4. Every case was evaluated inside the main-owned
Meter and launched through run_seed_with_remaining_budget.py with its
cumulative hard timeout. Batch3 added the original C83 quadratic bound
to endpoint selection. For fixed C,a,b, put

    Qprefix=3+a+b,
    j0=(Qprefix^2+1/(a^2*b))/(1+b),
    j1=(1/(a^2*b)-b*Qprefix^2)/(1+b),
    t83=2*j0/(sqrt(j1^2+4*C^2*j0)-j1).

On b*Qprefix<C the scalar cut bounds c/(2+d)<1/b, so the previously
derived repaired-cut condition C^2*t^2<=j0+j1*t becomes t<=t83.
With Ktail=c+d this is the endpoint lower bound
d>=(Ktail-2*t83)/(1+t83). Batch4 also selected b as a registered
fraction of the explicit mixed-cut/first-triangle upper bounds. This
was one candidate construction per charged callback, not an inner
sweep. The source and request hashes, original statuses, and endpoint
diagnostics are retained publicly. These recalled screening formulas
are not mathematical premises of Sections1--7.

All24 requests were prefiltered:21 had empty endpoint components and
3 failed the scalar conditions independent of the last endpoint. There
was no new conic solve. The final allocation readback was

    charged_cases_used=61 of64,
    discovery_cases_used=56 of56,
    measured_seconds_used=31.476150974999655,
    external_timeout_seconds_charged=11.999997916000211,
    accounted_seconds_used=43.476148890999866 of60.

The whole two-round allocation therefore retains20 returned conic solves
(18 optimal,2 optimal_inaccurate),40 prefiltered requests, and the one
conservatively charged startup timeout. Raw inaccurate statuses have not
been relabeled. Three reserved non-discovery cases remain unused; the
discovery quota is exhausted and does not renew. No numerical result
is a premise of Sections1--7. No installation, subordinate agent,
formal verification, external publication, or finalization was performed.

The full original threshold certificate remains absent. Finite failure
of these56 discovery requests is not global nonexistence. Conditions
(A)--(E) are necessary, not sufficient, and no full scalar-region cover
has been proved. This draft supplies a concrete original-row obstruction
for future joint checking under a separately selected verifier gate.

Literature grounding: the targeted Matlas searches returned unrelated
results; the web fallback recovered the already audited composition
literature. No external theorem is used, and no new literature-based
branch was rejected. All proof work remains inside the existing full
primitive interface and elementary exact algebra.

# Near-critical ruler: a quantitative parabolic elliptic obstruction

Status: complete candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. No solver or new
fact_submit is used. Accepted facts1252b8823bb5a290 and b98bb807d514e80d
supply the interpolation convention and the benchmark definition/bound.
Every new phase, averaging, polynomial and conditioning estimate is proved
below. In particular the earlier isotropization counterexample and the
earlier elliptic interface are not invoked as unverified lemmas.

## 1. The actual assigned schedule and the entire parameter regime

For each integer m>=128 put

    s=1+sqrt(2),      c=c_m=s(1+1/m),
    a=a_m=(1-1/m)/c,  d_j=1+a c^j,
    H_0=(),          H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j),
    n=2^m-1.

All parameters remain fixed as j varies within this horizon. Let h_pre be
H_m with its last d_0 removed, so it has n-1 steps. All n queried prefix
points, including the last point x_(n-1), will be retained.

Fix arbitrary finite constants U>0 and 0<v_-<=v_+. For each m allow ANY
real u_m and v_m with |u_m|<=U and v_-<=v_m<=v_+, and set

    alpha_m=1-u_m/m,     beta_m=v_m/sqrt(m),
    z_m=alpha_m+i beta_m.

Choose ANY real invertible2x2 matrix T_m with ||T_m e1||=1. Its norm and
condition number may depend arbitrarily on m. At the actual prefix query
indices use the elliptic polynomial data

    x_i=T_m(Re P_i(z_m),Im P_i(z_m)),
    g_i=T_m(Re[z_m P_i(z_m)],Im[z_m P_i(z_m)]),
    P_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_pre,t z).

Assume that SOME values f_i make these same real jets and(0,0,0) fully
interpolable by a convex differentiable1-smooth function. For every such
interpolant F, with F(0)=min F=0, and every final step b in[0,2], we prove
the uniform estimate, for all sufficiently large m,

    2F(x_(n-1)-b g_(n-1))
      <= (2304/v_-^2)m exp[-2^(m+1)/(3m)].                (1)

The threshold in m depends only on U,v_-,v_+. Thus even with arbitrary
horizon-dependent metrics, values and free terminal completion, this
ENTIRE compact parabolic regime has payoff o(U_n^ZJ). The interval[0,2]
includes both the common comparison step1 and the original final d_0.

A necessary condition for legality, uniformly over the same regime, is

    u_m >= (1+A0)v_m^2-o(1),
    A0=sqrt(2)/(3-sqrt(2)).                              (2)

This is not an exclusion of all elliptic frequencies, an upper bound on
unrestricted W, or a resolution of the original exact-profile problem.

## 2. Complete-cycle averaging over actual early copies

For this proof abbreviate alpha=alpha_m, beta=beta_m, z=z_m and
M=T_m^T T_m. Then M is positive definite and M_11=1. Multiplication by z
has real matrix B=[[alpha,-beta],[beta,alpha]]. Define the full partial
word polynomials and copy multipliers

    p_j(z)=product_(ell<j)(1-d_ell z)^(2^(j-ell-1)),
    R_j(z)=p_j(z)(1-d_j z).

Every query position in H_j, in complex coordinates, is a product of a
subset of R_0,...,R_(j-1). Indeed the right copy in H_(j+1) multiplies
every old position by R_j, whereas the left copy leaves it unchanged.

The entire first H_(m-1) is contained in h_pre. It contains2^(m-2)
copies of the two-query word H_1=(d_0), with starting multipliers equal
to all subset products of R_1,...,R_(m-2). All these multipliers are
nonzero because z is nonreal and all d_j are real.

Consider the directed triangle star -> next query -> first query -> star
in any one of these copies. Sum the full interpolation inequalities on
that triangle, eliminating the values. Divide by the squared modulus of
its starting multiplier. Its legality becomes the same base triangle
tested with the rotated metric R_theta^T M R_theta, where theta is the
argument of the starting multiplier and R_theta is planar rotation.
Averaging all these ACTUAL cycle inequalities therefore gives legality
of the base triangle under one effective metric M_bar.

Put tau=tr(M)/2. Rotation averaging preserves its scalar part tau I. On
the traceless part, each binary copy average multiplies its norm by
|cos(arg R_j)|. To verify this directly, identify a traceless symmetric
matrix[[q,r],[r,-q]] with q+i r. Rotation conjugation multiplies it by
exp(+/-2i theta), and averaging identity and this rotation multiplies
its modulus by |(1+exp(2i theta))/2|=|cos theta|. Consequently

    M_bar=tau I+E,
    ||E||_op<=tau Gamma_m,
    Gamma_m=product_(j=1)^(m-2)|cos(arg R_j(z))|.          (3)

The bound on the initial traceless part uses only positive definiteness:
its eigenvalues have magnitude at most tau. It is uniform in conditioning.

## 3. A finite-horizon averaging estimate that includes resonances

For m>=128, the inequalities12/5<s<17/7 give
c<=2193/896<5/2 and a>=(127/128)/(5/2)=127/320>3/8;
also c>12/5. The bounds on s follow by squaring7/5 and10/7 to bound
sqrt(2). For all sufficiently large m, uniformly in our parameter range, alpha>=3/4,
alpha<=2, beta<=1/8, and |1-alpha|<=a/4. Thus

    alpha d_j-1=alpha a c^j-(1-alpha)>=a c^j/2>0.

Choose the principal factor phases

    phi_j=arg(1-d_j z)
          =-pi+arctan[beta d_j/(alpha d_j-1)],
    Phi=sum_(j>=0)2^(-j-1)phi_j=-pi+delta.

The series converges by the bounded phases and summable weights. Since
alpha<=2, alpha>=3/4 and a>=3/8, the arctangent arguments are bounded
between beta/2 and8beta. For sufficiently small beta, arctan t is between
t/2 and t for this range. Therefore

    beta/4<=delta<=8beta.                                (4)

As j grows, phi_j decreases to phi_infinity=-pi+arctan(beta/alpha).
The difference of its two arctangent arguments is exactly

    beta[d_j/(alpha d_j-1)-1/alpha]
      =beta/[alpha(alpha d_j-1)]<=8beta c^(-j).

Since arctan is1-Lipschitz,
0<=phi_j-phi_infinity<=8beta c^(-j). Direct summation of polynomial
multiplicities gives an unwrapped phase identity

    arg R_j=2^j Phi+epsilon_j,
    epsilon_j=phi_j-sum_(ell>=0)2^(-ell-1)phi_(j+ell),
    0<=epsilon_j<=8beta c^(-j).                          (5)

Choose k so that2^k delta lies in[pi/4,pi/2). For large m this integer
satisfies1<=k<=m-2 and2^k<=8/beta, by(4). Discarding earlier cosine
factors only increases the product. Because2^j Phi differs from2^j delta
by an integer multiple of pi, the1-Lipschitz property of |cos| and a
telescoping difference of products of numbers in[0,1] give

    Gamma_m
      <=product_(j=k)^(m-2)|cos(2^j delta)|
                          +sum_(j=k)^(m-2)epsilon_j
      <=[1/(2^(m-1-k)|sin(2^k delta)|)]
                          +8beta c^(-k)/(1-c^(-1)).      (6)

The product identity in the last line follows by iterating
sin(2t)=2sin(t)cos(t); its denominator is nonzero because
2^k delta is in[pi/4,pi/2). This calculation works unchanged at exact
dyadic resonance, where the numerator may vanish.

Let r0=log_2(12/5)>0. Since2^(-k)<=4delta/pi<=11beta, c<=5/2 and
c>=12/5, the bounds above imply, for an absolute constant C,

    Gamma_m<=C[2^(-m)/beta+beta^(1+r0)].                  (7)

For example c^(-k)=(2^(-k))^(log_2 c)<=C beta^r0, since beta<1 and
log_2 c lies in a fixed compact interval. Because beta is bounded above
and below by positive constants times m^(-1/2), (7) yields

    Gamma_m/beta ->0                                    (8)

uniformly in all the stated parameters. This is a FINITE-HORIZON bound
in this specific regime, not the false assertion that every fixed nonreal
parameter has a vanishing infinite tail product.

## 4. The averaged triangle forces the parabolic legality constant

Write d=d_0, e1=(1,0), v0=B e1, r1=e1-d v0 and v1=B r1. The base
triangle weight under a metric M is tr(M Z), with the symmetric matrix

    Z=(v1 v1^T)/2+d sym(v0 v1^T)
          +(v0-v1)(v0-v1)^T/2-sym(e1 v0^T)+(v0 v0^T)/2,
    sym(C)=(C+C^T)/2.                                  (9)

This follows by writing its three edge weights: ||v1||_M^2/2,
d<v1,v0>_M+||v0-v1||_M^2/2, and-<v0,e1>_M+||v0||_M^2/2.
For M=I, direct expansion gives

    tr(Z)=d beta^2-(alpha-|z|^2)(1-d alpha+d^2|z|^2).    (10)

We also need an error bound uniform in M. At z=1, Z=0 for every d.
When |z|<=2 and1<=d<=2, put w=z-1. Then
||v0-e1||<=|w|, ||r1-(1-d)e1||<=2|w|, and
||v1-(1-d)e1||<=7|w|; also||v0||<=2, ||v1||<=10.
Applying ||aa^T-bb^T||_op<=(||a||+||b||)||a-b|| to(9), and expanding
its mixed product, bounds its five differences from z=1 by respectively
38.5|w|,34|w|,56|w|,|w| and1.5|w|. Thus

    ||Z||_op<=132|w|<=264beta                            (11)

for all sufficiently large m, since |alpha-1|=O(m^(-1))=o(beta).

The averaged legal triangle from Section2 satisfies tr(M_bar Z)<=0.
Using(3),(11), and |tr(E Z)|<=2||E||_op||Z||_op, gives

    d beta^2-(alpha-|z|^2)(1-d alpha+d^2|z|^2)
                  <=528 Gamma_m beta=o(beta^2).         (12)

Here tau>0 cancels; no condition-number loss occurs. Now d tends to
sqrt(2), alpha tends to1, and |z|^2 tends to1, so the denominator
1-d alpha+d^2|z|^2 tends to3-sqrt(2)>0. Moreover

    alpha-|z|^2=(u_m-v_m^2)/m-u_m^2/m^2.

Divide(12) by beta^2=v_m^2/m. This proves precisely the uniform
necessary condition(2).

## 5. The full polynomial potential, with a uniform remainder

Put w=z-1, t_j=d_j/(d_j-1)=1+1/(a c^j), and N=2^m-1. For m>=128,
1<=t_j<4. Factor each complex magnitude exactly as

    |1-d_j z|=a c^j |1+t_j w|.

The level multiplicities satisfy sum_j2^(m-j-1)=N and
sum_j j2^(m-j-1)=N-m. Define

    L_m(w)=log(ac)+Re sum_(j>=0)2^(-j-1)log(1+t_j w),

where the logarithms use their convergent power series near1. For
|w|<=1/8, their cubic remainders are at most128|w|^3: bound the terms
of degree>=3 by2|t_j w|^3. Summing the linear and quadratic terms yields

    L_m(w)=log(1-1/m)+D_m Re(w)-(J_m/2)Re(w^2)+O(|w|^3),
    D_m=1+1/[a(2-c^(-1))],
    J_m=1+2/[a(2-c^(-1))]+1/[a^2(2-c^(-2))].            (13)

All remainder constants are uniform over the compact parameter regime.
Because w=-u_m/m+i v_m/sqrt(m),

    L_m(w)=[-1-u_m D_m+(v_m^2/2)J_m]/m+O(m^(-3/2)).     (14)

At the limits c=s, a=1/s, use s^2=2s+1 to obtain

    D=lim D_m=2+2/(2s-1)>5/2,
    J=lim J_m=6+4/(2s-1)+2/(4s+1)<15/2.                (15)

For the inequalities,12/5<s<5/2 implies2s-1<4 and
4/(2s-1)+2/(4s+1)<20/19+10/53<3/2. Also

    1+A0=3/(4-s)>15/8.

By(2),(15), the coefficient in(14) is at most

    -1-[(1+A0)D-J/2]v_m^2+o(1)
          <=-1-(15/16)v_m^2+o(1).

In particular legality forces L_m(w)<=-1/(2m) for sufficiently large m.

The exact finite product differs from its infinite weighted sum only by
a controlled tail:

    log|p_m(z)|
      =2^m L_m(w)-log(ac)-m log c
             -2^m Re sum_(j>=m)2^(-j-1)log(1+t_j w).

The absolute value of the last term is at most8|w|, since
|log(1+t_j w)|<=2t_j|w|<=8|w|. Therefore, uniformly over all legal
parameters under consideration,

    log|p_m(z)|<=-2^m/(3m)                              (16)

for all sufficiently large m. This includes zeros without a division;
in fact nonreal z makes every factor nonzero here.

## 6. Conditioning, the deleted last step, and free completion

The two initial optimum interpolation inequalities give
<x0,g0>>=||g0||^2. Since ||x0||=1, this implies ||g0||<=1. But

    g0=alpha T_m e1+beta T_m e2,

so ||T_m e2||<=(1+|alpha|)/beta<=3/beta. Hence

    ||T_m||_op<=1+3/beta<=4/beta.                       (17)

This bounds the actual two-vector realization despite arbitrary initial
conditioning. Let p_pre(z)=p_m(z)/(1-d_0 z), the polynomial of the
actual queried prefix. From Section3,
|1-d_0 z|>=alpha d_0-1>=a/2>=3/16, so

    |p_pre(z)|<=6|p_m(z)|,
    ||x_(n-1)||<=4|p_pre(z)|/beta,
    ||g_(n-1)||<=8|p_pre(z)|/beta.                      (18)

No condition at a later gradient is used. For any convex1-smooth
interpolant of these queried data, optimum interpolation gives
f_(n-1)<=<x_(n-1),g_(n-1)>-||g_(n-1)||^2/2. Smoothness along a final
step b in[0,2] gives F(x-bg)<=f_(n-1)-b(1-b/2)||g||^2<=f_(n-1).
Equations(16)--(18) now imply

    2F(x-bg)<=2||x||||g||
       <=2304|p_m(z)|^2/beta^2
       <=(2304/v_-^2)m exp[-2^(m+1)/(3m)],

proving(1) for EVERY completion and every final b in the stated interval.

## 7. Exact benchmark comparison and remaining scope

Acceptedb98bb807d514e80d gives B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3). Since n<2^m,
the ratio of(1) to U_n^ZJ=1/B_n is at most

    (2304/v_-^2)m(1+4*3^m) exp[-2^(m+1)/(3m)] ->0.

The exponential dominates because2^m/m grows faster than any constant
multiple of m, which follows for example by successive doubling after
a fixed sufficiently large index. This uses the actual accepted B_n
bound, not a guessed dyadic benchmark constant.

The obstruction covers all horizon-dependent elliptic metrics in the
entire compact regime alpha=1-O(1/m), beta comparable to m^(-1/2).
It specifically closes the natural near-unit polynomial-growth attempt:
the same cycles that allow a free terminal completion force damping of
the chronological polynomial before that completion. Other imaginary
scales, other real parts, multiple modes and changing generators are not
covered. The arbitrary-schedule exact-profile target is unchanged.

Matlas retrieval returned unrelated statements in arXiv:2002.05991 and
2009.11657; none is used. All calculations are symbolic, with no numerical
search, isotropization assumption, or additional verifier allocation.
