# Correlated upper-rank inverse folds and the exact price of the inactive group

**JOINT UNVERIFIED DRAFT.** Worker max2, project gd-exact-profile-20260926,
one allocated invocation after ended68, ceiling69/attempted70. No formal
submission is authorized or performed. No assertion below is a new fact.
All new construction, algebra, dynamics, and comparisons are derived jointly.

Two finite, correlated, all-history laws are analyzed. They use one static
centered-simplex objective, exact coordinate counts, and an initial point fixed
from the entire word before the forward run. They are not Cartesian copies of
both inverse branches over every future level.

* **Law I:** select the two largest future coordinate levels when their gap
  permits the two-coordinate inverse; otherwise select the largest alone.
  Arbitrary N>=3, arbitrary scale, and arbitrary allowed inactive-coordinate
  variations are included. The two upper coordinate identities persist, but
  their actual positive support can leave and re-enter the edge. On EVERY
  SAME-m H_(m,1/4), m>=1536, its whole domain satisfies

      E <= 1/(2S-2),       B_n E < 19/20.                       (A)

  The global Huber score 1/(1+2S) is attained within the family. Thus its
  possible gain over that member is at most the relative factor
  (1+2S)/(2S-2)=1+3/(2S-2). This does not prove E<=K_n or E<=F_ray_n.

* **Law II:** replace each upper coordinate by r identical coordinates and
  retain q>=1 inactive coordinates, N=2r+q. Every count is exact and finite.
  Its transverse/carrier energy ratio is kappa=N/q, which is unbounded.
  The same argument gives a precise deficit when kappa is not too large,
  but a uniform extension of (A) would be unjustified. Over all finite r,q,
  the exact supremum of this law is the worst-case profile of ONE scalar
  symmetric Huber function over its initial point. This is a reduction, not
  an evaluation of that scalar profile, and not an attained improvement.

In particular, the original all-word exact-profile target remains OPEN.
There is no claim about arbitrary simplex starts or arbitrary completions of
these prefixes. Only the static function actually constructed here is used.

## 1. Target, benchmark, and accepted scopes

For a finite nonnegative word h of length n and L>0, the original W_n(h) is
the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over finite-dimensional
convex differentiable functions with L-Lipschitz gradient and an attained
minimum, with x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)grad f(x_t). The exact-profile question
retains every n and every nonnegative word, including zero and very large
steps. A result about this one diagnostic family is not that theorem.
Normalization here is W=2R.

The original all-integer-split denominators are

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,
    Gbench(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) Gbench(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.                                             (1)

These are definitions, not executed recurrences. Accepted facts used at
their exact scopes are:

* 1252b8823bb5a290: the full ordered interpolation convention and necessity;
  no free-terminal optimization from it is used to change this objective.
* b98bb807d514e80d: K_n includes the complete-word Huber member 1/(1+2S).
* 1fca3e5dd555cb4a: K_n<=F_ray_n. No converse or scalar-family completeness
  statement is contained in that fact or asserted here.
* 5fd396eba75651e7, Section10, equation(77), ONLY its stated benchmark bound
  B_(2^m-1)/(1+2S_m)<237/250 on H_(m,1/4), m>=1536.

The older f7f9930b98aa7caa, its Cartesian-width obstruction, and its first-leaf
counterexample were read for scope. None of their unverified mathematics is
imported as a premise. The general inverse identity is rederived below.

Fix the diagnostic's SAME enclosing m throughout every child:

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j, S=S_m.                       (2)

Here all h_t>1. Counting entries of each level gives

    S_j+1=(1-v)2^j+v c^j, v=beta/[c(c-2)]=a/(c-2).            (3)

For m>=1536, 1/3<a<1/2, 2<c<5/2, beta>999/1000, and 0<v<1.
Indeed c>lambda and lambda(lambda-2)=1 give v<1. The elementary bounds
lambda>12/5, lambda<121/50, beta>999/1000 imply the displayed coarse
estimates. Since c-2<1/2, v>2a, and consequently

    a*c^j < S_j+1  for every j>=0.                            (4)

Also S>n>=m>=1536. No small-horizon or reset-parameter substitute is used.

## 2. One finite real dictionary and its global smooth objective

For any integer N>=3 let

    E_N={x in R^N:sum_j x_j=0}, u_N=(1/N,...,1/N), sigma>0,
    v_j=sigma(e_j-u_N), D=conv{v_j:1<=j<=N},
    F(x)=max_(g in D) {<x,g>-||g||^2/2}, x in E_N.            (5)

All offsets are zero. The slopes are real, fixed, and have Gram

    <v_i,v_j>=sigma^2(1_(i=j)-1/N).                           (6)

This is an ordinary Euclidean space of dimension N-1. If literal coordinates
in R^(N-1) are desired, use the orthonormal columns
b_k=(e_1+...+e_k-k e_(k+1))/sqrt(k(k+1)), 1<=k<N, and their transpose.
No extra carrier coordinate or variable metric is added.

The maximizer is the unique nearest-point projection g=P_D(x). Its defining
normal inequalities are <x-g,v_j-g><=0 for every j. At two inputs, adding
the normal inequalities proves <x-x',g-g'> >= ||g-g'||^2, hence g is
1-Lipschitz. Evaluating (5) at the two maximizers bounds the first-order
remainder between zero and ||x-x'||^2. Thus F is finite everywhere, convex,
differentiable, and grad F=P_D. Near zero the projection is x, because zero
is interior to D in E_N. The least gradient Lipschitz constant is exactly1.

Its COMPLETE minimum set is {0}, with value0. For nonzero x in E_N, some
x_j>0, and a sufficiently small positive multiple of v_j lies in D and
gives a positive expression in (5). Consequently the OWN closest radius is
exactly ||x_0||, including all inactive-coordinate variation.

Write x=(sigma/N)w, sum w_j=0. Let theta be the unique threshold satisfying

    (1/N)sum_j(1+w_j-theta)_+=1,
    P_j=(1+w_j-theta)_+.

Then

    g_j=(sigma/N)(P_j-1),
    y_j=(x-g)_j=(sigma/N)min(1+w_j,theta),
    delta=<y,g>=sigma^2 theta/N,
    2F(x)=(sigma^2/N)[2theta+(1/N)sum_j(P_j-1)^2].             (7)

To verify this directly, sum P_j=N implies sum min(1+w_j,theta)=0.
Every positive P_j has residual sigma*theta/N and every zero P_j has
residual no larger; this proves the normal inequality against ALL vertices.
It proves (7) without importing a projection theorem. The threshold is
nonnegative because the positive-part average at theta0 is at least1.

For ANY two actual samples i,j, including the terminal and the optimum,
expansion of f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 gives

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<y_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                                  (8)

This proves every ordered remote, reversed, terminal, and optimum row on
the same static function. Repeated samples require no deletion. In
particular Q_(i,star)=0 and Q_(star,i)=-delta_i<=0.

## 3. General correlated inverse identity, jointly rederived

For h=1+d>1, the forward rule in w coordinates is

    w'_j=w_j+h-h(1+w_j-theta)_+
        =w_j+h             on an inactive coordinate,
        =-d w_j+h theta    on an active coordinate.           (9)

Given a centered future list w', choose a proper nonempty set A and put
p=|A|/N and mu=(1/N)sum_(j in A)w'_j. Solving (9) coordinatewise and
requiring mean zero yields

    theta=[mu+d(1-p)]/p,
    w_j=w'_j-h                     if j outside A,
    w_j=(h theta-w'_j)/d            if j in A.                (10)

For example, the mean of the candidates multiplied by d is
-h*mu-dh(1-p)+ph*theta, which vanishes exactly for this theta.
If theta>0 and max_j w'_j<theta+d, then on A
1+w_j-theta=(theta+d-w'_j)/d>0, while outside A it equals
w'_j-(theta+d)<0. Their average positive part is
[p(theta+d)-mu]/d=1. Thus (10) really is the positive-height inverse
with the prescribed exact support, not just a formal preimage.

The following two laws give concrete sets satisfying these guards at
EVERY original index. Both branches are never independently duplicated.

## 4. Both complete laws, with exact multiplicities and terminal data

Partition the coordinates into G_+, G_-, and I with

    |G_+|=|G_-|=r>=1, |I|=q>=1, N=2r+q.

Law I has r=1 and arbitrary q=N-2. Law II allows all positive integers
r,q. Every coordinate in either upper group has the same value; coordinates
in I may have unequal values. Define the fixed face averages and axes

    c_+=sigma(1_(G_+)/r-u_N), c_-=sigma(1_(G_-)/r-u_N),
    c_face=(c_++c_-)/2, alpha=||c_face||=sigma sqrt(q/(2rN)),
    b=sigma/sqrt(2r), e=c_face/alpha,
    u=(1_(G_+)-1_(G_-))/sqrt(2r),
    kappa=b^2/alpha^2=N/q.                                   (11)

The vectors e,u are orthonormal. The vector called a carrier here is the
actual barycenter c_face INSIDE E_N; it is not an added geometric direction.
Let Z be zero on both upper groups, supported on I, with sum_(j in I)Z_j=0.
It is orthogonal to e,u. Choose any terminal scalar t in R, any

    R>max{1,|t|},
    max_(j in I) Z_j < [sigma/(2r)](R-max{1,|t|}).            (12)

The terminal point, fixed before the run, is

    x_n=alpha R e+b t u+Z.                                  (13)

The Z=0 choice is always allowed. Its exact terminal projection is

    g_n=alpha e+b clip(t)u, clip(s)=max(-1,min(1,s)).          (14)

To check (14), for |t|<1 the residual is constant on both upper groups,
and comparison with an inactive residual is precisely
Z_j<sigma(R-1)/(2r). For |t|>=1 the projection is c_+ or c_-;
the inactive residual condition is Z_j<=sigma(R+|t|-2)/(2r), which follows
from the stronger ordering bound Z_j<sigma(R-|t|)/(2r). At |t|=1 the
same formulas agree. Thus all inactive coordinates are strictly lower,
and the height is positive. These are tests against every original vertex.

Now fix the whole backward scalar dictionary, with no forward oracle:

    z_n=t, R_n=R,
    R_i=R_(i+1)+h_i,
    z_i=-z_(i+1)/d_i               if |z_(i+1)|<d_i,
    z_i=z_(i+1)+h_i sign(z_(i+1))  if |z_(i+1)|>=d_i,
    d_i=h_i-1.                                               (15)

The second case cannot have z_(i+1)=0. Its branch set A_i is both upper
groups in the first case, and the group of the sign of z_(i+1) in the
second. Fractions are exactly 2r/N or r/N. For Law I this is exactly the
top-two-coordinate rule with a singleton fallback: the two-level gap in
w' is (N/r)|z_(i+1)|, so the strict two-group guard is |z_(i+1)|<d_i.

All z_i, branch sets, thresholds, initial coordinates, and multiplicities
are finite expressions determined from the full known word. Equation(15)
is a mathematical definition, never an executed recurrence. If desired,
write z_i=A_i^sc z_(i+1)+B_i^sc with A_i^sc=-1/d_i,B_i^sc=0 in the first
case, and A_i^sc=1,B_i^sc=h_i sign(z_(i+1)) in the second. Then

    z_0=t product_(i<n) A_i^sc
        +sum_(i<n) B_i^sc product_(j<i) A_j^sc.               (16)

This is not an average over incompatible itineraries. Once the finite
dictionary is fixed, start the actual function (5) at

    x_0=alpha(S+R)e+b z_0 u+Z.                              (17)

Equivalently its coordinate levels are

    w_(0,j)=q(S+R)/(2r) + N z_0/(2r), j in G_+,
    w_(0,j)=q(S+R)/(2r) - N z_0/(2r), j in G_-,
    w_(0,j)=-(S+R)+(N/sigma)Z_j, j in I.                    (18)

Their sum is zero. This specifies the full real initial vector and every
coordinate multiplicity, even when r or q is enormous.

## 5. Entire actual run, thresholds, positive heights, and Gram

At every original i define x_i=alpha R_i e+b z_i u+Z. The terminal
inequality(12) persists backward. In a two-group inverse, |z_i|<1 and
R_i=R_(i+1)+h_i, so R_i-max(1,|z_i|) is strictly larger than the previous
lower bound. In a one-group inverse, |z_i|=|z_(i+1)|+h_i>1, so
R_i-|z_i|=R_(i+1)-|z_(i+1)| >= R_(i+1)-max(1,|z_(i+1)|).
Thus the same Z satisfies every original support and ordering condition.

The actual gradient and value at every query, terminal included, are

    g_i=alpha e+b clip(z_i)u,
    F(x_i)=alpha^2(R_i-1/2)+b^2 Hub(z_i),
    Hub(s)=s^2/2 if |s|<=1, and |s|-1/2 otherwise,
    delta_i=alpha^2(R_i-1)+b^2(|z_i|-1)_+>0.                 (19)

One can also see the strict correlated guards directly from (10). In the
two-group case, with R'=R_(i+1), z'=z_(i+1),

    theta_i=q(R'+d_i)/(2r),
    max w'=[qR'+N|z'|]/(2r),
    theta_i+d_i=[qR'+N d_i]/(2r).

In the one-group case,

    theta_i=[qR'+N|z'|+2d_i(N-r)]/(2r),
    theta_i+d_i=max w'+N d_i/r.

All theta_i>0, and the guards are strict. The first choice's guard is
exactly the first condition in (15). Therefore (10) yields every displayed
x_i and support. Alternatively, (19) gives the actual update

    R_(i+1)=R_i-h_i,
    z_(i+1)=z_i-h_i clip(z_i), Z_(i+1)=Z_i,                  (20)

which is verified by both cases of (15). Induction proves the WHOLE
forward run on the unchanged function and every physical step. All large
bridges and leaves in (2) remain present.

For c_i=clip(z_i), the full Gram of x_0 and every queried/terminal gradient
is given explicitly by

    <g_i,g_j>=alpha^2+b^2 c_i c_j,
    <x_0,g_i>=alpha^2(S+R)+b^2 z_0 c_i,
    ||x_0||^2=alpha^2(S+R)^2+b^2 z_0^2+||Z||^2.              (21)

It is the actual Euclidean Gram, hence positive semidefinite. Together
with (8), this proves every directed interpolation and optimum row; no
independent PSD surrogate or expected support was substituted.

## 6. Explicit support reentry on the ENTIRE prescribed word

This is a parameter specialization of either law, not a third law. Take
terminal t=a/2, R=2, Z=0, with any allowed r,q. Apply definition(15) to
the entire H_m, retaining all earlier indices. Its last five query scalars
can be written symbolically without replaying the word:

    z_(n-1)=-1/2,
    z_(n-2)=1/(2beta),
    z_(n-3)=J:=1+a+1/(2beta),
    z_(n-4)=-J/(beta c),
    z_(n-5)=-J/(beta c)-(1+a).                              (22)

The excesses at these queries, in backward order, are a,beta,a,beta*c,a.
The first two choices are two-group choices since a/2<a and 1/2<beta.
The next is one-group since 1/(2beta)>1/2>a. Next J<beta*c:
J<3/2+500/999<21/10, while beta*c>(999/1000)(12/5)>23/10.
Finally J/(beta*c)>a because a beta c=beta^2<1<J.

Consequently the actual queried supports, chronologically, are

    G_-, G_+ union G_-, G_+, G_+ union G_-, G_+ union G_-.

The minus group leaves and then re-enters; every height is strictly
positive by (19). At the two-group queries z_i is nonzero and |z_i|<1,
so the projected coordinates on G_+, G_-, and I have three distinct
levels. This is not a two-level initial-gradient assertion or a formal
face expectation. Earlier queries are exactly the full finite law(15).

For this specialization the whole-word attained formula is

    E=[3+kappa*a^2/4]/[(S+2)^2+kappa*z_0^2].                 (23)

The z_0 in (23) is the full-history quantity(16), not the five-step prefix
or suffix. Formula(23) supplies no useful gain claim.

## 7. Exact own radius and terminal formula across the whole history

Since the complete minimum is {0}, put zeta=||Z||^2/alpha^2. The exact
radius and payoff of each finite instance are

    rho^2=alpha^2[(S+R)^2+kappa*z_0^2+zeta],
    Phi(t)=2Hub(t),
    E(F,h)=[2R-1+kappa*Phi(t)]/
                  [(S+R)^2+kappa*z_0^2+zeta].                (24)

In the original w normalization the complete initial variance is

    (1/N)sum_j w_(0,j)^2
      =q/(2r)(S+R)^2+N/(2r)z_0^2+(N/sigma^2)||Z||^2.       (25)

This is an equality, not a first-fold estimate. It retains rare-coordinate
normal displacement, arbitrary finite dimension and every backward fold.
No terminal function completion is used. We have not transferred this
radius to a different interpolating function or claimed a bound for all
completions of the same prefix.

For L>0 replace F by L*F and use steps h_i/L. The entire run is unchanged,
and (24) is the same normalized W=2R payoff.

## 8. A full-hierarchy scalar interval lemma, proved within this draft

Let T_h(s)=s-h clip(s). Write Psi_j for its chronological composition
over EVERY entry of H_j, and M_j=1+S_j. The following precise assertions
hold for the SAME enclosing m:

    |s|<=M_j => |Psi_j(s)|<=1,
    |s|>=M_j => Psi_j(s)=sign(s)(|s|-S_j).                  (26)

At equality the formulas agree. The exterior assertion follows directly:
every partial physical mass is at most S_j, so all intermediate points
retain their sign and have magnitude at least1.

For the interior assertion use induction, starting with H_0 empty and
M_0=1. Put b_j=1+d, d=a*c^j<M_j by (4), and consider
H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), whose M_(j+1)=2M_j+d. All maps are odd, so
take s>=0. If s<=M_j, the first child outputs magnitude at most1;
the bridge then has magnitude at most d<M_j. The second child outputs
magnitude at most1. If M_j<s<=M_(j+1), the first child gives y=s-S_j>1.
The bridge gives y-b_j, which lies between -d and M_j. The second child
again outputs magnitude at most1. This proves (26) for every j.

The lemma is only an interval assertion. It does NOT say
|Psi_m(s)|<=|s|/sqrt(1+2S) inside that interval, and does not evaluate
the optimized scalar Huber profile. Replacing it by that stronger
pointwise claim would be a new, unpaid theorem.

## 9. Whole Law I deficit, and the exact count-dependent screen

All statements in this section concern the ENTIRE parameter domains in
Section4, on every m>=1536, not a finite-prefix improvement.

First suppose |z_0|>S+1. By (26) the scalar trajectory stays saturated
with one sign. The whole gradient is the same c_+ or c_- at every query
and at the terminal. Call it g. From (19),

    2F(x_n)=2<x_0,g>-(1+2S)||g||^2
            <=||x_0||^2/(1+2S),                            (27)

because ||x_0-(1+2S)g||^2>=0. This already pays every component of x_0.

Otherwise |z_0|<=S+1 and (26) gives |t|<=1. Equation(24) yields

    E <= [2R-1+kappa]/(S+R)^2.                              (28)

If D_count:=2S+1-kappa>0, put U=S+R>0. The identity
U^2-D_count(2U-D_count)=(U-D_count)^2>=0 proves

    E <= 1/(2S+1-kappa).                                   (29)

Together (27)--(29) give the same upper bound on the entire domain when
D_count>0, since kappa>0. In Law I, kappa=N/(N-2)<=3. Thus

    E <=1/(2S-2).                                          (30)

By accepted5fd396eba75651e7 at exactly equation(77),

    B_n E < (237/250)(1+2S)/(2S-2) < 19/20.                (31)

For the last strict inequality, (237/250)*(475/474)=19/20, and
(1+2S)/(2S-2)=1+3/(2S-2)<475/474 whenever S>712; here S>1536.

The Huber member is attained by t=0, R=S+1, Z=0, any r,q. Then z_i=0
at every index, and (24) gives exactly1/(1+2S). Therefore for Law I

    1/(1+2S) <= sup E <=1/(2S-2),
    sup E / H_base <=1+3/(2S-2).                            (32)

All new claims in (26)--(32) are unverified. If correct, they exclude a
nonvanishing useful gain of Law I on the required benchmark. They do not
prove its exact optimum is Huber and do not exclude a tiny finite gain
over K_n or F_ray_n. Since those channels are at least H_base, any gain
over either is also at most the relative error in (32).

For Law II the exact sufficient screen is

    kappa <= (1+2S)/475 => B_n E <19/20.                    (33)

More generally, whenever 0<kappa<1+2S,

    B_n E < (237/250)/[1-kappa/(1+2S)].                    (34)

The dimension/cardinality condition is essential: kappa=N/q. There is no
uniform <=3 bound after copying the two active groups. Taking q=1 and
arbitrary integer r gives kappa=2r+1. These are exact finite coordinates,
not negligible-probability formal states. Equation(25) still charges their
real radius. A small inactive fraction can defeat this screen, which is
not evidence that it produces a useful terminal score.

## 10. What the thin-inactive-group law actually reduces to

For any fixed word h with all h_i>1 define the scalar symmetric Huber
profile, on the real line and with its own minimum0, by

    V_Hub(h)=sup_(s!=0) Phi(Psi_h(s))/s^2,                   (35)

where Psi_h is the actual chronological composition of T_(h_i). This is
one fixed convex C1 1-smooth function Hub, varying only its initial point.
The elementary constant-slope example s=1+2S gives V_Hub>=H_base.
No accepted fact has identified V_Hub with K_n or the alternating-ray
channel; no such identification is assumed here.

For an instance of either law with z_0!=0, remove the nonnegative zeta
in the denominator of (24) to obtain the weighted-average bound

    E <= [ (S+R)^2 E_car(R) + kappa*z_0^2 E_sc ] /
                       [ (S+R)^2 + kappa*z_0^2 ],
    E_car(R)=(2R-1)/(S+R)^2 <=1/(1+2S),
    E_sc=Phi(t)/z_0^2 <=V_Hub(h).                            (36)

The carrier bound follows from
(S+R)^2-(1+2S)(2R-1)=(R-S-1)^2. If z_0=0, then t=0 and the
same upper bound follows directly. Consequently every Law II instance
satisfies E<=V_Hub(h), for every word in its h_i>1 domain.

The converse SUPREMUM relation needs attention to inverse branches and
strict guards. For one scalar step h=1+d, the smallest possible magnitude
of an input with output magnitude u>=0 is

    rho_d(u)=u/d if 0<=u<=d, and u+h if u>d.                 (37)

To prove this, inputs of magnitude at most1 give output magnitude d times
their magnitude. For inputs of magnitude r>=1, the output magnitude is
|r-h|. When u<=d the outer inverse h-u is at least1 and the core inverse
u/d is at most1. When u>d, the only admissible outer inverse is h+u.
All alternatives are included; oddness removes the sign ambiguity.

The function rho_d is increasing and left-continuous. Therefore composing
(37) backward minimizes the initial magnitude among ALL scalar trajectories
having a given terminal magnitude. Indeed a larger intermediate magnitude
cannot lower the earlier minimum, by monotonicity at each preceding step.
Denote the resulting finite composition by r_h(u).

Our strict inverse (15) agrees with these minimal magnitudes except when an
intermediate u equals d. Those equality choices are not silently admitted:
(15) uses its outer branch there so that every declared support is strict.
However (37) and its finite composition are left-continuous and strictly
increasing. For any terminal u>0, choose positive u_l increasing to u and
avoiding the finitely many threshold preimages of this finite piecewise
affine composition. The strict construction then has

    |z_0(u_l)|=r_h(u_l) ->r_h(u), Phi(u_l)->Phi(u).            (38)

Only finite real instances are used at each l. No trajectory limit is
offered as an attained witness. Thus the supremum of E_sc over the strict
law is exactly V_Hub(h): replacing any scalar prehistory by the minimal
one cannot reduce Phi(u)/initial^2, and (38) approximates boundary cases.

Now choose such a strict scalar history, any fixed R>max(1,u), and Z=0.
With q=1 and r arbitrarily large, kappa=2r+1 tends to infinity and (24)
tends to its E_sc. Together with (36), this proves, as one joint draft,

    sup_(ALL finite Law II instances) E(F,h)=V_Hub(h).       (39)

This is an equality of suprema. It does not assert finite attainment when
the carrier term is strictly below E_sc. The required finite-real control
is explicit: if E_sc>eta>E_car(R), any allowed finite kappa satisfying

    kappa > (S+R)^2[eta-E_car(R)] /
                         [z_0^2(E_sc-eta)]                 (40)

gives E>eta. This still pays the actual radius(24). Formula(40) is
conditional on a scalar history with that score; none beating the target
is supplied in this invocation.

## 11. Remaining scalar interface and two unsuccessful direct attempts

For the hierarchy, (26) gives an exact division of (35):

    V_Hub(H_m)=max{1/(1+2S), L_m^2},
    L_m=sup_(0<|s|<=S+1) |Psi_m(s)|/|s|.                   (41)

The exterior supremum is 1/(1+2S), attained at |s|=1+2S, by direct
optimization of [2(|s|-S)-1]/s^2. On the interior the terminal is in
[-1,1], so its value is the square appearing in (41). Thus Law II's
remaining issue is a concrete scalar amplitude profile, not a free
multidimensional covariance or an uncharged coordinate multiplicity.

Direct attempt 1 was to use interval absorption(26) alone to bound (41).
It gives |Psi_m(s)|<=1 but cannot bound the ratio for small or intermediate
|s| with the required 1/sqrt(B_n) constant. This is precisely the term
which survives when kappa grows. Treating the bound1 as if it were a
Lipschitz constant of order1/sqrt(S) would be invalid.

Direct attempt 2 was to propagate the interior ratio through the two
identical children using independent child suprema. This gives only a
sufficient bound and may combine incompatible maximizing inputs. The
actual second child receives T_(1+a*c^j)(Psi_j(s)); it cannot choose its
own maximizing argument. Conversely, the exact inverse (37) is a finite
description but its chronological, threshold-conditioned extremum has
not been evaluated here. Neither restating that composition nor assuming
its critical inputs align establishes an attained gain or a deficit.

The construct-counterexamples audit refutes the tempting extension of
the Law I carrier bound: q=1,r>1 gives kappa>3, and r can grow with S.
All strict guards and the finite real radius remain valid. This is a
counterexample to that proposed proof step, not a counterexample to the
minimax conjecture or to V_Hub<=K_n. No counterexample to the latter
inequality was found or asserted.

To obtain a useful Law II result one still must either produce an exact
scalar history with quantitative Phi(Psi_m(s))/s^2 beyond the COMPLETE
K/ray channel and toward1/B_n, or prove the relevant uniform upper bound
for (41). To leave the scalar reduction, a genuinely new correlated rule
must change the persistent upper-group identities, split their equality
classes, use unequal group dynamics, or otherwise break the single fixed
face's affine-plus-Huber decomposition. These are interfaces, not extra
laws attempted in this invocation. Arbitrary correlated simplex starts
and the separate every-n/every-h target remain open.

## 12. Literature, verification status, and resource accounting

The query-memory, direct-proving, search-math-results, and
construct-counterexamples procedures informed the work. Search was a
mature-subproblem mutation: replace the Cartesian preimage duplication by
exact rank-correlated support selection and understand its whole radius.

Matlas was called for simplex threshold/inverse overrelaxed projection
results. It returned arXiv2211.05205 Corollary5.1, 1810.11640 Definition3,
and 2401.14871 Definition1. None supplies the all-history estimate and none
is a proof dependency. Web retrieval recovered the exact already-local
primary paper by Weiran Wang and Miguel A. Carreira-Perpinan,
*Projection onto the probability simplex: An efficient algorithm with a
simple proof, and an application*, arXiv1309.1541 (2013), Section3/Theorem1.
Its local extracted KKT proof was read at
downloads/1309.1541_public_pdf_text.txt. The ordinary simplex is nonnegative
vectors of sum1; here its input is (1+w)/N and its threshold is theta/N.
The necessary projection and normal facts were independently derived in
Section2. No external theorem is used as an unproved black box.
Primary reference: https://arxiv.org/abs/1309.1541

The sole accepted correctness inputs are the specifically scoped facts in
Section1. Everything new, including (A), (33), and (39), is UNVERIFIED.
The active assignment closes the formal ledger, so there is no fact_submit
and no new fact_id. These results cannot yet be used as fact-graph bricks.

Resource counts: mathematical programs0; numerical evaluations0;
arithmetic/trajectory/recurrence replays0; optimizers/solvers/DP0;
formal calls/fact_submit0; installations0; consults0; subagents0;
outward publication/finalization/revocation/shutdown0. Only text retrieval,
symbolic reasoning, literature reading, own-file writes, shared-memory
publication, and text-integrity checks were used.

Some early broad read batches were truncated. The previous complete draft,
the required accepted comparison scopes, and the exact primary projection
proof were subsequently read in bounded calls. No mathematical conclusion
was drawn from an empty or truncated result. No service/verifier action was
taken. The one allowed invocation is not automatically renewed; the actual
post-return controller counter or process exit is not observable here.

The original target is OPEN. The useful new scope is the full Law I
benchmark deficit together with the paid, exact Law II scalar reduction;
neither is a solution or a verified partial-result Goal completion.

# Concrete asymmetric eight-step candidate and the required hostile checks

Status: UNVERIFIED SCHEDULE CANDIDATE ONLY. There is no universal upper bound.
The following is a symbolic construction, not a reopened numerical screen.

The equal balanced three-block obstruction was extended in candidate
eedba6be86f2ff0a. The actual shortened-pair matrix in4339b7cebe8d6905 suggests
changing only the final pair, so that a nonprimitive intermediate tail could
be repaired by retained cross-block information.

## 1. Exact physical schedule

Set

    u=10/7, C=1+2u=27/7, Pi=(1-u)^2=9/49,
    D=(165+sqrt(65641))/42,
    s=D-55/7,
    T=(4+D+sqrt((4+D)^2+16D))/2,
    r=T-4-D.

The proposed eight steps, in chronological order, are

    h=(3/2,3/2,1+r,3/2,3/2,1+s,10/7,10/7).        (1)

Their physical capacity is exactly T. The scalar identities are

    D^2-(55/7)D-196/9=0,
    sD=196/9,
    rT=4D,
    T r s=784/9.                                  (2)

This makes the first bridge's primitive cut test an equality and also makes
the full-curvature quadratic primitive test an equality: the two initial
pairs each have quadratic product1/4, and the last has product9/49, so

    T |product_i(1-h_i)|=T r s (9/784)=1.

The second bridge's cut test passes strictly. Indeed 10<D<101/10, and the
positive root equation r^2+(4+D)r-4D=0 gives 12/5<r<5/2. Therefore

    r(8+r)>624/25>5488/243,

which is equivalent, using (2), to Ts<C(8+r). The internal pair-step cut tests
also pass, as follows. The upper bound D<101/10 gives T<17 by substituting17
into the defining quadratic for Omega(4,101/10). At the first two pair
queries, the right-hand sides of the cut are T-3/2 and(5/2)(T-3),
respectively, both at least T/2. At the two middle pair queries, the prefix
capacity is greater than5 and the tail capacity is greater than C, so their
product exceeds5C>17/2>T/2. At the last two queries the right-hand sides are
(17/7)(T-20/7) and T-10/7, respectively. These exceed the left-hand side
3T/7 since T>82/5>170/49 and T>5/2. Thus all eight original-index cuts have
been checked.

## 2. The physical sum clears the amplification threshold

The first polynomial in (2) is negative at D=10, so D>10. Monotonicity of
Omega therefore gives

    T>Omega(4,10)=7+sqrt(89)>82/5.

Let rho=log_2(1+sqrt(2)), the Silver exponent. The exact identity

    (1+sqrt(2))^11=8119+5741sqrt(2)<2^14

implies rho<14/11. The upper bound on the power follows, for example, from
sqrt(2)<10/7: 8119+57410/7<16384. Finally

    82^11-9^14 5^11=10042975364984229043>0,

so 9^rho<9^(14/11)<82/5<T. This is only a physical-sum statement. A genuine
primitive proof for (1), if one exists, would satisfy the seed hypothesis of
the previously recorded conditional amplification argument. Physical sum and
the tests in part1 do not establish that proof.

## 3. An available input matrix at the exact shortened pair

For the last pair, the joint identity from the complete coefficient formulas
in shortened_pair_storage.md still holds at u=10/7. With its actual gradients
p,m,q and z=p+m-2q, w=p-m, it is

    V-(4/5)H
      =(2/5)I_01+(58/35)I_10+(172/35)I_12+(316/35)I_21
        +(1/4)[P||z||^2+2D0<z,w>+Q||w||^2].        (3)

Here D0 names a matrix coefficient, distinct from the capacity D in (1).
Direct rational substitution in the displayed coefficient formulas gives

    P=102916/178605, D0=-2084/6615, Q=172/245,
    (P Q-D0^2)/16=278302/14586075>0.

Their positivity is a finite exact check, not a numerical feasibility result
for the joined eight-step schedule. The full3x3 matrix on(p,m,q) is the
explicit lift in equation5 of shortened_pair_storage.md.

## 4. Stronger hybrid witness, and why uniform shortening is unprofitable locally

There is an additional exact hostile test which is stronger than the global
quadratic near the balanced baseline. Let the first two blocks have arbitrary
nonnegative physical steps and capacities A,B, and let the last pair be(u,u),
1<u<=3/2. Use bridges1+r and1+s and total capacity
T=A+B+(1+2u)+r+s. If

    rs<=B, (u-1)s<=1,

take g(x)=clip(x,-r/B,1), f(x)=integral_0^x g(t)dt, x_0=A. The first block
stays on the positive plateau and ends at1; the first bridge and middle block
travel from-r to-r/B with constant negative gradient-r/B. The second bridge
lands at rs/B. The two final pair steps stay in the quadratic core by the
displayed assumptions. The terminal point and gradient are

    q=(u-1)^2 rs/B.

Every sampled optimum interpolation residual is zero, so its primitive energy
is exactly A^2-T^2 q^2. Therefore primitive validity NECESSARILY requires

    T rs (u-1)^2<=AB.                              (4)

For (1), rs=(784/9)/T>B=4, so this particular witness does not apply. The
failure of its core condition must not be hidden.

For three equally shortened pairs u=3/2-delta, K=1+2u, define normalized
bridges r=Kc,s=Kd and M=3+c+d. The balanced right-associated baseline has

    delta=0, d0=sqrt(2)-1,
    M0=Omega(1,1+sqrt(2)), c0=M0-3-d0>d0.

Both core assumptions for (4) hold strictly there. Along any differentiable
primitive family through this baseline, they continue to hold for small
delta>=0. The first cut and (4) give

    Mc<=2+d,
    Mcd<=1/kappa(u), kappa(u)=K(u-1)^2.

Both inequalities are equalities at delta=0. Since kappa'(0)=-9/2, their
one-sided derivative constraints imply

    M'(0)<= (9/2)/p',
    p'=2sqrt(2)[1-2/(M0+1)^2]>46sqrt(2)/25.

For detail, M'=c'+d'. Differentiating the cut gives
d'>=(M0+c0)M'/(M0+1). Differentiating the product then gives
d0(M0+c0)M'+M0(c0-d0)d'<=9/2. Substituting the lower bound for d' yields
the displayed p', using M0 c0=2+d0.

Hence the actual capacity K M has derivative

    (K M)'(0)<=-2M0+18/p'<-8+225/(23sqrt(2))<0.     (5)

This rejects the positive infinitesimal improvement obtained by shortening
ALL three pairs together near that baseline, even though the full-curvature
test alone misleadingly leaves room. It does not reject shortening only the
last pair in (1).

## 5. The exact unresolved proof obligation

A proof for (1) must use the last pair's actual matrix (3) across BOTH
bridges. The intermediate tail(middle pair,1+s,last pair) is not being assumed
primitive. Its ordinary two-block primitive cut test fails, so applying the
usual primitive recursion to it would be invalid. The outer anchor and
retained cross-block interpolation must repair that signed deficit in a
single complete certificate, or a legal function must refute the candidate.
All samples, function values and gradients must belong to one fixed function.
No additional solver or verifier request is made.

# max36: actual suffix-first inversion of a fixed capped quadratic

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. No fact is asserted.
Project: gd-exact-profile-20260926. Worker: max.
Steering: TASK.md and master_guidance 4226374d0649bf55.
This is the first analytical round of the newly authorized two-round wave:
completed baseline35, proposed completion36, completed ceiling37, attempted
ceiling38 including the historical interruption. No numerical, solver,
machine-algebra, replay, formal-verifier, installation, consult, or subagent
call is used. Operational file and publication checks are separate.

## 1. Scope and accepted interfaces

The original target remains the uniform assertion

    for every epsilon>0, for every sufficiently large n, for EVERY h>=0,
    W_n(h) >= (1-epsilon)/B_n,
    B_n=1+2 S_bullet(n),

or a genuine asymptotic counterexample with a full all-function upper
certificate. Throughout W is 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L|x_0-x_star|^2).

This draft does not establish either resolution. It solves one explicit
suffix-first extension problem, gives its paid arbitrary-depth continuation
rule, and proves a COMPLETE two-preceding-step comparison for that family.
The family is a scaled, translated symmetric capped quadratic. The comparison
is uniform in both preceding steps, the whole nonnegative suffix, every scale,
every preserved suffix location/value, every allowed minimum, and EVERY inverse
branch. It is not a theorem about all functions that interpolate these suffix
jets. In particular a nonsymmetric or multidimensional extension is outside the
comparison.

Accepted facts used as external interfaces, rather than unverified findings:

* 1252b8823bb5a290: full ordered smooth-convex interpolation, its actual static
  interpolant, and the exact unqueried-terminal reduction. Here the terminal
  jet is also actual; no incoming terminal inequality is omitted.
* b98bb807d514e80d: the legal scalar lower-bound functional

      K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2 sum h),
                  max_j (h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+sum_{i<j}h_i)^2
                                           (1+2sum_{i>j}h_i)]}.

* 1fca3e5dd555cb4a: the legal alternating-ray functional F_ray_n(h), with
  retained offsets, retained value areas, and K_n(h)<=F_ray_n(h)<=W_n(h).
  The exact two-cut specialization needed below is stated and checked in
  Section9 before it is applied.
* 0be8b54e0a33381c: finite terminal-block improvements wash out for its specified
  ConPD schedule construction and primitive sum caps. This is a separate,
  restricted schedule theorem. It is not a converse for our adversaries and
  does not furnish a comparison for the inverse maps below.

No other mathematical result is imported. The literature check read the
definition/conjugacy discussion, Theorem4 proof and Corollary1 in the already
downloaded Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur paper arXiv:1502.05666. Matlas also returned
the equivalent interpolation statement as Theorem5 of arXiv:1910.01845. Those
references are background only; the new construction and its full row check
are proved directly below, and accepted1252b8823bb5a290 already supplies the
general interpolation interface.

The old fixed-prefix obstruction48bf4f8bbf4e2b1d, and the failed backward
blending records695cc7b3cda3b59e/9949b0d613e96cde/1350be2cea28858c, informed
the choice of lane but are not proof premises.

A later, more specific recall found e6841b2627f03bfd and53024613ee396747:
an earlier UNVERIFIED binary-word calculation already excluded the whole
full-curvature symmetric-Huber family at the original target scale. That
qualitative obstruction is NOT claimed as new progress here. Section11
reproves a broader soft-threshold composition criterion inside this joint
draft, including the binary specialization and a different descending-linear
word. The new local interface is the exact suffix-preserving two-step domain,
paid inverse rule, full same-word ray comparison, and its anchor jump. The
family must not be reopened as a plausible universal lower-bound route.

## 2. Symbols and the fixed function

Define the dimensionless profile Hcap and its derivative ccap by

    Hcap(z)=z^2/2                       if |z|<=1,
             |z|-1/2                   if |z|>=1,
    ccap(z)=max(-1,min(1,z)).

The two formulas agree with their first derivatives at +/-1. The function
ccap is continuous and nondecreasing, and for u<=v it satisfies

    0 <= ccap(v)-ccap(u) <= v-u.

This follows directly by dividing [u,v] at -1 and1: the slope is either0
or1 on each resulting interval, and the increments add. Hence Hcap is convex,
differentiable, and1-smooth. It is nonnegative and vanishes only at0.

Let L>0 be the prescribed smoothness. Let eta>0 be a spatial scale, xi a
minimizer anchor, and mu a minimum value. Our ONE STATIC function is

    f(x)=mu+L eta^2 Hcap((x-xi)/eta),                 (2.1)
    f'(x)=L eta ccap((x-xi)/eta).

Its gradient is L-Lipschitz by the displayed clipping inequality. Its unique
minimizer is xi and its minimum is mu. For x=xi+eta z, one GD step of size h/L
is exactly

    z -> Tcap_h(z):=z-h ccap(z).                    (2.2)

Thus the construction follows the ORIGINAL supplied word forward. A backward
calculation merely selects its starting point; it does not reverse or replace
the supplied step order.

## 3. Preserved suffix family and its free value/anchor parameters

Fix any finite nonnegative suffix word

    k=(k_0,...,k_(m-1)),  m>=0,
    S=sum_j k_j,
    s_j=sum_{l<j} k_l,  0<=j<=m.

The empty suffix is allowed: m=0, S=s_0=0. No upper bound or positivity
assumption on any k_j is imposed.

Choose arbitrary real X,V and eta>0. These specify ALL the old suffix jets:

    x_(2+j)=X-eta s_j,
    g_(2+j)=L eta,
    f_(2+j)=V-L eta^2 s_j,           0<=j<=m.       (3.1)

The location X and the absolute value V are free, independent parameters.
They are not silently reset when earlier queries are added.

There is also a free relative anchor parameter r satisfying the exact domain

    r>=C:=1+S.                                      (3.2)

Set

    xi=X-eta r,
    mu=V-L eta^2(r-1/2).                            (3.3)

Then the function(2.1) realizes every jet(3.1). Indeed their dimensionless
coordinates are r-s_j>=r-S>=1, where Hcap=r-s_j-1/2 and ccap=1. This also
verifies every original suffix GD update. The terminal gap is positive:

    f_(2+m)-mu=L eta^2(r-S-1/2)>=L eta^2/2.          (3.4)

Changing r while holding X,V,eta fixed changes BOTH the actual minimum value
mu and its actual position xi, but leaves every old suffix jet unchanged.
Both changes will be paid in(6.3). Thus the free suffix value is not mistaken
for a free objective gap.

Within the stipulated positive-affine-suffix family, domain(3.2) is exact:
the final coordinate r-S must be at least1 to have derivative L eta for the
profile(2.1). Equality is legal. If a strictly positive affine reserve beyond
the junction is desired, replace(3.2) by r>C; the boundary statements below
then describe limits where applicable, not an attained strict-reserve optimum.
The task does not require that additional strict inequality.

## 4. Complete one-step predecessor sets, including zero and unit steps

For any h>=0 and target y, all real solutions of Tcap_h(z)=y are exactly
the union of the following sets; coincident boundary entries are counted once:

    {y+h}             if y+h>=1;                    (4.1+)
    {y-h}             if y-h<=-1;                   (4.1-)
    {y/(1-h)}         if h!=1 and |y|<=|1-h|;        (4.1c)
    [-1,1]            if h=1 and y=0.               (4.1u)

Every unlisted branch is absent. In particular(4.1c) is never used at h=1.
Proof: on z>=1 the map is z-h, on z<=-1 it is z+h, and on [-1,1]
it is(1-h)z. Solving these three affine equations WITH their interval
conditions proves necessity and sufficiency of the list. At h=1 the middle
equation is0=y and gives precisely(4.1u). No generic inverse notation hides
the exceptional branch.

For y>0 the same-sign positive branch always exists whenever y+h>=1. If that
inequality fails then h<1 and y<1-h, so the core branch exists. For y<0 use
oddness. For y=0, z=0 is always a preimage. Consequently every real target
has a predecessor for every h>=0. This includes arbitrarily large h.

Define mcap_h(t), for t>=0, as the smallest absolute value of a predecessor
of a target of absolute value t. Oddness makes this independent of its sign.
The exact closed formula is

    mcap_h(0)=0,
    mcap_0(t)=t,
    mcap_1(t)=t+1                           for t>0,

    mcap_h(t)=t/(1-h)                       if 0<h<1, 0<t<=1-h,
                t+h                        if 0<h<1, t>1-h,

    mcap_h(t)=t/(h-1)                       if h>1, 0<t<=h-1,
                t+h                        if h>1, t>h-1.            (4.2)

To check minimality when h>1 and 0<t<=h-1, the core predecessor has modulus
t/(h-1)<=1. The opposite outer predecessor, when distinct, has modulus
h-t>=1, and the same-sign outer predecessor has modulus h+t>1. Thus the
core is minimal, including equality. For t>h-1 only the same-sign outer
predecessor remains. For 0<h<1 there is only the core for t<1-h, only the
same-sign outer predecessor for t>1-h, and they coincide at the boundary.
At h=1,t>0 there is only the same-sign outer predecessor. This proves(4.2)
from the full list rather than deleting an inconvenient inverse branch.

For fixed h, mcap_h is strictly increasing on(0,infinity) and nondecreasing
on[0,infinity). In the small-step case the two formulas meet at value1.
In the large-step case the value at t=h-1 is1, whereas the right limit is
2h-1>1. Both pieces have positive slope, so this is an upward jump and
does not destroy monotonicity. The unit-step map has value0 at0 and right
limit1. The zero-step case is the identity.

A minimizing signed predecessor is explicit: use y/(1-h) on a legal core
branch of(4.2), use sign(y)(|y|+h) on an outer branch, and use0 for y=0.
At h=0 this is just y. Thus the formula chooses actual points, not merely
their possible lengths.

## 5. Solve the TWO preceding steps, without changing an old suffix jet

Let the full word be

    h=(a,b,k_0,...,k_(m-1)),     a,b>=0.             (5.1)

For any r in(3.2), first take a minimizing signed predecessor z_1 of r under
Tcap_b. Then take a minimizing signed predecessor z_0 of z_1 under Tcap_a.
Set z_(2+j)=r-s_j and define ALL new and old values and gradients by(2.1).
The two new queries are x_i=xi+eta z_i, i=0,1. They obey

    Tcap_a(z_0)=z_1,   Tcap_b(z_1)=r,               (5.2)

so they precede the unchanged suffix in its original chronology. Their
values are mu+L eta^2 Hcap(z_i); they are not chosen independently of the
gradient/geometry restrictions.

The exact smallest initial distance among ALL two-step predecessor branches
of this same function and this same suffix is

    Dcap_ab(r):=mcap_a(mcap_b(r)),
    dist(x_0,argmin f)=eta Dcap_ab(r).               (5.3)

Proof of the global, rather than just local, minimum: for any valid z_1 one
has |z_1|>=mcap_b(r). Every predecessor z_0 of that z_1 satisfies

    |z_0|>=mcap_a(|z_1|)>=mcap_a(mcap_b(r)),

where the second inequality uses the monotonicity proved in Section4.
The two displayed signed choices attain equality simultaneously. Since r>0,
both mcap values are positive, so the initial point is not the minimizer.

All parameter choices

    a,b>=0; arbitrary finite k_j>=0; eta>0; X,V real; r>=1+S             (5.4)

are feasible. This is an explicit nonempty domain, not an unsolved feasibility
condition. One can always choose the two same-sign outer predecessors
z_1=r+b,z_0=r+b+a; the minimizing rule may improve them. Both a=0 and b=0
are identity steps with identical repeated jets. The a=1 or b=1 branches
use t+1, never division by zero. Every large-step possibility in(4.1) is
retained in the minimum-radius proof.

## 6. Every ordered interpolation row and the true normalization

Here is a direct row proof for any two real points u,v, with no adjacency
assumption. Write c=ccap. If u>=v, put d=c(u)-c(v)>=0. Lipschitzness gives
d<=u-v and, on [v,u],

    c(t)-c(v) >= max{0,d-(u-t)}.

Integrating the right-hand side over its final interval of length d yields

    Hcap(u)-Hcap(v)-c(v)(u-v) >= d^2/2.             (6.1)

If u<=v, put d=c(v)-c(u)>=0. Then d<=v-u and on [u,v]

    c(v)-c(t) >= max{0,d-(t-u)}.

Integrating over the first interval of length d proves the same inequality
(6.1), now with d^2=(c(u)-c(v))^2. Thus for EVERY ordered pair u,v,

    Hcap(u)>=Hcap(v)+c(v)(u-v)+(c(u)-c(v))^2/2.

Scale by L eta^2 and translate to(2.1). For all sample indices i,j, including
the minimizer and every new/old/terminal index, this says

    f_i>=f_j+g_j(x_i-x_j)+(g_i-g_j)^2/(2L).         (6.2)

The proof holds for both orderings of EACH pair. It therefore covers new-old
rows in both directions, the two new queries against one another, every remote
suffix row, and both optimum rows. A zero step repeats a point and gives
equality. There is no omitted constraint or terminal height surrogate.

The actual minimum is mu and its unique location is xi. Combining(3.4) and
(5.3), the exact best normalized value for the fixed r in this function family
is

    Vcap_ab,k(r)=(2r-2S-1)/Dcap_ab(r)^2.             (6.3)

Every other two-step inverse branch has the same terminal gap and a greater
or equal true initial distance, so its normalized value is no larger.
Neither X nor V nor eta remains in(6.3): their cancellation follows from the
actual minimum and distance formulas, not from resetting the radius.

## 7. Full branch table and exact optimization of the remaining anchor

Put d=2S+1 and C=S+1. Call an inverse branch 'core' when the linear middle
piece of(4.2) is selected. Assign the identity h=0 to the outer label; its
formula t+h=t makes this a harmless unambiguous convention. The unit step
also has the outer label for positive targets.

At r>=C>=1, the step b has a core inverse precisely when b>1 and
r<=t:=b-1. Otherwise it has the outer inverse r+b. For the next inverse,
write u=mcap_b(r): step a is core precisely when

    a in (0,1) union (1,infinity), and u<=|a-1|.

The following four rows partition the entire domain (5.4). The first label
refers to a and the second to b; the backwards calculation still applies b
first and a second.

    labels    domain in addition to r>=C                    Dcap_ab(r)

    CC        b>1, r<=t; a!=0,1, r<=|a-1|t                 r/(|a-1|t)

    OC        b>1, r<=t; a=0 or a=1 or r>|a-1|t             r/t+a

    CO        [b<=1 or r>b-1]; a>1, r+b<=a-1               (r+b)/(a-1)

    OO        [b<=1 or r>b-1];
              [a<=1 or r+b>a-1]                            r+a+b.     (7.1)

In CC, the condition a!=0,1 is read together with a>=0, so a>0. In OC,
the last disjunct is only needed for a not equal to0 or1. Boundaries are
assigned to core except for the identity convention. A row may be empty;
their union is never empty. In particular the OO row contains all sufficiently
large r. At a=0,b=0,r=1, the identity endpoint is assigned to OO and has
value1. No formula that divides by a-1 is applied to that identity step.

The formulas display every division and its strictly positive denominator.
They also show that the only branch breakpoints on r>=C can be drawn from

    b-1, |a-1|(b-1), a-b-1.                         (7.2)

Not every listed number is an active breakpoint, which causes no problem.
On each nonempty interval of the partition, write Dcap_ab(r)=A r+B.
Here A>0 and B>=0. Direct differentiation, within that interval only, gives

    d/dr [(2r-d)/(A r+B)^2]
      =2[B+A(d-r)]/(A r+B)^3.                       (7.3)

Thus its sole possible stationary maximum is r=d+B/A. For the four rows
the possible stationary radii are respectively

    d,  d+a(b-1),  d+b,  d+a+b.                     (7.4)

Consequently an EXACT finite formula for the optimum over every allowed r is

    max_{r in Ecap_ab,S} (2r-d)/[mcap_a(mcap_b(r))]^2,               (7.5)

where Ecap_ab,S is the following explicit list after deleting entries less
than C and deleting repetitions:

    C, b-1, |a-1|(b-1), a-b-1,
    d, d+a(b-1), d+b, d+a+b.                         (7.6)

These are at most eight analytic candidate expressions; this draft does not
numerically enumerate or evaluate a parameter grid. Extra inactive candidates
are allowed because they are still legal radii in the original domain.

For completeness, an excluded right-hand interval endpoint cannot conceal a
larger supremum. On positive arguments mcap_h is left-continuous, with only
upward jumps to its right. Its composition has the same property: if an inner
map jumps across an outer breakpoint, the outer monotonicity preserves the
upward direction. Therefore Vcap has only downward jumps to its right. The
value at a breakpoint is at least the limiting value of any immediately
following interval. Formula(7.3) handles the interiors, C is included, and
Vcap(r) tends to0 at infinity since the final row is OO. Every possible
maximum is therefore attained at a listed breakpoint or stationary point.
The nonempty list contains C. This proves(7.5) even at every zero/unit-step
degeneracy.

## 8. The paid continuation invariant exists at arbitrary depth

For any finite nonnegative prefix word w=(w_0,...,w_(p-1)), define the explicit
map

    Mcap_w(t)=mcap_(w_0)(mcap_(w_1)(...mcap_(w_(p-1))(t)...)),
    Mcap_empty(t)=t.                                 (8.1)

This is composition of the CLOSED formulas(4.2), not an unspecified feasible
set, optimization oracle, or Bellman lemma. It is the exact smallest modulus
of an initial point whose GD run on Hcap under the original w ends at a given
signed target of modulus t. Induction proves this: for the first step,
minimize over possible later starting moduli; monotonicity of mcap_(w_0)
shows that the smallest available later modulus is optimal, and the signed
choice in Section4 attains it. Equivalently one can induct by prepending a
step and apply the two inequalities in Section5.

For words u,v, where u followed by v denotes their original chronological
concatenation,

    Mcap_(u followed by v)=Mcap_u composed with Mcap_v.              (8.2)

All these maps are nondecreasing and strictly increasing for positive input.
Their exact state on(0,infinity) can be kept as at most p+1 intervals with
affine formulas A t+B, A>0,B>=0, and the values at their breakpoints. This
assertion is symbolic, not a computation: start with the single piece t.
Prepending one mcap_h leaves all existing breakpoints and introduces at most
one more, because a strictly increasing inner map can cross its sole positive
threshold |1-h| at most once. If the crossing occurs within an affine piece,
the new threshold is explicitly (|1-h|-B)/A. If a jump crosses it, it uses an
already existing breakpoint. h=0 adds nothing and h=1 is t+1 on positive
inputs, also adding nothing. The composition coefficients remain A>0,B>=0.

For the old suffix jets(3.1), a fixed r>=C, and any such prefix, the actual
function, minimum and suffix remain(2.1),(3.3). The true paid quantities are

    initial distance = eta Mcap_w(r),
    terminal gap = L eta^2(r-S-1/2),
    normalized payoff = (2r-2S-1)/Mcap_w(r)^2.         (8.3)

All gradients and values are actual samples of the SAME static function,
and Section6 still checks every remote row. This proves scalable feasibility
and exact normalization in this restricted family for arbitrary h, including
all zero and unit entries. It does not prove the original-B lower comparison.

For perspective, if every entry of w is strictly less than2 and the incoming
target is r>=1, every inverse branch is outer: its input remains at least1,
while its core threshold is less than1. Thus Mcap_w(r)=r+sum w. Optimizing
the positive-affine final-state family then gives just the corresponding
global sum-based Huber value. Equality h=2 is genuinely different at target1
and must not be silently put into this observation.

## 9. Complete SAME-word comparison for two preceding steps

We now compare EVERY construction of Sections3--7 with accepted witnesses
on exactly the original word(5.1). No tail entry is reordered or replaced.

The following three entries belong to K_(m+2)(h) by b98bb807d514e80d:

    K_global=1/[d+2a+2b],
    K_first=(a-1)_+^2/[d+2b],
    K_second=(b-1)_+^2/[(1+a)^2 d].                 (9.1)

They need not be the COMPLETE K, since there are also possible cuts inside
the suffix. Bounding by any one entry is enough to bound by the complete K.

If a>1, b>1, put P=(a-1)(b-1). For the checkpoint choice a,b in accepted
1fca3e5dd555cb4a, the ordinary blocks before a and between a,b are empty,
and the final ordinary block is the unchanged k with sum S. Its recurrence
therefore gives

    m_0=1, m_1=a-1, O_0=O_1=0,
    z_term=P, m_old=1, e_term=0.

Its precise feasibility requirement is P>=1+S=C. In that domain the allowed
last slope is q in [1,P/C], and the optimized value is

    R_two(P)=P^2/d                  if P>=d,
               2P-d                if C<=P<=d.       (9.2)

Indeed q_opt=max(1,P/d). If P>=d then P/d<=P/C since d>=C, so it is
feasible and gives P^2/d. If P<=d the feasible maximum is q=1 and gives
2P-d. At P=d the formulas agree. Thus (9.2) is a legal entry of the
COMPLETE F_ray_(m+2)(h), with every remote ray offset and value condition
retained. We only invoke it after proving P>=C in the relevant branch.

Here are the four comparisons, using the actual table(7.1).

OO. Here Dcap=r+a+b. For any r>0 and c>=0,

    (r+c)^2-(d+2c)(2r-d)=(r-d-c)^2>=0.

Take c=a+b to get Vcap<=1/(d+2a+2b)=K_global.

CO. Here a>1 and Dcap=(r+b)/(a-1). Use the same square with c=b:

    Vcap=(a-1)^2(2r-d)/(r+b)^2
         <=(a-1)^2/(d+2b)=K_first.

OC. Here t=b-1>0, r<=t, and Dcap=r/t+a. Put u=r/t in(0,1].
The identity (tu-d)^2>=0 gives

    2tu-d <= t^2 u^2/d.

Because a>=0 and u<=1,

    u/(u+a) <= 1/(1+a),

as follows by multiplying the positive denominators and cancelling u.
Therefore

    Vcap=(2tu-d)/(u+a)^2
         <=t^2/d [u/(u+a)]^2
         <=t^2/[(1+a)^2 d]=K_second.

CC with 0<a<1. Put P=(1-a)t. Then Dcap=r/P and

    Vcap=P^2(2r-d)/r^2 <=P^2/d,

using (r-d)^2>=0. Also (1-a)(1+a)=1-a^2<=1, so

    P^2/d <= t^2/[(1+a)^2d]=K_second.

CC with a>1. Put P=(a-1)t. The exact branch condition gives r<=P,
and r>=C proves P>=C. Thus the ray witness(9.2) IS feasible. Write
phi_d(r)=(2r-d)/r^2. The square(r-d)^2>=0 gives phi_d(r)<=1/d.
If P>=d this proves Vcap=P^2 phi_d(r)<=P^2/d=R_two(P).
If C<=P<=d, the derivative

    phi_d'(r)=2(d-r)/r^3

is nonnegative for r<=P<=d. Hence

    Vcap=P^2 phi_d(r)<=P^2 phi_d(P)=2P-d=R_two(P).

This exhausts the domain, including every equality case by the branch
conventions. No bound on a,b or S has been imposed. Consequently

    for all parameters(5.4) and EVERY two-step inverse branch,
    actual normalized payoff <= F_ray_(m+2)(h).      (9.3)

Taking the exact maximum(7.5) preserves(9.3). The conclusion covers every
minimum position/value obtained by varying r, eta, X,V in this family.
It is therefore a complete obstruction to gaining a NEW lower witness from
this two-preceding-step capped-quadratic extension alone. It is not merely
a comparison with K_global or with a selected weak Huber channel.

For avoidance of a quantifier error: the above is an UPPER bound on a
specified witness FAMILY. It is not an upper bound on W_(m+2)(h), whose
adversary class is much larger. A bad word or a failed witness family is
not a minimax disproof.

## 10. Exact nontrivial example and the anchor discontinuity

Take a=b=3 and suffix k=(1), so S=1,C=2,d=3. At r=2 use eta=L=1,
xi=mu=0, equivalently X=2,V=3/2. The exact minimal predecessor trajectory is

    z_0=1/2, z_1=-1, z_2=2, z_3=1,
    gradients=(1/2,-1,1,1),
    values=(1/8,1/2,3/2,1/2).

Each update is the ORIGINAL sequence(3,3,1):

    1/2-3(1/2)=-1,
    -1-3(-1)=2,
    2-1(1)=1.

The unique minimum is0 at0. The true initial squared distance is1/4 and
the true terminal gap is1/2, so W-normalized payoff is4.

This is the OPTIMUM of the entire displayed suffix-preserving family for
this word: its domain is r>=2. At r=2 both inverse branches are core and
give4. At every r>2 both are outer, so

    Vcap(r)=(2r-3)/(r+6)^2<=1/15,

by the OO comparison, with equality at r=9. Thus the overall maximum is4.

The COMPLETE scalar K for this same word is

    max{1/15, 4/9, 1/12, 0}=4/9.

The legal two-cut ray uses m_0=1,m_1=2,P=4,q=4/3 and has payoff16/3.
This is also the complete F_ray here. A checkpoint set including the last
step1 and at least one earlier checkpoint is infeasible: if m_last>0 denotes
the slope just before the last checkpoint and O>=0 the sum of its ray offsets,
its final z_term=(1-1)m_last-O<=0 while the previous terminal-ray slope is
strictly positive. Selecting step1 alone gives0. The only remaining
possibilities are the empty set, the two single3 cuts, and both3 cuts,
whose values are exactly those just listed. Therefore

    K_3(3,3,1)=4/9 < 4 < F_ray_3(3,3,1)=16/3.       (10.1)

The nine-fold gain over K does not beat the already accepted stronger
witness on the SAME word. No schedule-improvement claim follows from it.

Now keep the ORIGINAL suffix jets X=2,V=3/2,eta=L=1 fixed and change
the relative anchor to r=2+epsilon for any epsilon>0. The function(2.1)
has true minimizer xi=-epsilon and true minimum mu=-epsilon, while its old
suffix positions2,1, gradients1,1 and values3/2,1/2 remain unchanged.
The b=3 core preimage disappears because r>2. Therefore

    Dcap_3,3(2)=1/2,
    Dcap_3,3(2+epsilon)=8+epsilon,
    Vcap(2+epsilon)=(1+2epsilon)/(8+epsilon)^2.       (10.2)

For epsilon>0 the actual minimizing path has dimensionless points
8+epsilon,5+epsilon,2+epsilon,1+epsilon, and therefore actual points
8,5,2,1. Its gradients are all1 and its actual values are
15/2,9/2,3/2,1/2. These match the moved minimum and the old suffix.
As epsilon decreases to0 the true-distance payoff tends to1/64, whereas
its value AT epsilon=0 is4. The discontinuity is in the minimum-radius
inverse selection, not in the validity of any of the individual functions.
Both the numerator and the distance in(10.2) include the moving minimum.

This explicitly refutes continuity, and any unqualified smooth parameter
update assumption, for this minimum-radius backwards state. It does not
claim discontinuity of the unrestricted interpolation problem or of W.

## 11. All-depth obstruction: exact soft-threshold schedules

This section strengthens the scope of the closure from two preceding steps
to the entire scalar family(2.1), with ANY terminal regime and any initial
point. The earlier unverified binary counterexample e6841b2627f03bfd prompted
a memory check; nothing from it is assumed as a fact. The full proof follows.

For A>=0 write

    Rsh_A(z)=sign(z) max(|z|-A,0),

with Rsh_A(0)=0. This notation is a soft-threshold map, not the project's
objective normalization R_n. If A>=1 and h>=0, then the exact identity

    Rsh_A composed with Tcap_h = Rsh_(A+h)           (11.1)

holds IF AND ONLY IF h<=A+1.

Proof of sufficiency: both sides are odd, so take z>=0. For 0<=z<=1,
Tcap_h(z)=(1-h)z has absolute value at most |1-h|<=A, since h<=A+1
and A>=1. The left side vanishes, as does the right side because A+h>=1.
For z>=1, Tcap_h(z)=z-h. If z-h<0, its negative modulus is h-z<=h-1<=A,
so the left side vanishes, again agreeing with the right side. If z-h>=0,
the left side is max(z-h-A,0), exactly the right side. This proves(11.1)
on every real z. For necessity, if h>A+1, take z=1. The left side equals
-(h-1-A)<0, whereas the right side is0. Thus the identity fails. The zero
step is included in the sufficient condition and gives the identity map.

Now let a nonempty original word w end with a physical unit step, and suppose
each preceding step satisfies the explicit tail-sum condition

    w_i <= 1+sum_{j>i} w_j.                         (11.2)

Let H=sum_i w_i. The last unit step equals Rsh_1. Applying(11.1) successively
backwards proves that the COMPLETE forward GD map under w is Rsh_H. Every
tail used in the induction has sum at least1, so every hypothesis of(11.1)
is met. Zero steps at any preceding positions are allowed. Trailing zero
steps may also be appended without changing the map. No order restriction
beyond(11.2) is needed for this claim.

We next optimize over the ENTIRE family(2.1), including all possible terminal
core points, while paying the true minimum and distance. By translation,
scaling and oddness, let the initial dimensionless radius be z>0. If z<=H,
the terminal point is the minimizer and the payoff is0. If z>H, write the
terminal radius as v=z-H>0. The normalized payoff is

    2 Hcap(v)/(H+v)^2.

For 0<v<=1 this equals v^2/(H+v)^2, an increasing function of v because
H>=1. Its maximum on that interval is1/(H+1)^2. For v>=1 it equals
(2v-1)/(H+v)^2. Differentiation gives

    2(H+1-v)/(H+v)^3,

so its maximum occurs at v=H+1 and equals1/(2H+1). The inequality
(H+1)^2>=2H+1 shows that the latter is the overall maximum. Therefore

    sup over ALL eta,xi,mu,x_0!=xi in family(2.1)
        2(f(x_n)-mu)/(L|x_0-xi|^2) = 1/(1+2H).     (11.3)

The value is attained at dimensionless initial radius2H+1, ending at H+1.
In particular changing the whole-word-dependent scale, moving the minimum,
or using a terminal core instead of the affine suffix cannot repair(11.3).

For a polynomial-size example choose the actual length-n word

    w_dec^(n)=(n,n-1,...,2,1), n>=1,
    H=n(n+1)/2.

A step j>=2 has tail sum j(j-1)/2, and

    j <= 1+j(j-1)/2

is equivalent to (j-1)(j-2)>=0. Thus(11.2) holds. Formula(11.3) gives the
EXACT entire-family value

    Vcap_dec(n)=1/[1+n(n+1)].                       (11.4)

Accepted b98bb807d514e80d supplies B_n<=1+4n^p, p=log_2(3)<2. Its
applicability is only the original benchmark recurrence and the present
length n; no schedule restriction is involved. Hence

    0 <= Vcap_dec(n)/U_n^ZJ
      = B_n/[1+n(n+1)]
      <= (1+4n^p)/[1+n(n+1)] -> 0.                 (11.5)

On EXACTLY the same word, the first-step scalar cut from the same accepted
fact gives, for n>=2,

    W_n(w_dec^(n)) >= K_n(w_dec^(n))
                   >= (n-1)^2/[1+n(n-1)] -> 1.      (11.6)

Therefore the exhibited small restricted-family value says nothing small
about the unrestricted W_n; indeed a previously accepted scalar witness is
already much stronger. Since F_ray>=K, the full accepted ray reference also
dominates this calibration. Equations(11.5)--(11.6) are a scoped obstruction
to the family, not a negative answer to the original minimax question.

For comparison with the RECALLED old finding, descending binary steps
(2^(n-1),2^(n-2),...,1) have H=2^n-1 and satisfy equality in(11.2) at every
preceding position. Formula(11.3), proved here independently, specializes
to1/(2^(n+1)-1), exactly the earlier recorded family obstruction. This is
a consistency check, not a new claim of discovering that obstruction.

## 12. What has and has not been closed

Closed in this UNVERIFIED joint draft:

1. A concrete positive-payoff suffix with independent old location/value,
   a free scale, and an explicit range of moving minimum anchors.
2. The ACTUAL two-preceding-step extension on the original word, including
   ALL inverse branches, joint earlier points/values, h=0,h=1 and arbitrarily
   large steps, the full nonempty domain, every ordered row, and true radius.
3. Exact optimization of its remaining relative anchor via at most eight
   analytic candidates, with jumps and boundary ownership accounted for.
4. An explicit paid continuation invariant for this SAME static-function
   family at arbitrary depth, without a hidden feasibility oracle.
5. A complete SAME-word domination theorem for its ENTIRE two-step extension
   family by accepted scalar/alternating-ray references, and a sharp finite
   example showing why comparison only to K is inadequate.
6. An exact all-depth soft-threshold composition criterion, with a complete
   whole-family payoff optimization. It re-establishes the already known
   scoped failure of full-curvature symmetric Hubers and gives a descending
   linear word whose restricted ratio to the ORIGINAL B profile tends to0.

Still OPEN:

* A useful original-B estimate for a genuinely larger continuation family.
  The maps(8.1) are exact feasibility/payoff invariants for their fixed family,
  but Section11 proves that they CANNOT satisfy the required all-word original-B
  lower estimate. That family-level question is closed, not merely unproved.
* Whether a richer suffix family, asymmetric clipping, additional affine
  offsets, or genuine transverse geometry can escape the two-step domination
  while retaining a paid all-row continuation rule. Merely allowing an interior
  terminal jet in the unchanged symmetric family does not escape Section11.
* The original arbitrary-h exact-profile minimax problem in PROBLEM.md.

One must not apply(9.3) repeatedly without a new proof. After a general earlier
inverse step the retained portion contains core and/or alternating gradients,
and is not the positive constant-gradient suffix assumed in Section3. Resetting
the clipping threshold or anchor to restore that assumption would in general
change old jets. Likewise the accepted ConPD washout theorem has schedule
hypotheses not furnished by(8.2); scalar map composition is not an all-function
certificate composition.

Suggested remaining analytical round: retain the paid minimum/jet bookkeeping
but select a genuinely larger concrete suffix family, such as asymmetric
clipping or a jointly constructed nonhomogeneous transverse support. Do not
retune the closed symmetric full-curvature family, its threshold law, or its
moving-minimum parametrization in hope of recovering the target. A lower core
curvature is outside this obstruction but needs a paid whole-word rule, not
a free parameter claim. Any new lemma must be reproved in a new joint draft
or separately accepted before it can be used as a fact.

No fact_submit was authorized or called. All claims above remain UNVERIFIED
until a future selected gate accepts the final object. No external citation is
used as a new black-box premise, and no CPU computation supplies a proof step.

**bfff4aa387a9d818** is persisted as an UNVERIFIED proof_attempt, with

The candidate closes the assigned remaining MAIN band C<c(c-2) and
assembles ONE full proof for BOTH original static realizations. For
every m>=1536, every 0<tau<=1/4, all independent kappa>0 and
0<epsilon<=1/2, and every respective eligible chronological selection:

- Every nonempty MAIN actual closest-distance payoff is less than
  (441/500)/(1+2*S_m).
- Every nonempty SOURCE actual closest-distance payoff is less than
  (9801/10000)/(1+2*S_m).
- The exact MAIN-only and combined family optima equal the genuine
  Huber value 1/(1+2*S_m). The exact SOURCE-only optimum is its own
  k=0 member 1/[2*(1+S_m)], with no identification of these two members.
- At every such m the original all-split benchmark ratio is <237/250
  at tau=1/4 and uniformly <79/80 for 1/5<=tau<=1/4. For each fixed
  0<tau<=1/4 the exact scaled limit is Z_1/(2*exp(tau)).

Here S_m is the mass of the unchanged H_(m,tau), all children retain
the enclosing(m,tau), C=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)*exp(kappa*theta_sp),
theta_sp=arctan(1/epsilon), c=(1+sqrt(2))*(1+tau/m), and Z_1 is the
accepted original benchmark ray limit from fact04219554e41d9750.

The new mechanism is Section9A, equations(46a)--(46n). For a complete
physical child H_j, each step alpha is controlled by its actual suffix
mass y_mass-1, and the finite sum obeys

    sum alpha/y_mass <= (c/2)*log(1+S_j)+5/4.

The EXACT discrete update, including both cap and uncapped branches,
gives a per-step polar turn <=(101/100)*kappa*alpha/y_mass. All
projected angles on H_j consequently exceed1/16. This proves a real
surplus from that ordered child:

    P(H_j)+Q(H_j)/epsilon-C*S_j >= S_j/4.

An incoming block between consecutive selected levels lev_prev,lev_i
ends with the complete H_j for j=min(lev_prev,lev_i). Its additional
prefix can cap; every such prefix slope still pays nonnegative surplus.
Thus its ACTUAL full-step main denominator satisfies

    P_i+max(epsilon*b_i,epsilon+(1+Q_i)/epsilon)
      >= C*(s_i+1)+(c*(c-2)-C)*(1+S_j).

This lower bound, coupled to the unchanged first-radius formula(43),
yields an UPPER comparison that remembers adjacent selected levels.
Minimum-level deletion explicitly pays the changed next denominator:
the merged surrogate is at most c*(c-2)*(1+merged physical mass).
The interior and first-factor differences then reduce to the same
nonnegative quantity c*(c-2)-u*(2+u), where
u=(b_j-1)/(1+S_j)<=c-2. Terminal deletion uses the original tail factor.
Deletion acts only on this proved comparison, never on actual P,Q.

Every reused unverified geometric, source, hierarchy and benchmark
deduction is re-established in the SAME complete manuscript. The only
accepted input is fact04219554e41d9750 in its original benchmark and

The full functions, actual trajectories, true minimum sets/radii,

Scope remains restricted to the TWO specified actual families at their

It is a joint analytical candidate, not an accepted fact. Main selects
