# A necessary asymmetric region for a three-step capacity improvement

Status: complete candidate reductions and a scoped optimization result;
NOT verifier-accepted. No solver or fact_submit. The public max4 candidates
5b4359baa88bf3e4 and 500d209094028aef suggested the witnesses; all needed
legal data and deductions are included here, not used as unverified facts.
Accepted input: smooth-convex interpolation in 1252b8823bb5a290.

## 1. Statement

For h=(u,v,w) in [0,infinity)^3 put T=1+u+v+w. Write V_h for the
intrinsic primitive energy defined below. Call h primitive if V_h>=0
on every finite-dimensional convex differentiable 1-smooth GD run with
a minimizer. Put t=sqrt(2), lambda=1+t, and T_0=lambda^2=3+2t.

If h is primitive and T>=T_0, then either

    h=(t,2,t), T=T_0,

or necessarily

    t<u<7/4,   1<v<2,   1<w<t,
    T_0<=T<7,  u+w>=2T/lambda-2.                   (1)

The scalar constraints below further restrict the remaining region.
This does NOT exclude region (1) and does NOT prove the global
three-step capacity bound. It is a global, orientation-sensitive
reduction beyond the existing local and fixed-middle-step results.

For the remaining region, the one-parameter completion of the mixed
two-dimensional witness in Section5 cannot strengthen its original
endpoint choice. Every finite interpolation inequality is checked.

## 2. Actual witnesses and all their legality conditions

For any legal run use

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0,
    V_h=2sum_(i<3)h_i(f_i-f_3)
         +sum_(i<3)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
         -(T-1)T||g_3||^2.

For data with f_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2 and x_3=g_3, set
y_i=x_i-g_i. Expansion gives

    Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>, Q_(i,3)=0,
    V_h=||x_0||^2-T^2||g_3||^2.                   (2)

All witnesses used below have these properties and include the legal
optimum jet (0,0,0). Besides the finite Q conditions, optimum legality
requires <g_i,y_i>>=0; the other direction is saturated by the value
formula. A feasible finite array is realized by the conjugate maximum
of quadratics in accepted 1252b8823bb5a290. Thus the arrays specify
fixed globally smooth convex functions with a minimizer.

The interval-projection witness at any selected step b, with ordinary
sums P,Q, has gradient 1 before b and -(b-1)/(Q+1) after it, starting
at P+1 and ending at its negative threshold, when b>1. Formula (2)
gives the necessary cut condition

    T(b-1)_+<=(P+1)(Q+1).                         (3)

This is a direct fixed clipped-derivative construction, including zero
ordinary steps. Apply it at the first and last step to obtain

    u,w<=M(T):=2T/(T+1)<2.                        (4)

For u,v,w>1 abbreviate

    a=u-1, A=u+1, r=v-1, D=w+1, eta=r/D;
    T=A+D+r.

The full-curvature quadratic f(x)=x^2/2, x_0=1, gives

    F_Q=1-T^2 a^2 r^2(w-1)^2>=0.                  (5)

The middle-cut interval, with x=(A,1,-r,-eta) and
g=(1,1,-eta,-eta), gives

    F_B=A^2-T^2 eta^2>=0, or Tr<=AD.              (6)

If r(w-1)<=1, the one-sided Huber derivative min(x,1), started at A,
has x=(A,1,-r,r(w-1)) and g=(1,1,-r,r(w-1)). It gives

    F_C=A^2-T^2 r^2(w-1)^2>=0.                   (7)

When eta a<1, a two-dimensional mixed witness gives

    F_N=a u F_B+(1+eta)u^2(2-u^2)>=0.             (8)

Here is its complete verification. Combine the middle-cut base array
from (6) with the auxiliary scalar array

    x_aux=(1,-a,ra,eta a),
    g_aux=(1,-a,eta a,eta a),
    f_aux,i=x_aux,i*g_aux,i-g_aux,i^2/2.

The auxiliary proximal points are (0,0,w eta a,0). Its only positive
finite residual is Q_aux,(0,2)=(1-eta a)w eta a. Every optimum
inequality is legal because g_aux,i*y_aux,i>=0. The base proximal
points are (u,0,-w eta,0); its Q_(0,2)=-(1+eta)w eta and all its
residuals are nonpositive. With

    epsilon=(1+eta)/[a(1-eta a)],

take the orthogonal sum of the base and sqrt(epsilon) times the
auxiliary points and gradients, with the summed values. Residuals add;
the sole positive auxiliary edge is exactly canceled and every other
edge is nonpositive. The energy equals

    F_B+epsilon(1-T^2 eta^2 a^2)
       =F_N/[a(1-eta a)].                         (9)

For the algebra use 1-a^2 A^2=u^2(2-u^2). This proves (8) with
all actual edges, rather than an assumed two-dimensional interpolant.

## 3. A uniform box where all four witnesses apply

Assume T>=T_0 and all the cut conditions (3). First v>1. Otherwise
T=1+u+v+w<=2+2M(T), so T^2-5T-2<=0. At T=T_0 this polynomial
equals 2t>0 and it increases thereafter, a contradiction.

Thus r>0 and the middle cut (6) applies. As T=A+D+r,

    T(T-A-D)<=AD<=(A+D)^2/4,
    T<=lambda(A+D)/2<=lambda(M(T)+1).

Consequently

    T^2+(1-3lambda)T-lambda<=0.                    (10)

Its left side increases for T>=T_0, and at T=7 equals
56-22lambda>0 because lambda<5/2. Hence T<7 and (4) gives
u,w<7/4.

Both u and w exceed1. If, for example, u<=1, then A<=2, D<11/4,
so r=T-A-D>T-19/4. But

    Tr>T(T-19/4)>=T_0(T_0-19/4)
       =11/4+5t/2>11/2>AD,

contradicting the middle cut. The argument for w is identical.

On this entire necessary box the extra hypotheses of (7) and (8) hold:

    r(w-1)<=A(w^2-1)/T<363/(64T_0)<1,
    eta a<=Aa/T=(u^2-1)/T<33/(16T_0)<1.           (11)

For the first strict numerical comparison, 363<64T_0 is equivalent
to 171<128sqrt(2), and squaring gives 29241<32768. Thus no local
neighborhood restriction is being silently used in this global screen.
The earlier inequality T<=lambda(A+D)/2 also gives

    u+w>=2T/lambda-2>=2t.                         (12)

## 4. Excluding three of the four endpoint orientations

Suppose u<=t. By (12), w>=t. Since T>=T_0 and w<2, the difference
between the two sides of the necessary inequality Tr(w-1)<=A is
at least

    T_0(T_0-t-w-2)(w-1)-(t+1)
       =T_0(w-t)(2-w)>=0.                         (13)

It is strictly positive unless u=w=t and T=T_0. In that exceptional
case v=2. Hence every other candidate must have u>t.

Suppose next that also w>=t. On the rectangle [t,M(T)]^2 consider

    q_T(u,w)=T(u-1)(w-1)(T-u-w-2).

This function is strictly concave in either endpoint with the other
fixed. Its minimum is attained at a corner. At T=T_0, M(T_0)=1+t/2,
the corner values are

    q_(T_0)(t,t)=1,
    q_(T_0)(t,M(T_0))=q_(T_0)(M(T_0),t)=lambda/2>1,
    q_(T_0)(M(T_0),M(T_0))=lambda/2>1.

Each corner value strictly increases with T>=T_0. All its positive
factors increase; in particular the derivatives of
T-t-M(T)-2 and T-2M(T)-2 are respectively
1-2/(T+1)^2 and 1-4/(T+1)^2, both positive.
Thus q_T>1 except at the single point T=T_0,u=w=t. This contradicts
the quadratic condition (5). Every nonexceptional candidate therefore
satisfies u>t>w.

Finally its middle step is less than2. For u>t the expression F_N in
(8), with u,w held fixed, strictly decreases as r>0 increases: the
negative squared term in F_B decreases, while u^2(2-u^2)<0.
At r=1 a direct expansion gives

    D^2 F_N/u
       =P_u(D):=u^2 D^2+(4-2u^2-u^3)D-(u-1)(u+2)^2.

This polynomial has one positive and one negative root, since its
leading coefficient is positive and its constant term negative. Also

    P_u(lambda)=-(lambda+1)u^3-2u^2+4(lambda+1)<0

for u>t: the right side vanishes at t and has strictly negative
derivative for u>0. Its positive root is therefore beyond lambda,
and P_u(D)<0 for 0<D<=lambda. Since w<t gives D<lambda, F_N<0
at r=1 and at every r>=1. Necessarily r<1, proving (1).

The remaining constraints can be written without a mixed-energy
abbreviation. In the box (1), a primitive candidate must satisfy

    Tr<=AD,
    T a r(w-1)<=1,
    u^2+u-1+u(2-u^2)eta-(u-1)T^2 eta^2>=0.        (14)

The last expression is F_N/u after expansion. No sufficiency of (14)
is asserted, and no global feasibility or infeasibility conclusion for
this remaining box is claimed.

## 5. Optimizing a continuous auxiliary completion does not improve F_N

Work in box (1) and assume the scalar tests F_B,F_Q>=0. Keep the same
base array in (6), but vary the auxiliary third gradient. Put k=ra<1
and choose z in [k/(w+1),k]. Use

    x_aux=(1,-a,k,k-wz),
    g_aux=(1,-a,z,k-wz),
    f_aux,i=x_aux,i*g_aux,i-g_aux,i^2/2.

Every GD equation is exact. The sole possibly nonzero proximal point
is y_aux,2=k-z>=0. The residuals entering query2 are

    Q_aux,(0,2)=(1-z)(k-z),
    Q_aux,(1,2)=(-a-z)(k-z)<=0,
    Q_aux,(3,2)=(k-(w+1)z)(k-z)<=0.

Every other finite residual is zero, and every optimum constraint is
valid. For z<k the complete legal range of mixing coefficients is

    0<=epsilon<=epsilon_max(z)
       =(1+eta)w eta/[(1-z)(k-z)].                 (15)

The same active edge is exactly canceled at the upper endpoint.
The mixed energy is

    F_B+epsilon[1-T^2(k-wz)^2].                    (16)

Let q=k-wz, A_0=w-k>0, B_0=k(w-1)>0. The quadratic test says
T B_0<=1. Since q ranges from q_0=k/(w+1) down to -B_0, the bracket
in (16) can be negative only when q>1/T. In that case epsilon_max
is optimal. Apart from its positive constant factor, the improvement
over F_B is

    R(q)=(T^2 q^2-1)/[(q+A_0)(q+B_0)].

For q>1/T its derivative has numerator

    T^2(A_0+B_0)q^2+2(T^2 A_0B_0+1)q+(A_0+B_0)>0.

The greatest improvement is therefore at the largest q=q_0, namely
z=k/(w+1)=eta a. This is exactly the auxiliary choice in (8)--(9).
If q_0<=1/T there is no negative auxiliary energy anywhere in the
family. At z=k all auxiliary proximal points are zero and its energy
is F_Q>=0, so this boundary supplies no additional improvement.

Thus the original mixed witness is already optimal in this whole
one-parameter completion family, with arbitrary admissible mixture
weight. Repairing the remaining region (1) requires another change of
geometry or a separate algebraic argument, rather than this retuning.

## 6. Reversal caution

The downloaded and read Grimmer--Shu--Wang paper arXiv:2410.16249v2,
Section3.1, and Zhang--Jiang arXiv:2410.12395, Proposition5.5, establish
reversal properties for their recursively constructed basic schedules.
The checked statements do not supply reversal invariance for every
primitive schedule. No reversal theorem is used in Sections1--5.
The asymmetric region (1) cannot be discarded by silently swapping
u and w.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001067 -->
# A two-dimensional obstruction at a saturated primitive cut

Status: complete candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. No solver or verifier
call. The only existing fact used is 1252b8823bb5a290, for its explicit finite
smooth-convex interpolation construction. Every other identity is proved here.

## 1. Definitions and an energy identity

For a nonnegative physical schedule h of length n let T_tot=1+sum_i h_i.
For normalized smoothness 1 and an optimum jet (0,0,0), put

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2 sum_(i<n) h_i(f_i-f_n)
          +sum_(i<n) h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
          -T_tot(T_tot-1)||g_n||^2.

Here primitive validity means V_h>=0 on every legal convex 1-smooth GD
trajectory. Suppose the sampled data additionally satisfy

    f_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2 for every i,
    x_n=g_n.

The first condition is exactly Q_(i,star)=0; the second gives
f_n=||g_n||^2/2. Expanding

    ||x_0||^2-||x_n||^2
       =2 sum h_i<g_i,x_i>-sum h_i^2||g_i||^2

therefore gives the exact identity

    V_h=||x_0||^2-T_tot^2||g_n||^2.                 (1)

For any such data, whether or not yet jointly interpolable, write y_i=x_i-g_i.
Direct substitution also gives

    Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>.                             (2)

## 2. General two-bridge construction

Let a,b,c be arbitrary finite nonnegative blocks, with capacities

    A=1+sum a_i, B=1+sum b_i, C=1+sum c_i.

Take r_L,r_R>0 and h=(a,1+r_L,b,1+r_R,c). Define

    D_tail=B+C+r_R, T_tot=A+D_tail+r_L,
    eta_cut=r_L/D_tail, beta_inner=r_R/C,
    p_aux=-B/r_L.

The first scalar array is a legal Huber trajectory: start at A and use
derivative clip(x,-eta_cut,1). During a and at its endpoint the gradient is
1. Its endpoint is 1. The first bridge lands at -r_L=-eta_cut D_tail.
Every remaining query has gradient -eta_cut, and the final point is
-eta_cut. All the data satisfy the hypotheses of (1), so

    V_base=A^2-T_tot^2 eta_cut^2.                   (3)

The second scalar array is defined as follows. During a use constant
gradient p_aux, start at A p_aux, and end at p_aux. The first bridge lands
at -r_L p_aux=B. On the remaining schedule (b,1+r_R,c) use the ACTUAL
Huber trajectory with derivative clip(x,-beta_inner,1), starting at B.
The b block ends at 1, the second bridge lands at -r_R=-C beta_inner,
and c ends at -beta_inner. Assign every value by
f_i=g_i x_i-g_i^2/2. Thus (1) yields

    V_aux=A^2 B^2/r_L^2-T_tot^2 beta_inner^2.        (4)

The second array is generally NOT interpolable across the first bridge.
It is only auxiliary data; its possible violations are accounted for below.

For epsilon_mix>0, combine both arrays into two-dimensional data

    X_i=(x_i^base,sqrt(epsilon_mix) x_i^aux),
    G_i=(g_i^base,sqrt(epsilon_mix) g_i^aux),
    F_i=f_i^base+epsilon_mix f_i^aux.

The GD equations hold coordinate by coordinate, all Q_(i,star) remain zero,
and X_n=G_n. Consequently

    V_mix=V_base+epsilon_mix V_aux.                 (5)

Here is a complete interpolation audit. Partition the samples into L (a
and its endpoint), M (b and its endpoint after the first bridge), and R (c
and its final endpoint after the second bridge). Overlaps are assigned to
the group whose gradient is used at the relevant bridge. The groups are
disjoint in original time: L includes the first bridge query, M includes
the second bridge query, and R includes the terminal sample.

* Within L all gradients in each scalar array agree, so Q=0.
* Within M union R the auxiliary data are one legal Huber trajectory;
  the base gradients agree, so all Q<=0 there.
* From L to M, the auxiliary target has y_j>=0 and the source gradient
  p_aux<1, so Q_aux<=0. From M to L, the auxiliary target y_i is a
  nonnegative multiple of p_aux<0 and 1-p_aux>0, so Q_aux<=0.
* From L to R, if R_j is the remaining sum of physical c steps after its
  target sample j, the two residuals are

      Q_base=-(1+eta_cut)eta_cut R_j,
      Q_aux=beta_inner(-p_aux-beta_inner)R_j.

* From R to L, if H_i is the remaining sum of physical a steps after its
  target sample i, then

      Q_base=-(1+eta_cut)H_i,
      Q_aux=(-beta_inner-p_aux)p_aux H_i.

Zero remaining sums give zero in both arrays. Thus duplicate positions
caused by zero steps do not require a separate limiting argument.

The optimum-outgoing inequalities hold too: Q_(star,i)=-<G_i,Y_i>,
and the inner product is nonnegative separately in each scalar array.
For the auxiliary L data this is H_i p_aux^2>=0, and in the other groups
it follows directly from their plateau coordinates. Q_(i,star)=0 already.

When r_L r_R<BC, one has -p_aux>beta_inner. The ONLY potentially positive
auxiliary residuals are L-to-R, and all interpolation inequalities hold for

    0<epsilon_mix<=epsilon_max,
    epsilon_max=eta_cut(1+eta_cut)
                    /[beta_inner(-p_aux-beta_inner)].             (6)

When r_L r_R>BC the ONLY potentially positive auxiliary residuals are
R-to-L, and all inequalities hold for

    0<epsilon_mix<=epsilon_max,
    epsilon_max=(1+eta_cut)
                    /[(-p_aux)(beta_inner+p_aux)].                 (7)

When r_L r_R=BC both cross families are nonpositive for any epsilon_mix.

All these are complete original-index interpolation checks, including both
cross directions and both optimum directions.

## 3. One fixed globally legal function

Include the zero optimum sample and define, on R^2,

    H_mix(v)=max_i { c_i+<X_i,v-G_i>+||v-G_i||^2/2 },
    c_i=<G_i,X_i>-F_i=||G_i||^2/2,
    f_mix(x)=sup_v { <x,v>-H_mix(v) }.

The maximum includes i=star. This is the explicit finite interpolation
construction of accepted fact 1252b8823bb5a290, proof section 2, applied to
the entire finite data set. The checked Q inequalities imply that f_mix
interpolates every (X_i,G_i,F_i), is convex differentiable and 1-smooth,
and has f_mix(0)=0=min f_mix. Thus the mixed data are one actual GD
trajectory of one fixed function; they are not an adaptive oracle or a
reset of the function at a bridge.

## 4. A universal quantitative two-cut necessary condition

Any primitive h as above necessarily satisfies

    r_L r_R [A^2+r_L(T_tot+A)] <= A^2 BC.           (8)

First assume r_L r_R<BC and choose epsilon_mix=epsilon_max in (6).
Using D_tail=B+C+r_R, substitution into (3)-(5) gives

    V_mix = (BC+r_R D_tail)(D_tail+r_L)
              /[D_tail^2 r_R(BC-r_L r_R)]
             * {A^2 BC-r_L r_R[A^2+r_L(T_tot+A)]}.

The prefactor is positive, proving necessity of (8) in this case. For an
algebra check, before the final factorization the numerator is

    (BC+r_R D_tail)
       {A^2[BC(D_tail+r_L)-r_L r_R D_tail]
                                     -T_tot^2 r_L^2 r_R}.

Use A^2 D_tail+T_tot^2 r_L
=(D_tail+r_L)[A^2+r_L(T_tot+A)] to obtain the displayed factorization.

If r_L r_R>BC, set z_aux=-p_aux=B/r_L and beta_inner>z_aux, and use (7).
Equation (5) becomes

    V_mix=(beta_inner+eta_cut z_aux)/(beta_inner-z_aux)
             * {A^2-T_tot^2[(1+eta_cut)beta_inner/z_aux-eta_cut]}.

The bracket is strictly smaller than A^2-T_tot^2<0. Hence no such schedule
is primitive. If r_L r_R=BC, V_aux=z_aux^2(A^2-T_tot^2)<0, so sufficiently
large epsilon_mix in the fully legal mixed data again gives V_mix<0.
This excludes the two remaining cases and completes (8).

No quadratic-balance assumption about a,b,c appears in this argument.

## 5. Saturated first cut forces its inner tail cut

Suppose in addition r_L T_tot=A D_tail. Then eta_cut=A/T_tot, V_base=0,
and A^2+r_L(T_tot+A)=A T_tot. Condition (8) reduces to

    T_tot r_L r_R<=ABC,
    equivalently r_R D_tail<=BC.                   (9)

Therefore a positive defect r_R D_tail>BC in the inner tail cannot be
repaired by any signed storage or full collection of interpolation edges
while keeping this outer cut exactly saturated. This statement concerns
primitive validity of this actual schedule, not all objective certificates.

The geometry also explains the conclusion without (8): at outer saturation
(4) becomes (T_tot/D_tail)^2[B^2-D_tail^2 beta_inner^2], which is strictly
negative exactly when the tail cut fails. Any sufficiently small positive
mixture allowed above then has V_mix<0.

## 6. Explicit refutation candidate for the outstanding eight-step seed

Set u=10/7, C=27/7, A=B=4, and

    D_tail=(165+sqrt(65641))/42,
    r_R=D_tail-55/7,
    T_tot=(4+D_tail+sqrt((4+D_tail)^2+16D_tail))/2,
    r_L=T_tot-4-D_tail.

The schedule is

    (3/2,3/2,1+r_L,3/2,3/2,1+r_R,10/7,10/7).

Directly from these root equations,

    r_R D_tail=196/9, r_L T_tot=4D_tail,
    BC=108/7<196/9.

Thus (9) fails. Here is a concrete mixing scale without an unspecified
smallness parameter. Define eta_cut=r_L/D_tail, beta_inner=r_R/C,
p_aux=-4/r_L, and

    epsilon_mix=eta_cut(1+eta_cut)
                      /[2 beta_inner(-p_aux-beta_inner)].          (10)

It is positive: the first defining polynomial has its positive root in
(10,101/10), and substituting 5/2 into
r_L^2+(4+D_tail)r_L-4D_tail shows r_L<5/2. Hence
r_L r_R<(5/2)(157/70)=157/28<108/7=BC. Formula (10) is half of (6).

For reference the two position arrays are

    base: (4,5/2,1,-r_L,-r_L+(3/2)eta_cut,
           -r_L+3eta_cut,-C eta_cut,-(1+u)eta_cut,-eta_cut),
    aux:  (4p_aux,(5/2)p_aux,p_aux,4,5/2,1,
           -r_R,-(1+u)beta_inner,-beta_inner).

The gradient arrays are

    base: (1,1,1,-eta_cut,-eta_cut,-eta_cut,
                           -eta_cut,-eta_cut,-eta_cut),
    aux:  (p_aux,p_aux,p_aux,1,1,1,
                           -beta_inner,-beta_inner,-beta_inner).

Values are g_i x_i-g_i^2/2 in each coordinate. Sections 2-3 show that
their mixed two-dimensional jets come from the explicit fixed f_mix.
Finally beta_inner D_tail=1372/243=4(343/243), so

    V_mix = epsilon_mix(T_tot/D_tail)^2
                  [16-(1372/243)^2]
          = -epsilon_mix(T_tot/D_tail)^2 * 937600/59049 < 0.        (11)

This gives an exact concrete primitive counterexample for the proposed
eight-step seed. It closes that seed's proposed primitive amplification.
It makes no claim that a different direct objective certificate is
impossible, and does not settle the unrestricted asymptotic target.

## Literature and verification status

Targeted Matlas and web retrieval supplied no theorem used in this proof.
The primitive/Huber regime had already been audited in previous rounds;
this continuation uses the accepted finite interpolation construction
rather than an external black box. All new results above remain candidates
pending a selected verifier allocation. No fact was submitted or written.

## 7. Retuning both bridges does not repair the fixed shortened-tail family

The obstruction is wider than the exact radical seed. For ANY nonnegative
blocks of capacities A=B=4 and C=27/7, every primitive schedule
(a,1+r_L,b,1+r_R,c) with r_L,r_R>0 has

    T_tot<81/5<9^rho, rho=log_2(1+sqrt(2)).         (12)

This rules out the required primitive amplification seed throughout the
two-parameter bridge family with these three physical blocks, without a
quadratic-product assumption and without requiring either cut to saturate.

Here is the entire scalar deduction. The first-cut Huber array from section2
gives T_tot r_L<=A(B+C+r_R). The same construction at the second bridge
(start on its positive plateau at A+B+r_L, negative slope r_R/C) gives
T_tot r_R<=C(A+B+r_L). For a fixed T=T_tot set

    R=T-A-B-C,
    U(T)=A(T-A)/(T+A), S(T)=C(T-C)/(T+C),
    L(T)=R-S(T).

Both cuts force L(T)<=r_L<=U(T), with r_R=R-r_L. At
T0=81/5 these endpoints and their complementary r_R values are

    r_L=L(T0)=128/65,  r_R=S(T0)=216/91,
    r_L=U(T0)=244/101, r_R=R-U(T0)=6812/3535.

Let Phi_T(z)=z(R-z)[A^2+z(T+A)]. Its derivative is

    Phi_T'(z)=A^2 R+2[(T+A)R-A^2]z-3(T+A)z^2.

Since R>0, this quadratic has one negative and one positive root. Thus
Phi_T increases and then decreases on (0,R); its minimum on any closed
subinterval is attained at an endpoint. The two exact endpoint excesses
over the necessary bound A^2 BC in (8), at T0, are

    Phi_T0(L(T0))-A^2 BC=26650944/1922375>0,
    Phi_T0(U(T0))-A^2 BC=97846272/1785175>0.

For T>=T0, U and S increase, while their derivatives
2A^2/(T+A)^2 and 2C^2/(T+C)^2 lie in (0,1). Hence L=R-S and R-U
also increase. Each endpoint product z(R-z)[A^2+z(T+A)] strictly increases,
because all its positive factors increase. If the cut interval is empty,
the cuts already fail; otherwise both endpoint excesses stay positive and
Phi_T(z)>A^2 BC throughout the interval. This contradicts (8) and proves
T<T0.

For the last inequality in (12), let lambda=1+sqrt(2), so 2^rho=lambda.
One has 9^6-2^19=7153>0 and

    lambda^19=9369319+6625109 sqrt(2)
       >9369319+(7/5)6625109
       =(81/5)^6+8890332269/15625.

Therefore 9^(6rho)>lambda^19>(81/5)^6 and (12) follows.

All endpoint arithmetic here is exact rational arithmetic for two displayed
values. It is not a reopened schedule search or numerical PEP screen.

# A static inward multiscale staircase on the original small-tau ruler

Project: gd-exact-profile-20260926. Worker: max3. Allocated round: 59.
Controlling guidance: c2181daa6b96056c. Frozen policy: 117f41cdec34f009.

**Status: complete JOINT UNVERIFIED proof candidate. No fact_id.**
The assignment permits one analytical round and forbids formal submissions.
Every new calculation below is part of this single joint proof attempt;
none is an accepted intermediate result. The original exact minimax problem
remains OPEN. No computation, trajectory evaluation, recurrence execution,
solver, mathematical replay, consult, or subagent was used.

The candidate is one one-dimensional static function with m-1 distinct
positive gradient magnitudes and m-1 actual ordinary-block shelf visits.
Its derivative is continuous and piecewise affine, with slopes only zero
and one. The number of spatial layers grows with m. All original steps
are retained, the minimum is attained uniquely at zero, and the closest
initial distance is calculated exactly.

This chosen mechanism fails to improve the same-word Huber payoff. For
every integer m>=8, its actual normalized terminal payoff V_m satisfies

    0 < V_m/H_base < 2*(25/49)^(m-3) < 1,
    H_base = 1/(1+2*S_m).                                      (A)

More generally, the same bound holds for the precise inward, disjoint,
constant-gradient block class defined in Section 8. That statement allows
arbitrary legal connectors and inner function values. It is NOT a bound
on all one-dimensional functions, overlapping returns, trajectories with
varying gradients inside those blocks, or the unrestricted W_n.

The exact constructed payoff is

    V_m = (1+2*S_(m-1))/A_0^2,                               (B)

where A_0 is the explicit backward-defined starting radius below. Formula
(B), the entire integrated function, and the bound (A) are all established
jointly. The proof does not end with an uncontrolled switching recurrence.

## 1. Recalled scopes and source discipline

The following shared drafts are awareness, not proof premises.

* 69b90f738a77a91b excludes all squared distances to an interval on its
  original tau=1 word, and one specified inward-return initialization of
  a fixed one-sided Huber function. It explicitly exhibits another start
  of that same function with Huber-scale payoff. Its fixed branch is not
  being retuned here.
* 1a23bf9f1a7799fd concerns a common-ell log-periodic staircase on the
  different c_sc=13/5, a_sc=c_sc^(-17), no-plus-one word. Independent ray
  phases do not remove its common-ell restriction.
* 3d2994746c1d983e allows arbitrary profiles on that different word only
  when all nonzero queried secants on each ray fit a factor-13/5 band.
* e08ee0674ce368d3 concerns the original tau=1 word but imposes a common
  reciprocal/small-curvature core, subpower derivative growth, and stated
  ray-comparison bounds. The outer unit ramps constructed below are not
  subpower ramps: on such a ramp r*Q'(r)/Q(r)>1.

Accepted fact 1fca3e5dd555cb4a constructs outward alternating-ray shelf
extensions and pays retained offsets and areas. Its extension rule is
not the inward nesting rule here, so its recurrence is not imported.
Accepted fact 1252b8823bb5a290 fixes the full directed interpolation and
W=2R conventions. We prove those inequalities directly for the function.
Accepted fact 04219554e41d9750 supplies only the unconditional ORIGINAL
benchmark definitions and positive finite ray limit used in Section 9.
No conditional seed-existence assertion is assumed.

Matlas was queried for the multiscale scalar construction mechanism. Its
2303.03534 small-step convergence result and 2306.07098 graph-learning
results were inapplicable. A primary-source web search located Altschuler
and Parrilo, *Acceleration by Stepsize Hedging II: Silver Stepsize Schedule
for Smooth Convex Optimization*, arXiv:2309.16530v1 (2023). Its exact HTML
and extracted text were saved in downloads/; Section 2, the opening of
Section 3, and Section 3.2 were read. The schedule and normalization differ
from ours, and its Silver gluing certificate is not applied to the perturbed
word. This was background on physical indexing and full pair consistency,
not an external theorem premise. All needed scalar arguments follow below.

## 2. The unchanged physical word and elementary inequalities

Fix an integer m>=8 and use exactly

    tau=1/4, lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta_word=1-1/(4*m),
    c=lambda*(1+1/(4*m)), a=beta_word/c,
    d_j=a*c^j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    S_j=sum H_j, n=2^m-1.

The notation d_j means a bridge EXCESS, so the physical step is 1+d_j.
Every occurrence of H_j retains this SAME enclosing m and tau.
The recurrence for the actual mass is

    S_0=0, S_(j+1)=2*S_j+1+d_j.                           (2.1)

Useful strict bounds are

    2<c<5/2, 0<a<1/2, S_j>d_j for 1<=j<=m.             (2.2)

For c<5/2, note that sqrt(2)<47/33, since 2*33^2<47^2.
Thus lambda<80/33, and c<=33*lambda/32<5/2. The other
parameter bounds follow from lambda>2 and beta_word<1.
At j=1, S_1=1+a>d_1=beta_word. If S_j>d_j then

    S_(j+1)-d_(j+1)
      =2*(S_j-d_j)+1+(3-c)*d_j >0,

which proves the last inequality in (2.2).

Select precisely the first occurrences of levels 2,3,...,m-1. Put

    k=m-2,
    b_i=1+d_(i+2), 0<=i<k,
    s_0=S_2,
    s_i=S_(i+1), 1<=i<=k.

The word, without omissions, is then

    (H_2,b_0,H_2,b_1,H_3,b_2,...,H_(m-2),b_(k-1),H_(m-1)). (2.3)

This follows by successively expanding the LEFT child of H_m. The
selected checkpoint b_i is at physical zero-based index 2^(i+2)-1.
Stage 0 begins at index zero; stage i>=1 begins at index 2^(i+1).
The first block has length 3, each subsequent block i has length
2^(i+1)-1, and the complete horizon is exactly 2^m-1.

For 0<=i<=k-2 define D_i=b_i-1=d_(i+2); also set
D_(k-1)=d_(m-1). Then

    D_(i+1)=c*D_i, s_(i+1)=S_(i+2)>D_i.                 (2.4)

Write s=s_k=S_(m-1), D=D_(k-1)=d_(m-1), and T=1+2*s.
By (2.1)--(2.2),

    S_m=2*s+1+D, D<s,
    1+2*S_m < 3*(1+2*s)=3*T,
    1+S_2=4+2*a+beta_word>4.                            (2.5)

## 3. The frozen backward knot policy

All scalars in this section are defined before GD is run. They are exact
finite mathematical recurrences, not numerically evaluated recurrences.

Set

    q_k=1, A_k=T,
    q_(k-1)=A_k/D_(k-1),
    A_(k-1)=(1+s_(k-1))*q_(k-1).                        (3.1)

For i=k-2,k-3,...,0, define

    q_i=(A_(i+1)+A_(i+2)-q_(i+2))/D_i,
    A_i=(1+s_i)*q_i+A_(i+2)-q_(i+2).                    (3.2)

The symbols q_i and A_i will be the gradient magnitude and starting
radius of stage i. Put

    R_i=A_i-s_i*q_i, 0<=i<=k.                           (3.3)

We prove that these definitions give ordered legal knots, rather than
assuming that they describe a realizable switching sequence.

First, for every nonterminal stage the definitions imply

    A_i+A_(i+1)=(b_i+s_i)*q_i.                          (3.4)

For i=k-1 this is (3.1) because A_k=D_(k-1)*q_(k-1).
For earlier i, substitute (3.2) and D_i=b_i-1.

Second, backward induction gives

    A_i>=q_i>0 for every i,
    q_(k-1)>q_k,
    q_i>(c+1)*q_(i+1) for 0<=i<=k-2.                   (3.5)

The last base ratio is q_(k-1)=T/D>1 because s>D.
The two last A_i are at least their q_i. If the later claims hold,
use (3.4) at i+1 in the first line of (3.2) to obtain

    D_i*q_i=(b_(i+1)+s_(i+1))*q_(i+1)-q_(i+2)
            >(D_(i+1)+s_(i+1))*q_(i+1)
            >(c+1)*D_i*q_(i+1).                        (3.6)

The first strict inequality uses q_(i+1)>q_(i+2); the second uses
(2.4). Also A_(i+2)-q_(i+2)>=0, so (3.2) gives A_i>=q_i.
This proves all statements by backward induction.

For the innermost two stages,

    R_(k-1)=q_(k-1), R_k=1+s>q_k.

For every i<=k-2, (3.2)--(3.3) give

    R_i=A_(i+2)+q_i-q_(i+2)>A_(i+2),
    A_i>R_i>=q_i.                                      (3.7)

Thus, on each ray, every new inner shelf lies strictly inside the inner
endpoint of the previous outer shelf. Each connector has exactly its
gradient change as its length. This is the shortest connector permitted
by 1-Lipschitz continuity for these particular endpoint data; it is not
asserted to optimize the entire class of scalar functions.

## 4. One globally defined static gradient

Stage i uses ray sign (-1)^i. For each parity sigma in {0,1}, let ell_sigma
be the largest i<=k with that parity. Thus the two innermost indices are
k and k-1. Construct a radial derivative Q_sigma on r>=0 as follows.

For the innermost index ell=ell_sigma set

    Q_sigma(r)=r                  on 0<=r<=q_ell,
    Q_sigma(r)=q_ell              on q_ell<=r<=A_ell.

For i=ell-2,ell-4,...,sigma extend it outward by

    Q_sigma(r)=q_(i+2)+(r-A_(i+2))
                          on A_(i+2)<=r<=R_i,
    Q_sigma(r)=q_i         on R_i<=r<=A_i.               (4.1)

Beyond the outermost A_sigma keep Q_sigma equal to q_sigma. Equations
(3.5)--(3.7) prove that all intervals are correctly ordered, nonempty,
cover the ray with no gap, and agree at their endpoints. Every ramp in
(4.1) has slope one, and every shelf has slope zero. There are m-1
distinct positive shelf magnitudes in total, and m-1 nonempty ramps.

Define the single derivative and its integrated function on all of R by

    g(x)=Q_0(x) if x>=0, and g(x)=-Q_1(-x) if x<=0,
    F(x)=integral_0^x g(t) dt.                           (4.2)

This g is continuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz, since its ordered
affine pieces have slopes in {0,1}. At zero both formulas agree. It
follows directly that F is convex, differentiable, and 1-smooth with
F'=g. The gradient has slope one on a neighborhood of zero, so its
optimal global Lipschitz constant is exactly one.

Each Q_sigma is strictly positive for r>0. Consequently the ENTIRE
minimum set is {0}, and F(0)=min F=0. The prescribed initial point
x_0=A_0 has exact closest distance A_0 and squared distance A_0^2.
For any advertised L>0, use f=L*F with physical steps h_t/L. The
positions are identical and the normalized payoff is unchanged.

## 5. Integrated values, including every retained inner area

For each shelf define its ramp endpoint U_i by

    U_k=q_k=1,
    U_(k-1)=q_(k-1),
    U_i=R_i for i<=k-2.

Put l_i=A_i-U_i and define the retained area

    E_in_i=sum_(j>i, j<=k, j congruent i mod 2) q_j*l_j.  (5.1)

All sums are finite. The innermost E_in_k and E_in_(k-1) are zero.
For i<k, l_i=s_i*q_i; the final shelf instead has l_k=2*s.
The full radial value on ANY shelf is

    F((-1)^i*r)=q_i^2/2+E_in_i+q_i*(r-U_i),
                         U_i<=r<=A_i.                  (5.2)

For an outer ramp in (4.1), its full value is

    F((-1)^i*r)=F((-1)^i*A_(i+2))
       +q_(i+2)*(r-A_(i+2))+(r-A_(i+2))^2/2.            (5.3)

To verify these formulas, start with F=r^2/2 in each unit core. An
inner shelf contributes precisely q_j*l_j. Integrating the next unit
ramp contributes (q_i^2-q_(i+2)^2)/2, so the earlier shelf areas
remain as E_in_i. This proves (5.1)--(5.3) successively on each ray.
The outer constant continuation has the matching linear continuation
of (5.2). Therefore every value comes from the SAME integral (4.2).

At a later inner stage, the outer areas are not erased. They remain
part of F at outer points and cease contributing to F at smaller radii
because those outer intervals lie outside the integration segment
from zero. This distinction is the geometric reason inward movement
can remove terminal area while still paying its initial-distance cost.

There is also an explicit check against an illegal value reset. For
any i<=k-2, E_in_i>0. At the two actual queries R_i and A_i the gradient
is q_i. Their correct values differ by q_i*(A_i-R_i). Reset only the
value at R_i to q_i^2/2 while retaining the value at A_i. The directed
interpolation residual from A_i to R_i becomes exactly E_in_i>0.
Thus resetting a new core is not an alternative reading of this law.

## 6. Every physical GD update, all crossings, and spatial order

Let P_i(r) denote the sum of the first r physical steps of block i in
(2.3), including P_i(0)=0. Every value lies in [0,s_i], with endpoints
zero and s_i. At every query in
that block, and at its endpoint before a checkpoint or termination,
the actual iterate is

    x=(-1)^i*(A_i-q_i*P_i(r)).                           (6.1)

For i<k the bracket lies in [R_i,A_i], and for i=k it lies in
[1+s,T], a subset of [U_k,A_k]. Hence the actual gradient there is
(-1)^i*q_i. Every individual step h in the block therefore changes
(6.1) by exactly -h*g(x). This verifies all its steps, including its
unselected large bridges. Replacing a block by its sum was used only
after proving that EVERY partial sum stays in the same actual shelf.

At selected checkpoint i the incoming point is (-1)^i*R_i. Identity
(3.4) gives R_i=b_i*q_i-A_(i+1), so the actual update is

    (-1)^i*R_i-b_i*(-1)^i*q_i
       =(-1)^(i+1)*A_(i+1).                            (6.2)

This proves each full crossing. The derivative at the departure is
fixed by the old shelf; the landing point is on the already defined
opposite-ray shelf. Every intermediate spatial knot crossed by the
jump belongs to (4.1)--(4.2). A GD jump uses the gradient at departure,
and does not integrate or change gradients along the jump. No new
objective, knot, offset, or step is inserted at a crossing.

Starting from x_0=A_0, (6.1)--(6.2) prove the entire original H_m
trajectory by induction through its actual blocks. The terminal point is

    x_n=(-1)^k*(1+s).                                   (6.3)

The complete order of sampled radii is also fixed. In each block they
decrease strictly because every physical step is positive. On the same
ray, all samples of stage i are outside every sample of stage i+2,
since R_i>A_(i+2). Opposite rays are separated by zero. There are no
coincident query points or zero landings in this construction. Were the
same point queried twice, (4.2) would of course fix the same gradient
and value; the proof makes no freedom out of repeated queries.

## 7. All ordered interpolation rows and actual Gram positivity

Here is a direct proof for all real points, not just adjacent samples.
For u<v put delta=g(v)-g(u)>=0. Lipschitz continuity gives
delta<=v-u. Monotonicity and the same Lipschitz bound imply

    g(t)-g(u)>=max(0,delta-(v-t)),
    g(v)-g(t)>=max(0,delta-(t-u)),  u<=t<=v.

Integrate both inequalities over [u,v] to get

    F(v)-F(u)-g(u)*(v-u)>=delta^2/2,
    F(u)-F(v)-g(v)*(u-v)>=delta^2/2.                    (7.1)

Thus, for every ordered pair of actual queries, the terminal point,
or the actual optimum, in the project's convention,

    Q_ij=F(x_i)-F(x_j)+g(x_i)*(x_j-x_i)
                           +(g(x_i)-g(x_j))^2/2 <=0.   (7.2)

Equal points give equality. All remote rows and both orientations
follow from (7.1). For f=L*F the corresponding term is divided by L
in the usual way. The gradients and positions are actual real numbers,
so every required Gram matrix is an outer-product Gram matrix and is
positive semidefinite. This is not a numerically perturbed PSD claim.

## 8. Exact payoff and a full scoped inward-shelf obstruction

The terminal point (6.3) is on the innermost shelf of its ray. By the
integrated formula (5.2),

    F(x_n)=s+1/2=T/2,
    V_m=2*F(x_n)/A_0^2=T/A_0^2>0.                     (8.1)

We now prove the bound for a wider, explicitly delimited class. This
is part of the same joint proof, not an invocation of an unverified lemma.

Consider ANY single static convex differentiable 1-smooth scalar F
with an attained minimum, translated and reflected so that zero is
the closest minimizer to its positive initial point A_0. Use exactly
the blocks and selected checkpoints in (2.3). Suppose there are
positive A_i,q_i such that block i lies on the constant-gradient
shelf [R_i,A_i] on ray (-1)^i, including the terminal point, where
R_i=A_i-s_i*q_i>0. Suppose every selected checkpoint lands on the
next opposite-ray shelf start, and require precisely the inward order

    A_(i+2)<=R_i for 0<=i<=k-2.                         (8.2)

No connector is prescribed, and the gradient may be arbitrary outside
those shelves. No adjacent-gradient comparison is assumed. A minimum
interval extending to the left of zero is allowed; zero must remain
the closest minimizer to the positive initial point, so the denominator
is still A_0^2.

Static monotonicity and 1-Lipschitz continuity on each ray give

    q_i>=q_(i+2),
    R_i-A_(i+2)>=q_i-q_(i+2).                           (8.3)

The actual checkpoint identity is still (3.4). Combining it with
(8.3), rather than treating gradients as free controls, yields

    D_i*q_i >= A_(i+1)+A_(i+2)-q_(i+2)
             =(b_(i+1)+s_(i+1))*q_(i+1)-q_(i+2).        (8.4)

At any sampled radius r, the magnitude of the gradient is at most r,
because g(0)=0 and g is 1-Lipschitz. In particular R_i>=q_i.
Put z=A_k/q_k. Then R_k>=q_k gives z>=s+1. Also the last
checkpoint and R_(k-1)>=q_(k-1) give

    q_(k-1)>=A_k/D = z*q_k/D > q_k,                    (8.5)

where z>=s+1>D. Backward induction in (8.4), using (2.4), now proves

    q_i>(c+1)*q_(i+1) for every i<=k-2.                 (8.6)

Indeed, once q_(i+1)>q_(i+2), its right side is strictly greater
than (D_(i+1)+s_(i+1))*q_(i+1)>(c+1)*D_i*q_(i+1).
Thus the adjacent decrease is a CONSEQUENCE of full same-ray history.
It was not assumed or supplied by a freely switching curvature model.

Since A_0>= (1+s_0)*q_0, (8.5)--(8.6) imply

    A_0 > (1+S_2)*(c+1)^(k-1)*z*q_k/D.                (8.7)

Let y=A_k-s*q_k=(z-s)*q_k be the terminal radius. On the whole
integration interval from zero to that point, the radial derivative
is at most min(r,q_k): it is nondecreasing, equals q_k at y, and
is 1-Lipschitz from zero. Therefore

    2*(F(x_n)-min F)<=2*q_k*y-q_k^2
                         =(2*z-2*s-1)*q_k^2.           (8.8)

This bound explicitly allows arbitrary retained inner areas. It does
not set F(x_n) to the core value q_k^2/2. If the minimizer is an
interval, integrating from zero still gives exactly the minimum
normalization; any flat part only reduces the same upper integral.

For T=1+2*s and z>=s+1,

    (2*z-T)/z^2 <= 1/T,

because z^2-T*(2*z-T)=(z-T)^2>=0. Combining this identity with
(8.7)--(8.8) gives the full terminal bound

    V < D^2 / [(1+S_2)^2*(c+1)^(2*(k-1))*T].           (8.9)

It holds for the whole stated inward-shelf class. The frozen choice
z=T in (3.1) maximizes this scalar terminal ratio, but is not asserted
to maximize the actual payoff over all profiles.

Finally divide (8.9) by H_base and apply (2.5), k=m-2, and
D=a*c^(m-1). This gives

    V/H_base
      < [3*a^2*c^4/(1+S_2)^2]
                         *[c/(c+1)]^(2*(m-3))
      < 2*(25/49)^(m-3).                               (8.10)

The last inequalities are exact: a<1/2, c<5/2, and 1+S_2>4 imply
the leading factor is below 1875/1024<2; also c/(c+1)<5/7.
For m>=8, 25/49<2/3 gives
2*(25/49)^(m-3)<=2*(25/49)^5<64/243<1.

Our frozen law satisfies all the class hypotheses by Sections 3--6.
Consequently (8.1) and (8.10) establish (A)--(B), including actual
full-horizon error, the exact closest radius, and every history constraint.

## 9. The ORIGINAL all-split benchmark comparison

Use exactly the accepted definitions in fact 04219554e41d9750:

    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4*u*v))/2,
    G(u,v)=2*u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8*u*v)/2,
    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=r<n) Omega(C_r,C_(n-r-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=r<n) G(C_r,B_(n-r-1)),
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

Both maxima retain EVERY original integer split. That fact's
unconditional Section 7 gives

    B_(2^m-1)<=Z_1*lambda^m,
    Z_1=lim_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m in (0,infinity).

Here beta_word>=31/32 and c<5/2 give a>31/80. Since S_m>D,

    B_n*H_base < Z_1*lambda^m/(2*a*c^(m-1))
                 =Z_1*c/(2*a)*(lambda/c)^m
                 < (100/31)*Z_1.

Combining with (8.10), EVERY trajectory in the precise class of
Section 8 has

    0<=B_n*V<(200/31)*Z_1*(25/49)^(m-3) ->0.            (9.1)

In particular the chosen static family cannot attain 1/B_n at large
depth, or exceed H_base by any fixed factor. This is a result about
its actual payoff, not the weakness of a merely lower certificate.
No substituted balanced benchmark, benchmark DP, or evaluated ray
constant appears in the argument.

For comparison, H_base itself is attained on this exact word by the
single usual Huber derivative g_H(x)=min(x,1), started at 1+2*S_m.
All queries then lie above one, the last value is S_m+1/2, and the
true squared radius is (1+2*S_m)^2. This explicit check establishes
that (8.10) compares against an actual same-word witness.

## 10. What this does and does not settle

This round supplies a new depth-growing scalar knot policy, its full
static objective, global legality, integrated values, original physical
trajectory, exact minimum and closest radius, exact terminal payoff,
and a uniform quantitative obstruction for its entire stated inward
constant-gradient block mechanism. The old constant-gradient cells
cost distance through (8.3)--(8.7), even though their outer areas no
longer contribute at the eventual inner terminal point.

The function is not a fixed two-curvature homogeneous law or a
one-shelf law. Every added depth introduces another nonempty ramp and
shelf, and the actual trajectory queries all m-1 different magnitudes.
The knot spacings depend on the whole predetermined word. No fixed
start of 69b90f738a77a91b was retuned.

The obstruction requires the exact decomposition (2.3), constant
gradient on each closed physical block path, and disjoint inward
same-ray order (8.2). It does not cover overlapping same-ray returns,
mixed outward/inward records, or gradient variation within these
blocks. Those are explicit ways to escape its hypotheses, not a
claim that an escaping construction has useful payoff. Other starts
of the same constructed function need not satisfy these hypotheses
and are not excluded. Other checkpoint selections are not covered.

No statement here is an upper bound on W_n, on all scalar functions,
or on all possible m-dependent piecewise-affine gradients. A lower
witness on H_m would itself still leave the original all-word,
all-horizon minimax target. That target remains OPEN.

## 11. Local glossary and resources

The following symbols are local aliases, with their full definitions above.

* H_j,S_j: original enclosing-m word and its physical mass.
* d_j: level-j bridge excess a*c^j; 1+d_j is the physical step.
* k,b_i,s_i,D_i: number of selected checkpoints, their physical steps,
  intervening actual block masses, and b_i-1.
* s,D,T: S_(m-1), d_(m-1), and 1+2*s.
* q_i,A_i,R_i: stage gradient magnitude, starting radius, and radius
  after its whole ordinary block.
* Q_sigma: complete radial derivative on ray sign (-1)^sigma.
* U_i,l_i,E_in_i: actual ramp endpoint, shelf length, and retained inner
  shelf area on stage i's ray.
* g,F: single static derivative and its integral with minimum zero.
* P_i(r): mass of the first r original physical steps of block i.
* V,V_m,H_base: actual twice-gap/true-radius-squared payoff, its frozen
  value, and the exact same-word Huber payoff. Smoothness is one.
* z,y: terminal shelf starting radius divided by q_k, and terminal radius.
* Q_ij: project's signed directed smooth-convex interpolation residual.
* B_n,C_n,Z_1: ORIGINAL accepted all-split benchmark quantities.

One policy was frozen, and no repair was made. Resource counts:
mathematical programs 0; numerical evaluations 0; arithmetic replays 0;
executed recurrences 0; solvers 0; benchmark DP 0; fact_submit 0;
new accepted facts 0; installations 0; consults 0; native subagents 0.
Only primary retrieval, text/data reads and writes, and administrative
metadata checks were performed. Initial broad recall displays were
truncated; necessary statements and scope passages were reread in
bounded extracts. No numerical or formal allocation was inferred.

All new files lie in the assigned absolute public directory. Existing
private logs may be appended, but no new private artifact is created.
This is the handoff for the one authorized round; it does not renew
the completed59/attempted60 ceiling and does not authorize a formal
gate, publication, finalization, revocation, or shutdown.

# Relaxed objective certificates: a full four-fan null-template obstruction

Status: complete self-contained UNVERIFIED candidate. No solver and no
fact_submit. This is a formal certificate-family obstruction, not a legal
lower witness and not a resolution of the original minimax problem.

The latest guidance asks for q above the Huber value to be tested separately.
This argument does not use Huber complementarity or a Huber kernel condition.
The shared C53 calculation (694fb2dacb4f7389) suggested its null template;
every identity and estimate needed here is proved again, with additional
left-history fans, arbitrary child scaling, and the relaxed objective.
Only fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies a mathematical predecessor: the
normalized smooth-convex interpolation inequalities.

## 1. Full objective identity and a relaxed proof-weight choice

Normalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Let

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0,
    C_ij=-2Q_ji
        =2(f_i-f_j)+2<g_j,x_j-x_i>-||g_i-g_j||^2 >=0,

and put C_ii=0. All positions use the exact physical GD recursion.
Every formal vector x_0,g_0,...,g_n is independent.

At a block h of length n put S=sum h_i, M=S+1, P=sum h_i g_i, and fix

    w_i=h_i for i<n, w_n=1, D=sum w_i=M,
    W=P+g_n,
    J_rel=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)
                   +sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2.    (1)

The same quantity can be written

    J_rel=2sum_i w_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_i w_i||g_i||^2
       +2sum_i w_i<g_i,x_0-x_i>-||W||^2.

To check this, expand ||sum h_i g_i||^2 and use
2sum_i h_i<g_i,sum_(t<i)h_t g_t>
=||sum h_i g_i||^2-sum_i h_i^2||g_i||^2.

Suppose finite nonnegative coefficients lambda_ij give the exact identity

    J_rel=sum_(finite i!=j)lambda_ij C_ij+R.          (2)

All function coefficients cancel if and only if

    out_i-in_i=w_i-M 1_(i=n).                       (3)

For EVERY real q the corresponding FULL objective polynomial is

    q||x_0||^2-2f_n
       +2/M sum_i w_i Q_(i,star)
       +2/M sum_(finite i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ji
      =(q-1/M)||x_0||^2+[||x_0-W||^2+R]/M.          (4)

This follows by expanding the optimum interpolation terms. All actual
multipliers are nonnegative. The array fixes Q_(star,i) multipliers to
zero; general relaxed certificates need not have that property.
If R were PSD, (4) at q=1/M would be the requested weaker objective
upper bound. This is genuinely above the Huber value when S>0.

## 2. The actual four-fan recursion, with signed corrections allowed

Use two copies of the same block. Let p be the left endpoint and q_end
the parent endpoint. Insert bridge b=1+d with d>0, and write

    g=g_p, e=g_(q_end), M'=2M+d,
    P_L=x_start-x_p, P_R=x_(p+1)-x_(q_end),
    W_L=P_L+g, W_R=P_R+e,
    V=d g+W_R, W_parent=W_L+V.

Choose level-dependent A,E>=0 and real B,C,p0,q0. Set

    gamma_L=1+C+E, gamma_R=1+A+B,
    p0-q0=(1-A+E)M+d.                              (5)

Require gamma_L,gamma_R>=0. Scale the COMPLETE left and right child
finite arrays by gamma_L and gamma_R. Add the fans

    C w_i on (p,i),       E w_i on (q_end,i),   i in left samples,
    A w_i on (p,i),       B w_i on (q_end,i),   i in right samples,

omitting self edges, and add p0 on (p,q_end) and q0 on (q_end,p).
Require EVERY FINAL finite multiplier nonnegative. In particular B,C,
p0,q0 can be signed when their actual resulting entries are nonnegative.
The new proof weights are the old physical weights in each queried
copy, with the left terminal weight changed from 1 to b, and the
right terminal weight retained as 1. Thus D_parent=M', exactly as in (1).

No earlier interpolation constraints are deleted. The final array is
the explicit sum just given. Its value flow is (3); alternatively the
following expansion checks every value coefficient directly.

The four weighted fan identities, derived by expanding C, are

    sum_L w_i C_(p,i)
       =-J_L-||W_L||^2+2<W_L,P_L+g>-M||g||^2,
    sum_L w_i C_(q_end,i)
       =-J_L-||W_L||^2+2<W_L,P_L+b g+P_R+e>
                                     -M||e||^2-2M(f_p-f_(q_end)),
    sum_R w_i C_(p,i)
       =2M(f_p-f_(q_end))-J_R-||W_R||^2
                                     -2d<g,W_R>-M||g||^2,
    sum_R w_i C_(q_end,i)
       =-J_R-||W_R||^2+2<W_R,P_R+e>-M||e||^2.       (6)

After adding the child identities and these fans, the only remaining
function coefficient is
2[(1-A+E)M+d-p0+q0](f_p-f_(q_end)), which is zero by (5).
Substitute P_L=W_L-g and P_R=W_R-e in the quadratic terms. The exact
FULL gradient remainder is

    R_parent=gamma_L R_L+gamma_R R_R+F_rel,

    F_rel=-(C+E)||W_L||^2+(A-B)||W_R||^2
       -2E<W_L,W_R+d g>
       +2(Ad-q0)<g,W_R>+2p0<W_R,e>
       +(gamma_L M-d^2-2dq0)||g||^2
       +(gamma_R M-2M-d)||e||^2+2p0d<g,e>.          (7)

This retains both physical displacements through the displayed W_L,W_R
and retains every child matrix. It is not a local-PSD assumption.

## 3. Exact undeformed calibration

Let lambda=1+sqrt(2). For the undeformed Silver word, M=lambda^j and
d=lambda^(j-1) at level j. Choose

    A=B=lambda, C=E=0, p0=0, q0=lambda d,
    gamma_L=1, gamma_R=lambda^2.                    (8)

Start with the one-step finite array lambda_(0,1)=lambda,
lambda_(1,0)=1, physical step sqrt(2). Direct expansion gives
R=M(M-1)||g_end||^2 at this seed. Equation (5) holds in (8) because
(1-lambda)M+d=-lambda d. All final coefficients are nonnegative.

Assuming the same remainder formula for both children, (7) proves it
for the parent. The g coefficient from the left child cancels
M-lambda^2 d^2=M-M^2. The e coefficient is
lambda^2 M(M-1)+lambda^2 M-M'=M'^2-M'. All mixed coefficients are
zero. Thus the relaxed identity passes the FULL undeformed benchmark
at every depth, with its true initial distance and all values canceled.

## 4. A formal template for EVERY allowed choice of the new fans

For the deformed word H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j), let
M_j=1+sum H_j. Define signed numbers

    kappa_1=M_1=2+a,
    kappa_(j+1)=-kappa_j^2/d_j.                     (9)

Every array produced by (5)--(7) has a recursively specified scalar
formal-gradient template with terminal gradient e such that

    W_j=kappa_j e,
    R_j=(kappa_j^2-M_j)e^2.                         (10)

At the one-step seed, take both formal gradients equal to e. The two
finite C residuals are zero after setting f_0-f_1=h_0 e^2. Equation
(1) gives R_1=M_1(M_1-1)e^2=(kappa_1^2-M_1)e^2 for ANY seed array
satisfying (3). PSD of the seed is not needed for this calculation.

For the induction use a right template with terminal e and a left
template with terminal g=-kappa_j e/d_j. Then

    V=d_j g+W_R=0,
    W_parent=W_L+V=kappa_j g=kappa_(j+1)e.

On V=0, the COMPLETE local expression (7) becomes

    F_rel=-(gamma_L-1)||W_L||^2
          +(gamma_L M_j-gamma_R d_j^2)||g||^2
          +(gamma_R M_j-M_(j+1))||e||^2.            (11)

Indeed W_R=-d_j g cancels both p0 mixed terms and the E term. Adding
gamma_L R_L+gamma_R R_R and W_L=kappa_j g cancels gamma_L, gamma_R
and M_j, leaving (kappa_j^4/d_j^2-M_(j+1))e^2. This proves (10).
No child PSD, attained zero, greedy storage selection, or saturation
assumption occurs anywhere in this induction.

Now insert (10) into the FULL initial-distance identity (4). After
multiplication by M_j its restriction to independent scalar x_0,e is

             [ M_j q       -kappa_j     ]
             [ -kappa_j  2kappa_j^2-M_j ].           (12)

If 2kappa_j^2<M_j this matrix is negative at x_0=0,e=1 for EVERY q.
If the lower diagonal is positive, PSD would force

    q>=kappa_j^2/[M_j(2kappa_j^2-M_j)]>=1/kappa_j^2.

The last comparison is exactly (kappa_j^2-M_j)^2>=0. Thus merely
increasing q cannot repair the late negative diagonal.

## 5. Explicit failure at the actual critical horizon

Put m>=128, epsilon=1/m, c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c and
d_j=a c^j=(1-epsilon)c^(j-1). All parameters are fixed within this
whole word. Solving (9) in absolute value gives

    |kappa_m|=(1-epsilon)c^m Rcrit^(2^(m-1)),
    Rcrit=(2+a)/[(1-epsilon)c].                     (13)

The formula holds at j=1 by definition, and squaring and dividing by
d_j proves the next term. Direct subtraction gives

    1-Rcrit=epsilon[2-lambda^2 epsilon(1+epsilon)]
                    /[lambda^2(1-epsilon)(1+epsilon)^2]
             >=epsilon/4.                         (14)

For the inequality, lambda^2<6, the numerator bracket exceeds 7/4,
and the denominator is less than 6(129/128)^2<25/4. Their ratio is
therefore greater than 7/25>1/4. Also 0<Rcrit<1.
Since 1-t<=exp(-t) and c<3,

    |kappa_m|<3^m exp[-2^(m-3)/m]
               <=(3/exp(2))^m<2^(-m).              (15)

Here 2^(m-4)>=m^2 holds at m=128 and propagates by induction because
2m^2>=(m+1)^2 for m>=3. Also exp(2)>6 follows from the four positive
series terms 1+2+2+4/3. Since M_m>=1, (15) implies
2kappa_m^2<M_m. Equation (12) is therefore a concrete negative FULL
matrix restriction at the actual target horizon, for every real q.

This excludes all arrays in the stated relaxed four-fan family,
including arbitrary nonsaturating child remainders and all the signed
corrections permitted by actual coefficient positivity. It does not
exclude unrestricted relaxed certificates. In particular independent
nonsaturated history weights, different interior cross-copy edges,
and nonzero optimum-source multipliers are not covered. The formal
template is not asserted to be a legal interpolated function.

The original uniform lower theorem and a rigorous fixed asymptotic
separation both remain unproved.
