# One fixed triangle: the impossible root switch and an exact folded debt

Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED. Worker max4, invocation89, project
gd-exact-profile-20260926. This is the single invocation authorized by
TASK.md and master_guidance f5819b6c946020a5. Plan65ee5f1537f246c3.
There were exactly two analytical mechanisms, zero mathematical programs,
zero numerical or recurrence replays, zero formal submissions, zero
consults and zero subagents. All new assertions below belong to one
unverified proof draft. No new fact_id is claimed.

The original all-horizon, all-word minimax problem in PROBLEM.md remains
OPEN. The normalization remains W=2R in the problem's performance
notation. The geometric R used below is explicitly a triangle side length,
not that performance quantity. Accepted d8b59c7d92425121 is used only for
the general smoothness/minimum properties of the stated envelope. Its
representation theorem gives no upper bound for this triangle or for the
full function class. Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 still requires a FULL-class
upper before its conditional799/800 conclusion can be used.

## 1. Results and their exact scopes

1. The entire assigned ORIGINAL-ROOT pattern is impossible: the complete
   left H_(m-1), including the root query, cannot stay on the upper
   sloping side while the root update enters the lower side and the
   complete right H_(m-1), including the original terminal, stays there.
   This holds for every positive finite angle, cap and start, including
   angles depending on m. Section4 gives necessary and sufficient guards,
   the exact original radius, and contradictory necessary inequalities.
   Both the large root factor and the small left-child normal product are
   retained. There is no nonempty domain requiring an objective estimate.

2. For arbitrary histories in the union of the two sloping-side cells,
   one coupled folded state gives the exact original updates, all cap
   guards and the axis tie. Through every complete child it yields an
   exact rotation correction and an exact original-radius slack identity.
   The natural proposal that this correction is always nonpositive is
   FALSE, even on one actual complete original H_1. A finite strict-side
   example has correction15/256. It also rules out a uniform O(1/m)
   correction to that same surrogate, measured by the child's original
   squared radius. It does NOT refute a1/D bound on the actual objective:
   the example has enough positive square slack to satisfy that bound.

No arbitrary-switch1/D bound, quantified total-loss bound, all-depth
threshold witness, unrestricted-triangle bound, or full-class certificate
is claimed. The complete-child counterexample is the alternative output
expressly allowed for Mechanism2; it is not presented as a full-word
adversary. The original full terminal has not been replaced by that child
terminal in any performance claim.

## 2. One static function, side cells, and exact word factors

Fix the enclosing integer m>=1536 and, once for all its children, set

    eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt(2))*(1+eps),
    a=beta/c, d_j=a*c^j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),
    S_j=sum H_j, D_j=1+2S_j, S=S_m, D=D_m.

There are 2^j-1 actual steps in H_j. All physical leaves are1+a.
No child resets eps, beta, c or a.

Choose arbitrary positive finite L,H and define

    K=conv{0,(L,H),(L,-H)}, alpha=(1,0),
    R=sqrt(L^2+H^2), u=L/R, v=H/R,
    e_sigma=(u,sigma*v), n_sigma=(-v,sigma*u), sigma in{+1,-1},
    phi(z)=min_(y in K)[alpha.y+||z-y||^2/2].

Thus u,v>0 and u^2+v^2=1. Set y(z)=P_K(z-alpha), where P_K is Euclidean
projection. Completing the square gives that minimizer, and

    g(z)=grad phi(z)=z-y(z).

The smoothness and minimum assertions of accepted d8b59c7d92425121 apply:
K is a compact convex polytope containing0, and alpha.y>0 for every
nonzero y in K. Thus phi is convex, differentiable and1-smooth, and its
complete minimum set is{0}, with minimum0. This invokes only the target
class properties in Section1 of that accepted proof, not its universality
conclusion. All new trajectory and inequality claims are derived below.

The elementary projection test is

    y=P_K(z-alpha) iff y in K and
    (z-alpha-y).(Y-y)<=0 for every Y in K.                 (2.1)

It follows by differentiating the squared distance along each segment
from y to Y; sufficiency follows by expansion of that squared distance.
In the side frame put r_sigma=e_sigma.z, w_sigma=n_sigma.z. The CLOSED
sloping-side cell (the closure of the region projecting to the relative
interior of that side) is exactly

    u<=r_sigma<=u+R, w_sigma>=-v.                        (2.2)

On it the projection, gradient and objective are

    y=(r_sigma-u)*e_sigma,
    g=u*e_sigma+w_sigma*n_sigma,
    2phi(z)=2u*r_sigma-u^2+w_sigma^2.                    (2.3)

Indeed z-alpha has side coordinates(r_sigma-u,w_sigma+v). The first
belongs to the finite side segment and the second is a nonnegative
outward-normal multiple. These conditions imply all tests(2.1) against
the entire triangle. Conversely, projection to the relative interior of
this side forces zero tangential residual and a nonnegative outward-normal
residual; taking the closure gives(2.2). Boundary overlaps with other
cells give the same projection and gradient. A larger vertex normal cone
is not included merely because its projection is a side endpoint.

A side cell has a forced vertical sign, since

    sigma*t=v*r_sigma+u*w_sigma>=v*u-u*v=0.             (2.4)

Equality in(2.4) requires r_sigma=u and w_sigma=-v, and is exactly the
point z=(1,0). Thus the two closed side cells intersect only there.

Under an actual step h=1+d, the same-frame output coordinates are

    r_hat=r_sigma-h*u, w_hat=-d*w_sigma.                (2.5)

Formula(2.5) computes the actual point; it does not assume that its new
projection lies on the same face. Membership must be tested again.

All ordered interpolation rows are automatic for any actual trajectory of
this one function. For samples i,j, write y_i=y(z_i), g_i=z_i-y_i,
f_i=phi(z_i), delta_i=alpha.y_i. Expansion gives

    f_i=delta_i+||g_i||^2/2,
    f_i-f_j+g_i.(z_j-z_i)+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
       =(g_i-alpha).(y_j-y_i)<=0.                      (2.6)

The inequality is(2.1), and includes the original terminal and optimum.
This does not choose compatible face labels independently.

### 2.1 Exact masses, products and prefix bounds

Let A_j=1+S_j. The defining word gives A_0=1 and
A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j. With eta=a/(c-2), one has0<eta<1, because
c>1+sqrt(2)>2 and c*(c-2)>1>beta. Direct substitution in that recurrence
therefore proves

    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j,
    A_j>=d_j/(c-2), A_j<c^j for j>=1,
    D_j<2c^j for j>=1.                                 (2.7)

Elementary parameter bounds used below are

    12/5<c<5/2, 99/100<beta<1, 1/3<a<1/2.              (2.8)

For example7/5<sqrt(2)<17/12 and eps<1/1000 give the bounds on c.
The displayed beta bound follows from m>=1536. Then
a> (99/100)/(5/2)>1/3 and a<1/(12/5)<1/2.

Let p_j be the unsigned product of all1-h over the actual H_j, with
p_0=1. Then p_(j+1)=d_j*p_j^2 and induction gives

    p_j=beta^(2^j-1)*c^(-j),
    d_j*p_j=beta^(2^j)/c.                              (2.9)

The SIGNED product is -p_j for j>=1 because the word length is odd.
In particular the complete perpendicular factor is

    Q=p_m^2=beta^(2*(2^m-1))*c^(-2m).                 (2.10)

Every nonempty prefix product of the factors d in H_j has magnitude at
most a. Here is a proof retaining every prefix. For H_1 it is exactly a.
In H_(j+1), a prefix inside the first H_j satisfies the assertion by
induction. A prefix ending at its bridge has magnitude p_j*d_j<=a,
by(2.9) and beta^(2^j)<=beta. A prefix extending into the right H_j
has this magnitude times a right-child prefix product, which is at most1
(the empty prefix is1 and every nonempty one is at most a<1).

As a result, a trajectory prescribed to stay in ONE side for an entire
nonempty H_j and its terminal has exactly the following start guards:

    u*(S_j+1)<=r_start<=u+R,
    -v<=w_start<=v/a.                                 (2.11)

Tangential coordinates decrease by the positive cumulative step mass,
so the two extreme samples give all their guards. Necessity of the two
normal bounds uses the start and the first leaf, whose normal is
-a*w_start. For sufficiency, a nonnegative start normal can violate a
lower bound only at negative signed prefixes; their magnitudes are at
most a. A negative start normal can violate a lower bound only at positive
signed prefixes; their magnitudes are at most1. This proves every normal
guard, including the child's terminal, without enumerating them.

Finally,

    D_j*p_j^2<2*c^(-j)<1 for every j>=1,              (2.12)

using(2.7),(2.9), beta<1 and c>2. In particular Q<1/D.

## 3. The retained fixed-side identity and finite tilt

For one legal same-side H_j, the actual terminal is

    r_end=r_start-S_j*u, w_end=-p_j*w_start.

Expanding a square in its exact objective(2.3) gives

    ||z_start||^2-D_j*2phi(z_end)
      =(r_start-D_j*u)^2+(1-D_j*p_j^2)*w_start^2.       (3.1)

This identity is jointly rederived here, not adopted from the unverified
round88 report. It keeps both the transported affine height and the
complete normal product. Applying it to a changed side without a correction
is the conjectural extension tested in Section7.

The prescribed lift has exact finite payment for every planar trajectory,
including any that later leave the two side cells. For finite T>0 put

    Ktilde={(y_1,y_2,0):y in K}, lambda=(1,0,T),
    Phi(z,q)=min_(Y in Ktilde)[lambda.Y+||(z,q)-Y||^2/2].

Separation of the third coordinate gives

    Phi(z,q)=phi(z)+q^2/2, grad Phi=(g(z),q).           (3.2)

The complete minimum set remains{0}. For the ACTUAL initial point

    X_0=(s,t,-s/T), lambda.X_0=0,

the planar dynamics are unchanged, while each physical step multiplies q
by1-h. Consequently at the ORIGINAL full terminal

    ||X_0||^2=s^2+t^2+s^2/T^2,
    2Phi(X_n)=2phi(z_n)+Q*s^2/T^2,
    E_T=[2phi(z_n)+Q*s^2/T^2]/[s^2+t^2+s^2/T^2].       (3.3)

For a complete H_j the same identity uses p_j^2 in place of Q and that
child's actual start and terminal. Since0 is the unique minimum, these
denominators are the true squared initial distances. If z_0!=0 and
chi=s^2/[T^2*(s^2+t^2)], then

    E_T=(E_plane+chi*Q)/(1+chi).                      (3.4)

Thus a planar upper at least Q passes to every finite tilt. No infinite
tilt is an admissible concrete witness, and none is used here.

## 4. Mechanism1: all guards at the original root, and impossibility

Write

    k=m-1, A=S_k, p=p_k, d=d_k, h=1+d,
    tau_rt=d*p=beta^(2^k)/c,
    kappa=u^2-v^2, omega=2u*v.

The original full word is(H_k,h,H_k), and S=2A+h. In particular

    A+1>2d, d>1, 0<tau_rt<1/2.                          (4.1)

The first follows from(2.7) and c-2<1/2. For d>1, k>=1535 and
d=a*c^k>(1/3)*2^k>1. The last follows from(2.9) and c>2.

Let(r_0,w_0) be the original start in the UPPER frame. Suppose the
complete left H_k, including its terminal which is the root query, lies
in the upper cell. By(2.11), this is equivalent to

    u*(A+1)<=r_0<=u+R, -v<=w_0<=v/a.                 (4.2)

At the actual root query the upper coordinates are

    r_L=r_0-A*u, w_L=-p*w_0.

The root update has upper-frame coordinates

    X=r_0-(A+h)*u, Y=tau_rt*w_0.                        (4.3)

The large root multiplier d is accompanied by the exact left-child
attenuation p. Replacing tau_rt by d would change the actual dynamics.

For the same point, denote LOWER-frame coordinates by(r,w). The exact
orthogonal change of frame is

    r=kappa*X-omega*Y, w=-omega*X-kappa*Y,
    X=kappa*r-omega*w, Y=-omega*r-kappa*w.             (4.4)

The matrix in(4.4) is its own inverse, because
kappa^2+omega^2=1. The complete right H_k AND original terminal lie
on the lower side exactly when

    u*(A+1)<=r<=u+R, -v<=w<=v/a.                     (4.5)

Combining(4.2)-(4.5), the following four pairs are necessary AND sufficient
for the entire requested pattern, parameterized by the root output(r,w):

    u*(A+1)<=r<=u+R,
    -v<=w<=v/a,
    u*(A+1)<=(A+h)*u+kappa*r-omega*w<=u+R,
    -v<=-(omega*r+kappa*w)/tau_rt<=v/a.                 (4.6)

Necessity was derived from every child's exact guards. Conversely, from
(4.6) define

    r_0=(A+h)*u+kappa*r-omega*w,
    w_0=-(omega*r+kappa*w)/tau_rt.

The last two guard pairs keep the complete left child and root query on
the upper side. Equations(4.3)-(4.4) send its root update exactly to(r,w).
The first two guard pairs keep the complete right child and original
terminal on the lower side. This proves sufficiency with one finite K.

The original planar squared radius, BEFORE either child, is exactly

    N0=[(A+h)*u+kappa*r-omega*w]^2
                        +(omega*r+kappa*w)^2/tau_rt^2.  (4.7)

Its global first coordinate is s_0=u*r_0-v*w_0, so the finite lift would
have denominator N0+s_0^2/T^2. The formal right-terminal objective is

    2phi(z_n)=2u*r-(1+2A)*u^2+p^2*w^2.              (4.8)

These formulas do not assert that(4.6) has a solution. In fact it has none.

### 4.1 Exact incompatible original guards, for every angle

The left root-query tangent and the original start normal in(4.2) imply

    X>=-d*u, Y>=-tau_rt*v.                              (4.9)

The lower root-output cell forces its actual global vertical coordinate
to be nonpositive by(2.4). Written in the upper frame this says

    v*X+u*Y<=0.                                     (4.10)

If kappa>=0, use X<=-(u/v)*Y from(4.10) in(4.4):

    r<=-(kappa*u/v+omega)*Y
      =-(u/v)*Y<=u*tau_rt.                              (4.11)

The equality uses kappa+2v^2=1. But(4.5) requires
r>=u*(A+1), whereas A+1>2d>2>tau_rt. This is a contradiction.

If kappa<0, multiply the first inequality in(4.9) by kappa, and use
the second there in(4.4). It follows that

    r<=u*(-kappa*d+2v^2*tau_rt)
      <=u*(d+2tau)<2d*u<u*(A+1).                     (4.12)

The middle strict inequality uses2tau<1<d, and the final one is(4.1).
This again contradicts the original right child's terminal tangential
guard in(4.5).

The contradiction cancels only the positive factor u; it never assumes
that u is bounded away from0. It retains the original cap guards in(4.6),
although their upper inequalities are not needed for the contradiction.
It covers equality ties and every positive finite L,H,R. In particular
no geometry varying with m evades the argument.

This is stronger than a vacuous scoped1/D upper: the prescribed domain is
empty. It excludes only the two complete fixed-side children with their
single ORIGINAL-root switch. It does not exclude root switches when
either child has other side changes, or histories involving any other
projection cell. No first-leaf/single-suffix pattern is reopened.

## 5. Mechanism2: one coupled folded state, with actual labels forced

The decisive Section4 result opens the second and last permitted
mechanism. Consider any actual original history, or a complete original
child, for which every queried sample AND its stated terminal belong to
the union of the two side cells. No independent face-label choices are
allowed.

For z=(s,t), set

    q=|t|, r=u*s+v*q, w=-v*s+u*q.                    (5.1)

For t!=0, the side sign is uniquely sigma=sign(t) by(2.4). The full
folded guard is

    q>=0, u<=r<=u+R, w>=-v.                         (5.2)

Thus all finite cap guards remain. When q=0, (5.2) implies both s>=1
and s<=1, hence z=(1,0). Both side descriptions there give g=(1,0).
For a queried tie and any physical h>1, the next state is(1-h,0), which
does not satisfy(5.2). Therefore a nonempty history confined to the two
sides has no axis tie at a queried sample. A terminal tie at(1,0) is
allowed. Formulae below are valid for either sign choice at a tie and
give the same update.

In global coordinates the gradient is

    g=(u^2-v*w, sigma*u*(v+w)).                      (5.3)

Using w=-v*s+u*q in z_next=z-h*g gives exactly

    s_next=(1-h*v^2)*s+h*u*v*q-h*u^2,
    t_next=sigma*((1-h*u^2)*q+h*u*v*s-h*u*v).         (5.4)

This derives the guidance's proposed coordinate law. The new folded
height is the absolute value of the bracket, never a freely selected sign.

Equivalently, for d=h-1 define the signed trial height and fold amount

    q_hat=v*(r-h*u)-u*d*w,
    ell=max(0,-q_hat).                               (5.5)

Unfolded upper-half-plane trial coordinates are r-h*u and -d*w.
Reflecting only a negative trial height changes r by -2v*q_hat and w
by -2u*q_hat. Thus ONE coupled update, valid in both cases, is

    r_next=r-h*u+2v*ell,
    w_next=-d*w+2u*ell.                              (5.6)

The actual height is q_next=|q_hat|, and s_next is unchanged by that
fold. It follows that r_next=u*s_next+v*q_next and
w_next=-v*s_next+u*q_next, as required by(5.1).

The old side persists if q_hat>0, changes if q_hat<0, and reaches the
axis if q_hat=0. A next sample can be retained in the two-side class
only if its actual coordinates satisfy(5.2). Starting with a legal
initial sample, these checks at every original next sample are both
necessary and sufficient for the asserted two-side history, by(2.1)-(2.3).
They enforce the same cap R throughout and include the actual terminal.

## 6. Exact chronological rotation correction through every complete child

Let a complete original H_j, j>=1, have steps h_0,...,h_(N-1), where
N=2^j-1. Indices here are relative positions in this actual child, with
the same enclosing m. Define the signed suffix product

    B_(i+1,N)=product_(i<k<N)(1-h_k),

with the empty product1. Along a legal two-side history define ell_i
by(5.5), and set

    Lfold=sum_(0<=i<N) ell_i,
    Zfold=sum_(0<=i<N) B_(i+1,N)*ell_i.               (6.1)

Lfold>=0, but the coefficients in Zfold retain their chronological signs.
There is no bound on the number of side changes and no label enumeration.
Telescoping the first equation in(5.6), and expanding the scalar affine
recursion in the second, gives

    r_N=r_0-S_j*u+2v*Lfold,
    w_N=-p_j*w_0+2u*Zfold.                          (6.2)

The affine expansion follows by induction on the number of actual steps:
each new fold is added with coefficient2u and is thereafter multiplied
by each following1-h. The initial coefficient is the complete signed
product -p_j from(2.9). This proves(6.2) for every length, including all
original leaves and both children of any larger block.

Substitute(6.2) into the exact terminal objective(2.3). Define

    Cfold_j=4u*v*Lfold-4u*p_j*w_0*Zfold+4u^2*Zfold^2.

Then the two exact coupled identities are

    2phi(z_N)=2u*r_0-D_j*u^2+p_j^2*w_0^2+Cfold_j,    (6.3)

    ||z_0||^2-D_j*2phi(z_N)
      =(r_0-D_j*u)^2+(1-D_j*p_j^2)*w_0^2
                                      -D_j*Cfold_j. (6.4)

An equivalent useful expression is

    Cfold_j=4u*v*Lfold+w_N^2-p_j^2*w_0^2.            (6.5)

All quantities in(6.3)-(6.5) use the child's actual original input; at
j=m that is the original full-word input. There is no replacement of the
initial radius at a switch. With no folds, Lfold=Zfold=Cfold_j=0 and
the jointly derived identity(3.1) is recovered.

The natural first proposed invariant is the unchanged same-side
majorant, now applied to the folded coordinates:

    2phi(z_N)<=2u*r_0-D_j*u^2+p_j^2*w_0^2
    for every legal two-side complete H_j.              (F0)

Equivalently, (F0) asserts Cfold_j<=0, or asserts that the left side
of(6.4) is at least the same two positive terms as in(3.1). If true for
j=m, it would immediately give the desired scoped1/D upper by(2.12).
It is a coupled height/normal assertion, not merely a scalar radius or
normal-contraction statement. Section7 refutes it with all child guards.

For comparison, the actual upper1/D_j needs only

    D_j*Cfold_j <= (r_0-D_j*u)^2
                         +(1-D_j*p_j^2)*w_0^2.       (6.6)

Equation(6.6) is a precise remaining interface, not a proved estimate.
A full-word(1+C0/m)/D upper would permit a further(C0/m)||z_0||^2
on its right at j=m. No such uniform accumulated bound is established.

## 7. A strict, finite complete-child counterexample to (F0)

Keep any enclosing m>=1536, with its actual a in(1/3,1/2). Choose the
ONE finite triangle and original planar start

    u=sqrt(3)/2, v=1/2, R=3,
    L=3sqrt(3)/2, H=3/2,
    h=1+a,
    r_0=u*(13/8+a), w_0=15/(16a),
    z_0=r_0*e_++w_0*n_+.                           (7.1)

Take the complete ORIGINAL leaf H_1=(1+a), beginning at this start.
The initial upper-side projection is strictly inside its side:

    r_0-u=u*(5/8+a) in(0,3), w_0+v>0.              (7.2)

The strict upper bound follows from a<1/2 and u<1. Its actual leaf
output in the OLD upper frame is, by(2.5),

    r_hat=5u/8, w_hat=-15/16,
    q_hat=v*r_hat+u*w_hat=-5u/8<0.                 (7.3)

Thus its side change is forced by the actual physical update. Here
ell=5u/8. Apply(5.6), using u^2=3/4, to obtain the new LOWER folded
coordinates

    r_1=5u/4, w_1=0.                              (7.4)

These also have strict side guards:

    r_1-u=u/4 in(0,3), w_1+v=v>0.                 (7.5)

In global coordinates the actual terminal is

    z_1=(15/16,-5u/8).                             (7.6)

Thus both queried and terminal projections are on the interiors of their
stated finite sloping sides, with positive outward residual. There is no
origin-, base- or nonzero-vertex pattern in this example, no implicit cap
limit, and no arbitrary label selection.

The true exact terminal objective is

    2phi(z_1)=2u*(5u/4)-u^2=9/8=288/256.           (7.7)

The right side of the proposed(F0), with D_1=3+2a and p_1=a, equals

    2u*r_0-D_1*u^2+a^2*w_0^2
       =2u*(5u/8)-u^2+(15/16)^2
       =273/256.                                  (7.8)

Consequently

    Cfold_1=15/256>0,                              (7.9)

independently of m. This is a legal complete-child counterexample to
(F0), including its terminal and the actual first physical leaf of the
enclosing word. It need not and does not claim that the unevaluated later
suffix of H_m stays in the two-side class. A complete-child state is
enough to refute the universal all-children invariant(F0).

### 7.1 Why the correction has no sign, and is not automatically small

For any one-step fold, with d=h-1 and ell>0, (6.5) gives

    Cfold=4u*v*ell+(-d*w+2u*ell)^2-d^2*w^2
         =4u*ell*(v-d*w+u*ell).

Use ell=-v*(r-h*u)+u*d*w from(5.5). Since
s_next=u*(r-h*u)+v*d*w, the bracket becomes
v*(1-s_next). Thus the exact one-step rotation correction is

    Cfold=4u*v*ell*(1-s_next).                     (7.10)

It is zero if there is no fold. Actual side guards alone impose no sign
on1-s_next. In(7.6) this factor is1/16, producing(7.9).
This pinpoints the failed sign argument; a small normal product by itself
does not dispose of a rotated affine height.

The example also excludes the relaxed surrogate

    2phi(z_N)<=2u*r_0-D_j*u^2+p_j^2*w_0^2
                              +(C0/m)||z_0||^2       (Fsmall)

uniformly over all complete children and all enclosing m, for any fixed
absolute C0>=0. Indeed its original squared planar radius is exactly

    N0=||z_0||^2=(3/4)*(13/8+a)^2+225/(256a^2).     (7.11)

The bounds1/3<a<1/2 imply

    N0<867/256+2025/256=723/64,
    Cfold_1/N0>5/964.                              (7.12)

For any fixed C0, choose an integer m>=1536 with m>964*C0/5.
Then(7.9)-(7.12) violate(Fsmall). This is a failure of a perturbatively
small correction to THIS surrogate. It is not a failure of a
(1+C0/m)/D bound on the actual objective, whose available positive
radius slack is different.

### 7.2 Original radius and finite lift: no objective-threshold claim

In fact the planar example satisfies the sharp child benchmark strictly.
From a<1/2 and a>0, (7.11) gives

    N0>507/256+900/256=1407/256>9/2,
    D_1*2phi(z_1)=(3+2a)*(9/8)<9/2.

Therefore

    2phi(z_1)/N0<1/D_1.                            (7.13)

Equivalently, the two positive original-radius terms in(6.4) exceed
D_1*Cfold_1 in this example. A positive Cfold does not by itself force
an objective-bound failure.

For a concrete orthogonal finite lift, choose T=1 and write

    s_0=u*r_0-v*w_0,
    t_0=v*r_0+u*w_0,
    X_0=(s_0,t_0,-s_0), lambda=(1,0,1).

These are finite explicit values, with t_0>0. Equation(3.2) shows that
the lifted leaf is exactly the same planar transition and has terminal
third coordinate a*s_0. Its exact score and TRUE initial radius are

    E_leaf,T=1=[9/8+a^2*s_0^2]/[N0+s_0^2],
    dist(X_0,argmin Phi)^2=N0+s_0^2.                (7.14)

Since a^2<1/4<1/D_1, (7.13)-(7.14) again give E_leaf,T=1<1/D_1.
The positive discrepancy(7.9) survives if the identical perpendicular
term a^2*s_0^2 is added to both sides of the failed surrogate.

No comparison of this leaf score with17/(16D_m) is meaningful as a
full-word witness. The ORIGINAL H_m terminal is still obtained only
after its entire remaining physical suffix, and no value for it is
asserted from(7.14). For that full evolution, irrespective of later
projection cells, the exact perpendicular factor remains Q in(3.3).

## 8. Preserved prior counterexamples and excluded repetitions

Round88 joint b2a4bfc15411d7af remains UNVERIFIED and is not a premise
for any new result above. Its two counterexamples remain relevant and
have NOT been superseded by the root impossibility:

* The proposed scalar distance tube fails on an actual complete leaf:
  with u=sqrt(15)/4,v=1/4,R=3, initial upper coordinates
  r_0=u+a/(4sqrt(15)),w_0=2, the true distances to K are2 before the
  leaf and35a/16 afterwards. The terminal is in the origin cell for
  the objective projection. This example is preserved in Section6.1
  of the previous report; it is outside the two-side-only histories
  of Mechanism2 here.

* The small-start capture fails on an actual entire H_m at finite tilt:
  u=d_m/(2D),v=sqrt(1-u^2),R=d_m,r_0=d_m/2=D*u,w_0=0,
  with every planar sample on the upper side and T=1. The true initial
  radius is less than d_m, while terminal2Phi>1. Its score is
  (1/D+u^2*Q)/(1+u^2)<1/D. This example is preserved in Section6.2
  of the previous report; it shows why an angle-independent small-radius
  capture cannot replace the coupled identity.

These are retained references to already shared unverified findings,
not additional mechanisms or accepted bricks. Their needed replacements,
the projection cells, exact word factors, fixed-side identity and finite
lift, were all derived jointly in Sections2-3 above.

The earlier full first-leaf upper-to-lower pattern followed by an entirely
lower-side original suffix is closed in that previous joint report and
was NOT retried. The new root pattern has a different chronology and is
empty. The Section7 example tests a complete-leaf invariant; it does not
reopen the old full-word single-switch objective analysis.

## 9. Recall, literature, and final quantitative interface

Read TASK.md, the original PROBLEM.md, latest guidancef5819b6c946020a5,
own prior notes/events, relevant shared findings/dead ends/verdicts, the
round88 report, and the relevant accepted statementsd8b59c7d92425121 and
931b9653ce0f7201. The target-class proof ofd8b59c7d92425121 was also read.
Fact searches returned no accepted root-pattern or folded-state result;
different static spiral/support-envelope triangles do not supply this
missing payment. Initial broad recall outputs were truncated; the exact
needed text and passages were reread in bounded form. No truncated text
is used as a mathematical premise.

Applied query-memory, direct-proving and construct-counterexamples within
the task's two-mechanism cap. The decomposition was: derive actual root
guards and radius; resolve their full domain; then derive and test one
coupled folded invariant. Both new mechanisms were carried to decisive
scoped outcomes. The zero formal allowance forbids promotion here; no
unverified intermediate has been cited as an accepted predecessor.

Search stage: mature_subproblem. Active program: this one affine-height
triangle under the SAME-m word. Missing mechanism: uniform total rotation
payment against the original radius. Mode: repair; intent: theorem and
proof background. Matlas returned small-step extragradient, diminishing
projected steps, and bounded-step definable-descent statements, none with
the present original long-step dynamics. A web fallback located the
silver-schedule background. The already present exact local text
downloads/release_middle_sector/2309.16530.txt was read at its multi-step
descent overview and recursive-gluing argument.

Reference for that background only: Jason M. Altschuler and Pablo A.
Parrilo, "Acceleration by Stepsize Hedging II: Silver Stepsize Schedule
for Smooth Convex Optimization", arXiv:2309.16530 (2023),
https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.16530 . Its long-range consistency and recursive
gluing motivate retaining signed chronological terms. Its schedule is
not the perturbed word here; no theorem from it is used in this proof.
This is the previously audited triangle regime, so no further literature
branch, new family or external black box was introduced.

For arbitrary two-side original full histories the exact unresolved
quantity is Cfold_m of(6.3), with every ell_i fixed by(5.5) and every
original cap guard(5.2) retained. A successful sharp bound must pay
D*Cfold_m by the positive original-radius squares in(6.4), or by those
squares plus a quantified absolute O(1/m) fraction of the original
radius. Section7 proves that one cannot instead declare all complete-child
corrections nonpositive, nor perturbatively small relative to that radius.
It leaves(6.6) OPEN. Summing an unbounded number of per-switch allowances
does not close it.

Even a proof of(6.6) for all two-side histories would leave trajectories
with other projection cells, general triangles, and the full function
class outside its scope. No original-target completion or conditional
799/800 implication follows from this report. The single invocation
allocation ends here, without automatic renewal or lifecycle action.

# C83: exact all-curvature repair at a cut

Status: complete joint UNVERIFIED analytical candidate. No new schedule,
optimizer/model evaluation, numerical replay, installation or verifier
call. This strengthens the shared unit-curvature cut/quad idea by using
every curvature kappa in(0,1], retaining its strictly negative original
interpolation residuals, and deriving the exact maximal repair weight.
The unit-curvature shared draft is motivation only; everything needed
is rederived here. Accepted facts used:1252b8823bb5a290 (interpolation)
and04219554e41d9750 Section10 (full primitive certificate identity).

## 1. Data and statement of the necessary condition

Let h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be any finite nonnegative word, let
s=sum h_i,A=1+s, and select an index j with h_j>1. Define

 P=sum_(i<j)h_i, R=sum_(i>j)h_i, H=P+h_j=s-R,
 eta=(h_j-1)/(1+R)>0, C0=eta(1+eta),
 V0=(1+P)^2-A^2 eta^2.

For kappa in(0,1] let

 p_0=1, p_i=product_(t<i)(1-kappa h_t) for0<=i<=j+1,
 E=1+kappa R, q=kappa p_(j+1)/E,
 t_kappa=(1-kappa)/(2kappa).

If R>0 put

 beta=max{0, max_(0<=i<=j)
       [q(kappa p_i-q)-t_kappa(kappa p_i-q)^2/R]}.

If R=0 put beta=0. Finally define

 V_kappa=
   kappa sum_(i=0)^j h_i[1+kappa(1-h_i)]p_i^2
   -H[s+R+1+1/kappa]q^2.                               (1)

Every full primitive certificate for the ORIGINAL word necessarily
satisfies, for every such j and every0<kappa<=1,

             beta V0+C0 V_kappa >=0.                    (2)

For beta=0 the stronger explicit interpretation is V_kappa>=0,
which is equivalent to(2) because C0>0. No other step is assumed
positive, above1, symmetric, ordered or part of a primitive prefix.

## 2. The legal scalar cut coordinate

Use the scalar convex function with derivative clip(x,-eta,1),
minimum0 at0, and initial point1+P. Put
P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t and R_i=sum_(t>=i)h_t. Its actual data are

 x_i^0=1+P-P_i,    g_i^0=1,             i<=j,
 x_i^0=-eta(1+R_i), g_i^0=-eta,         i>j,
 f_i^0=x_i^0 g_i^0-(g_i^0)^2/2.

The selected step sends1 to1-h_j=-eta(1+R), and the remaining
updates keep the points on the negative constant-gradient segment,
ending at-eta. All zero steps retain their original sample indices.
This verifies the full GD chronology on one actual function.

For this coordinate the ordered residual is

 Q_ik^0=(g_i^0-g_k^0)(x_k^0-g_k^0).

It is0 inside either block. From the prefix to a suffix target k>j,
it is exactly-C0 R_k. In the opposite direction it is
-(1+eta)(P-P_k)<=0. All optimum comparisons are nonpositive.
The norm-telescoping identity, or direct substitution, gives its
primitive energy V0 exactly as defined above.

## 3. A quadratic prefix and constant-gradient suffix

Define a second set of scalar data, not yet claimed to be legal:

 x_i^1=p_i, g_i^1=kappa p_i, f_i^1=kappa p_i^2/2,       i<=j;
 x_i^1=q(1/kappa+R_i), g_i^1=q,
 f_i^1=q x_i^1-q^2/(2kappa),                           i>j.

The point after the selected step is p_(j+1)=q(1/kappa+R),
so these data follow every original GD update and end at q/kappa.
Include the optimum sample (x_star^1,g_star^1,f_star^1)=(0,0,0).

Write Q_ik^1=f_i^1-f_k^1+g_i^1(x_k^1-x_i^1)
                  +(g_i^1-g_k^1)^2/2.
Direct substitution gives the COMPLETE list of residual types:

 * i,k<=j:
   Q_ik^1=-t_kappa(g_i^1-g_k^1)^2<=0.
 * i,k>j: Q_ik^1=0.
 * i>j,k<=j:
   Q_ik^1=-t_kappa(q-kappa p_k)^2<=0.
 * i<=j,k>j:
   Q_ik^1=R_k q(kappa p_i-q)
                    -t_kappa(kappa p_i-q)^2.
 * Q_(i,star)^1=-t_kappa(g_i^1)^2<=0 for all i.
 * Q_(star,i)^1=-t_kappa(g_i^1)^2 for i<=j, and
   Q_(star,i)^1=-(R_i+t_kappa)q^2 for i>j.

Thus only the fourth type can be positive. If R_k=0 it is
nonpositive. For R_k>0, its quotient by R_k is

 q(kappa p_i-q)-t_kappa(kappa p_i-q)^2/R_k,

which is nondecreasing as R_k increases. Since0<R_k<=R,
its largest possible value is at k=j+1, whose R_k equals R.
This proves that beta is the exact largest nonnegative ratio of
a possibly bad residual to its corresponding suffix mass.

In particular beta=0 makes ALL auxiliary data, including optimum
comparisons, legal. If R=0 there are no positive suffix masses,
so the convention beta=0 has exactly this same meaning.

## 4. Orthogonal repair gives an actual globally legal function

If beta>0 choose epsilon=C0/beta and set

 X_i=(x_i^0,sqrt(epsilon)x_i^1),
 G_i=(g_i^0,sqrt(epsilon)g_i^1),
 F_i=f_i^0+epsilon f_i^1,

with the zero optimum sample. GD still uses the same full word.
Every original residual is Q_ik^0+epsilon Q_ik^1. All types except
prefix-to-suffix are sums of nonpositive numbers. For that type
with R_k>0 the sum is at most

       -C0 R_k+epsilon beta R_k=0;

when R_k=0 the auxiliary residual itself is nonpositive. Therefore
all directed comparisons, including the optimum and original terminal
sample, hold. A maximizing i in beta and k=j+1 show that epsilon
is also the largest permitted weight of this fixed auxiliary data
against this fixed base coordinate. No residual was discarded.

By accepted1252b8823bb5a290, proof paragraph2, these planar jets have
an everywhere finite convex differentiable1-smooth interpolant with
minimum value0 at0. Concretely, with I={star,0,...,n}, define

 c_i=<G_i,X_i>-F_i,
 J_i(v)=c_i+<X_i,v-G_i>+||v-G_i||^2/2,
 J(v)=max_(i in I) J_i(v),
 F(x)=sup_v{<x,v>-J(v)}.

The cited fact proves F(X_i)=F_i and grad F(X_i)=G_i from exactly
the residual convention just verified. Thus these are actual GD data
of one function, not a sum of separately illegal functions.

If beta=0, apply the same accepted interpolation construction directly
to the auxiliary scalar jets, since their full residual list is legal.
No division by beta occurs in this case.

## 5. Exact energy calculation

For the auxiliary suffix, f_i^1-f_n^1=q^2 R_i and
f_n^1=q^2/(2kappa). The elementary finite identity

 2sum_(i>j) h_i R_i = R^2+sum_(i>j)h_i^2

follows by expanding R^2. Thus the combined suffix contribution of
the weighted value differences and gradient diagonals is R(R+1)q^2.
The prefix contribution is

 kappa sum_(i<=j)h_i[1+kappa(1-h_i)]p_i^2-H q^2/kappa.

Subtracting the terminal s(s+1)q^2 gives(1), because
s(s+1)-R(R+1)=H(s+R+1).

The primitive energy is linear in all values and Gram entries.
The repaired planar trajectory therefore has energy

          V0+(C0/beta)V_kappa

when beta>0, and the legal auxiliary trajectory has energy V_kappa
when beta=0. Accepted04219554e41d9750, Section10, says an original
full nonnegative flow/PSD certificate forces nonnegative energy on
every actual trajectory. This proves(2), including beta=0.

## 6. The unit-curvature slice and exact low-degree polynomial interface

At kappa=1, q=p_(j+1)/(1+R)=-eta p_j, t_kappa=0, and

 beta=max{0,max_(i<=j)q(p_i-q)}, V_1=1-A^2 q^2.

For the last identity, the prefix sum in(1) telescopes to
1-p_(j+1)^2, using p_(i+1)^2=(1-h_i)^2 p_i^2. Then
(1+R)^2+H(s+R+2)=A^2. This rederives the unit-curvature
repair without treating the shared max2 draft as a correctness premise.

For R>0 the entire curvature interval can be expressed through
finitely many POLYNOMIAL branches. Define

 d_i(kappa)=[E p_i-p_(j+1)]/kappa,                     0<=i<=j,
 T_i(kappa)=2R p_(j+1)d_i-(1-kappa)d_i^2,
 T_max(kappa)=max{0,T_0(kappa),...,T_j(kappa)},

 P_cut(kappa)=E^2 sum_(i<=j)h_i[1+kappa(1-h_i)]p_i^2
              -H[1+kappa(s+R+1)]p_(j+1)^2.

The numerator defining d_i vanishes at kappa=0, so d_i is a
polynomial, including at0. It has degree at most j. Consequently
T_i has degree at most2j+1, and P_cut degree at most2j+3.
The exact identities are

 beta=kappa^3 T_max/(2R E^2),
 V_kappa=kappa P_cut/E^2.

Since kappa,R,E are positive, (2) is equivalent to

 kappa^2 V0 T_max(kappa)+2R C0 P_cut(kappa)>=0,
                                             0<kappa<=1. (3)

At each branch where0 or a specific T_i is the maximum, (3) is
a polynomial inequality of degree at most2j+3. For a four-step word,
the two middle cuts j=1,2 therefore require degrees at most5,7.
Their branch selection inequalities are the explicit comparisons
T_i>=0 and T_i>=T_k, with the zero branch selected when all T_i<=0.
This gives a precise exact real-algebraic interface for further work;
no polynomial-root search or numerical interval scan is performed.

## 7. Scope and remaining task

The construction gives legal adversaries whenever(2) fails. Passing
all these tests gives no full certificate; no sufficiency is claimed.
The convex combination mechanism retains every original interpolation
pair and all function-value offsets. A favorable or unfavorable outcome
on any finite list of curvatures would not settle the universal interval.

No new word was selected, tested, rounded, or evaluated in deriving this
family. It neither constructs the missing four-step seed nor excludes
every h with sum>=168/25. The accepted conditional lift remains available
as fact04219554e41d9750; the seed existence problem is still OPEN.

# Both child defects, an actual common function, and the cost of resetting boundaries

Status: **UNVERIFIED joint proof candidate**, eighth launch, cumulative round19.
No fact_submit, optimization, parameter search, installation or subagent.
This is a construction and a limitation of its specified iteration class,
not a resolution of the original minimax problem. Definitions of W_n, Q_ij
and the exact B_n comparison are from accepted1252b8823bb5a290 and
b98bb807d514e80d. All additional mathematics used below is rederived here;
the previous round's unverified construction is not used as a truth premise.

## 1. Actual core data and the common projection function

The input is a nonnegative finite word p=(p_0,...,p_(ell-1)) and ALL vectors
x_i,g_i,0<=i<=ell, including the endpoint and every repeated/zero-step sample.
The optimum sample is0. Require

    x_(i+1)=x_i-p_i g_i, y_i=x_i-g_i,
    <g_j-g_i,y_i><=0 for every i,j, <g_i,y_i>>=0,
    x_ell=g_ell=w !=0.                                    (1)

Set K=conv(0,g_0,...,g_ell) and

    F_K(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2),
    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, f_*=0,
    A=1+sum p_i, R_i=sum_(t=i)^(ell-1) p_t, R^2=||x_0||^2,
    Delta=A^2||w||^2-R^2, kappa=A^2||w||^2/R^2.            (2)

Completing the square identifies the maximizer with the projection onto K;
(1) is exactly its normal condition. Adding the projection conditions for
two points gives ||P_K(x)-P_K(x')||^2<=<x-x',P_K(x)-P_K(x')>.
It follows that P_K is 1-Lipschitz. Evaluation of the two maxima at their
opposite maximizers bounds the differentiability remainder by ||x-x'||^2,
so F_K is convex differentiable and 1-smooth with gradient P_K and values (2).

Its minimizers are K^polar={z:<z,g><=0 for every g in K}. To prove the
nontrivial implication F_K(z)=0 => z in K^polar, test epsilon*g in the
definition and let epsilon decrease to0. The GD identity gives

    x_0=w+sum_(i<ell) p_i g_i.                             (3)

Thus <x_0,z><=0 for every minimizer z and
||x_0-z||^2>=||x_0||^2, with equality at0. R is the TRUE closest-minimizer
distance. Also direct expansion of the original interpolation residual gives

    Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>, Q_i*=0, Q_*i=-<g_i,y_i><=0.        (4)

Every subsequent normal check therefore covers all original ordered-pair,
bridge, old-sample and optimum interpolation inequalities, with the indicated
values. No child translation or common value shift is a free operation.

## 2. A finite explicit lift of every actual child

Call a child admissible for the joint operation if, in addition to (1),

    <g_i,w>>=0, <y_i,w>>=0,
    <g_i,x_0>>=0, <y_i,x_0>>=0 for every i.                 (5)

These are four lists of ACTUAL boundary pairings, not a condition on Delta
alone. Any input satisfying (1) can be converted into such a child. Define

    beta_min = max(0,
       max_i (-<g_i,w>),
       max_(i:R_i>0) (-<w,y_i>/R_i),
       max_i (-<x_0,g_i>/A),
       max_(i:R_i>0) (-<x_0,y_i>/(A R_i))).                (6)

An empty maximum is omitted. Choose any finite beta>=beta_min and set

    ghat_i=(g_i,sqrt(beta)),
    xhat_i=(x_i,(1+R_i)sqrt(beta)),
    what=(w,sqrt(beta)), Khat=conv(0,ghat_0,...,ghat_ell). (7)

The physical GD updates hold. For old vertices, the added gradient
coordinates cancel, so every old/old normal is exactly the original one.
The zero-vertex normal is -<g_i,y_i>-beta R_i<=0. The four pairings in
(5) become, respectively,

    <g_i,w>+beta,             <w,y_i>+beta R_i,
    <x_0,g_i>+A beta,         <x_0,y_i>+A beta R_i.         (8)

They are nonnegative by (6). If R_i=0, all remaining physical steps vanish,
so x_i=x_ell=w; uniqueness of projection gives g_i=w and y_i=0. Thus no
uncontrolled numerator was omitted in (6). Formula (6) is the exact minimum
beta within THIS constant-coordinate lift for all four signs (5).

The new values, minimizers and true distance are explicit:

    fhat_i=f_i+(R_i+1/2)beta,
    argmin F_Khat=Khat^polar,
    Rhat^2=R^2+A^2 beta,
    Deltahat=Delta,
    kappahat=1+Delta/(R^2+A^2 beta).                       (9)

The distance follows from (3) for the lifted samples. In particular all
positive defects remain strictly positive at every finite lift; the cost is
real and is included in (9). All nonzero original residuals remain present.

## 3. The joint construction with both complete children

Take any two inputs satisfying (1) and (5), with capacities A,C, initial
distances R_L,R_R, and endpoints w_L,w_R. Let r>0 be the positive solution

    P=A+C+r, rP=AC.                                      (10)

This is exactly the source primitive bridge1+r. Define unit vectors
e=w_L/||w_L||, d=x_R,0/R_R, and real isometries

    J_L u=(<u,e>,u-<u,e>e,0),
    J_R v=(-<v,d>,0,v-<v,d>d), a_scale=r||w_L||/R_R.      (11)

The common ambient space is R direct-sum e^perp direct-sum d^perp. Keep the
entire left transcript under J_L and the entire right one under a_scale J_R.
At the bridge, J_L w_L-(1+r)J_L w_L=a_scale J_R x_R,0, so the actual word
is (p_L,1+r,p_R). The parent endpoint W=a_scale J_R w_R is a core endpoint.
The cross Gram block, for all child vectors, is

    <J_L u,a_scale J_R v>
        =-r<u,w_L><v,x_R,0>/R_R^2.                        (12)

Equation (11) proves real PSD. The parent body is the convex hull of0 and
ALL displayed gradients. Left values are f_L,i; right values are
a_scale^2 f_R,j. Every within-child and optimum normal is inherited.
For every left sample i and right sample j, the two remaining normals are

    -r<g_R,j,x_R,0><w_L,y_L,i>/R_R^2-<g_L,i,y_L,i>,
    -r<g_L,i,w_L><x_R,0,y_R,j>/R_R^2
        -a_scale^2<g_R,j,y_R,j>.                          (13)

They are nonpositive because of (1) and (5). This checks EVERY cross pair,
including the left endpoint/bridge gradient and any nonzero right residual.
By (2)-(4) there is one actual smooth convex function realizing all data.
Its true initial distance is R_L, since the initial point is still the
positive combination of retained left gradients in (3).

Writing kappa_L,kappa_R for the full input ratios, exact norms give

    kappa_raw=P^2||W||^2/R_L^2=kappa_L kappa_R.            (14)

For future iteration apply (6)-(7) to this ALREADY LEGAL parent. Let
b=beta/||W||^2, where beta is its chosen reset buffer, and let

    sigma_L=<x_L,0,w_L>/(A||w_L||^2),
    sigma_R=<x_R,0,w_R>/(C||w_R||^2).

Using (12) and rP=AC gives the raw correlation
<X_0,W>/(P||W||^2)=-sigma_L sigma_R. Consequently the complete cost law is

    kappa_new=kappa_L kappa_R(1+b)/(1+b kappa_L kappa_R),
    sigma_new=(b-sigma_L sigma_R)/(1+b),
    R_new^2=R_L^2(1+b kappa_L kappa_R),
    Delta_new=R_L^2(kappa_L kappa_R-1).                  (15)

Here beta is the explicit finite maximum (6) for the actual raw parent.
Thus (11)-(15) are a concrete iterable operation with BOTH child defects.
It is not a scalar proxy or a bare matrix feasibility restatement. The
positive product term in Delta_new is present, and so is its normalization
cost. A reset cannot repair illegal cross pairs, because its old/old normals
are unchanged; that is why (5) is imposed BEFORE constructing the raw parent.

One old vertex forces a useful part of this cost. The raw parent retains
G_bridge=J_L w_L. To satisfy the first list in (5) after the reset requires

    beta >= -<G_bridge,W>,
    b >= C sigma_R/r=P sigma_R/A.                        (16)

Indeed (12) gives <G_bridge,W>=-r||w_L||^2
<x_R,0,w_R>/R_R^2 and ||W||^2=r^2||w_L||^2||w_R||^2/R_R^2.
This is a necessary inequality for the stated reset class. Other entries
in (6) may demand larger beta; none can remove (16).

## 4. The exact balanced identical-child iteration loses its relative surplus

Fix one actual input satisfying (1),(5), with kappa_0>1. At each level use
two identical copies of the current complete child and the balanced source
bridge, then ANY finite reset beta satisfying (5). This includes every
choice beta>=the exact maximum (6), with unrestricted retuning at each level.
Set lambda=1+sqrt2, theta=sqrt2-1. Then

    A_(j+1)=lambda A_j, r_j=theta A_j,
    u_j=1/kappa_j=R_j^2/(A_j^2||w_j||^2),
    sigma_j=<x_j,0,w_j>/(A_j||w_j||^2),
    b_j=beta_j/||W_j||^2,

where W_j is the raw endpoint before the jth reset. Equations (15)-(16) give

    u_(j+1)=(u_j^2+b_j)/(1+b_j),
    sigma_(j+1)=(b_j-sigma_j^2)/(1+b_j),
    b_j>=lambda sigma_j.                                (17)

These recurrences were derived from the actual vectors, not substituted for
their legality. The full construction in sections1-3 proves existence for
every finite depth, and every buffer selected by (6) is finite.

For every level, 0<u_j<1 follows from kappa_j>1 and (17). Also
0<sigma_j<1: (3) and the first sign list in (5) give
<x_j,0,w_j>>=||w_j||^2>0; Cauchy--Schwarz gives sigma_j^2<=u_j<1.
In particular a zero starting correlation is not an omitted boundary case
for a positive-defect input of this class.

Define

    F(s)=(lambda s-s^2)/(1+lambda s),
    s_min=min(sigma_0,theta)>0,
    eps_0=(theta-sigma_0)_+,
    c=(1-s_min)/(1+lambda s_min) in[0,1),
    mu=lambda eps_0/2 in[0,1/2).

The map (b-s^2)/(1+b) increases in b, so sigma_(j+1)>=F(sigma_j).
For 0<s<=theta,

    F(s)-s=(lambda+1)s(theta-s)/(1+lambda s)>=0,
    theta-F(s)=(theta-s)(1-s)/(1+lambda s).

For theta<=s<=1,

    F(s)-theta=(s-theta)(1-s)/(1+lambda s)>=0.

These identities prove by induction

    sigma_j>=s_min,
    (theta-sigma_j)_+<=eps_0 c^j,
    b_j>=1-lambda eps_0 c^j.                             (18)

Now set d_j=1-u_j in(0,1), so kappa_j-1=d_j/(1-d_j). From (17)-(18),

    1/d_(j+1)=(1+b_j)/[d_j(2-d_j)]
       >=(1-mu c^j)(1/d_j+1/2).                         (19)

The last step uses 1/[d(1-d/2)]>=1/d+1/2 for 0<d<1.
For 0<=t<=mu<1, log(1-t)>=-t/(1-mu), by integration of -1/(1-t).
Therefore every finite product of the factors in (19) is at least

    P_min=exp[-mu/((1-mu)(1-c))]>0.                      (20)

Iteration of (19), including every inhomogeneous1/2 term, gives

    1/d_j>=P_min(1/d_0+j/2),
    kappa_j-1<=1/[P_min(1/d_0+j/2)-1]                    (21)

whenever the last denominator is positive. Thus kappa_j-1=O(1/j) and
in particular tends to0, uniformly over EVERY sequence of allowed finite
buffers in this class for the fixed seed. If sigma_0>=theta, then P_min=1
and the last upper bound is2/[j+2/d_0-2]. No assumption of minimal buffers
or a favorable interaction sign has been made.

The absolute defect does retain both children:

    Delta_(j+1)=2Delta_j+Delta_j^2/R_j^2,
    R_(j+1)^2=R_j^2[1+b_j(1+Delta_j/R_j^2)^2].            (22)

Nevertheless the necessary distance charge (16) makes the normalized
surplus vanish. This is weaker loss than the previous serial exponential
loss, but it still precludes a fixed positive relative surplus from this
specified identical-child reset operation.

## 5. Actual defective seeds and a leading-order mass change

Here is an explicit seed, including its post-step residuals. Let a>=0,b>1,
H=a+1, z=b-1, xi=z/H, and let U=(1,0), V=(0,sqrt(xi)). Use word(a,b,a),
body conv(0,U,V), and

    x_0=(H+z)U+HV, g_0=U;
    x_1=bU+HV,     g_1=U;
    x_2=HV,        g_2=V;
    x_3=V,         g_3=V.                               (23)

The updates hold. At sample0 the normal against V is -a; at sample1 it
is0. The zero normals at these samples are -(a+b-1) and -(b-1). At
sample2, y_2=aV and the normals against U and0 are -a||V||^2. Sample3
has zero residual. These are all vertex normals. Every pairing in (5)
is nonnegative, and the first post-step residual y_1=zU+HV is nonzero.
The value and exact-distance formulas of section1 apply. Direct expansion
gives, with D=1+3xi+xi^2,

    A=H(2+xi), R^2=H^2 D,
    kappa=xi(2+xi)^2/D,
    kappa-1=(1+xi)(xi-theta)(xi+lambda)/D,
    sigma_0=1/(2+xi).                                   (24)

Thus every xi>theta is an actual positive-defect seed for the ALL-DEPTH
construction of sections2-4. No hypothetical decomposition of a schedule
into children is used.

For a concrete continuation of the detected small word, put
s=sqrt2, p0=(16-11s)/3, q0=(8s-10)/3, 0<t<=1/50,
a=s-p0 t, b=2+q0 t. The rational bounds
1.4142<s<1.4143 give .147<p0<.149 and .437<q0<.439.
Thus a>0 and

    xi-theta=(q0+theta p0)t/(lambda-p0t)>t/5.

Moreover differentiating kappa(xi) gives
kappa'(xi)=1+(2xi+3)/(1+3xi+xi^2)^2>1, so kappa_0>=1+t/5.
The actual capacity is

    A_0(t)=lambda^2+(10s-14)t,
    A_j(t)=lambda^j A_0(t),
    ell_j=4*2^j-1.                                      (25)

Every buffer modifies the witness, not the physical word. Hence the step
word at every depth is explicitly (H_j,1+theta A_j,H_j). Its total mass
relative to t=0 has the fixed leading ratio

    A_j(t)/A_j(0)=1+(10s-14)t/lambda^2>1.                 (26)

This is a genuine leading-order mass perturbation and not the previously
closed weighted common-offset family with only O(n) added mass. Actual
child hypotheses hold at EVERY depth by the construction above. The result
is nevertheless a cost obstruction for this witness operation, not an
improved upper algorithm or an all-schedule lower theorem.

## 6. Matched objective-tail conversion, without another hidden buffer

Take ANY final child satisfying (1),(5), of capacity A, endpoint w and
true distance R. Let the actual finite tail z have nonnegative entries,
total T>=0, and D=1+2T. Choose any d>0, set k=-(d/D)w, and append
bridge1+d followed by exactly that tail. Enlarge the same body to include k.

At every old sample, the k normal is

    <k-g_i,y_i>=-(d/D)<w,y_i>-<g_i,y_i><=0.

At any point v k with v>=1, the normal against an old vertex g_i is

    (v-1)<g_i-k,k>
      =-(v-1)[(d/D)<g_i,w>+(d/D)^2||w||^2]<=0.

The normals against0 and k also hold. Thus every old sample stays legal
and k is the gradient on the entire tail. After the bridge the iterate is
Dk; at a tail partial sum t it is (D-t)k, with D-t>=T+1>=1. At the final
point (T+1)k the exact value is

    2F_final=D||k||^2=d^2||w||^2/D.                       (27)

The true initial distance remains R by (3), because the old positive-cone
representation is retained. For the source dominant bridge choose

    d=(sqrt(D^2+8AD)-D)/4,
    2d^2+Dd=AD,
    B_concat=2A+2d+D=(D+2d)^2/D.                         (28)

The identity d(D+2d)=AD proves d^2/(DA^2)=1/B_concat. Hence the attained
normalized objective for the ACTUAL schedule is exactly

    E_tail=kappa/B_concat.                              (29)

This is the full conversion used here; it needs no C61/C62 premise and no
unrecorded final lift. It allows arbitrary ordering, arbitrary finite
nonnegative tail entries, zero mass, and the empty tail. Both L-scaling and
normalizing the true initial distance follow directly from (2).

For every resulting horizon n=ell+1+length(z), its exact comparison is

    B_n W_n(p,1+d,z)>=kappa B_n/B_concat.                (30)

Nothing replaces B_n by B_concat or by a dyadic asymptotic proxy. For the
iteration in section4, the EXTRA factor kappa tends to1 by (21).
The construction therefore supplies no persistent relative surplus over
its own actual reciprocal-mass witness. The right side of (30) must still
be compared with the exact B_n before drawing any schedule-level conclusion.

For example, with an empty objective tail, B_concat(A)=2A+1/2+sqrt(1+8A)/2.
Equation (25) gives B_concat(A_j(t))/B_concat(A_j(0)) tending to the fixed
ratio (26). This identifies the leading mass increase; it does NOT assert
that the unmodified split attains the optimizing B_n at those horizons.

## 7. Full-model calibration and the empty-child boundary

If both child ratios are1, (15) gives kappa_new=1 for EVERY buffer. The
orthogonal seed at t=0 has kappa_0=1 by (24); all its reset joins preserve1,
and (29) gives exactly1/B_concat, without a fictitious positive surplus.

There is also a complete exact benchmark calibration. With an empty core
x_0=w=1, A=1, an empty tail D=1 gives d=1/2, schedule(3/2), B_1=4,
K=[-1/2,1], and attained error1/4. On the ORIGINAL formal vectors
(x_0,g_0,g_1), using x_1=x_0-(3/2)g_0, direct expansion gives

    ||x_0||^2/4-2f_1+Q_(1,0)+Q_(0,*)+Q_(1,*)
       =||x_0-2g_0-2g_1||^2/4.                          (31)

Every multiplier is1 and the full remainder is PSD, with exact value flow.
The recurrence in accepted b98bb807d514e80d gives B_1=4, so (31) and the
constructed function match the exact known benchmark. At horizon0,
||x_0||^2-2f_0+2Q_(0,*)=||x_0-g_0||^2 gives B_0=1 as well.

Empty and zero-mass children are allowed in the general construction:
A=1, x_i=g_i=w, y_i=0 and kappa=1. Formula (6) has no division by zero,
and sections2-3 still check all actual samples. A positive-defect seed in
section4 is necessarily nonempty and has positive mass. Its loss statement
concerns repeated IDENTICAL full children and resetting (5) at each level.
It does not apply to serial addition of empty right blocks without those
resets. Indeed, starting at1 on the function with projection body[-1,1]
and word(2), the endpoint is-1, A=3, Delta=8. Serial empty-right joins with
r_j(A_j+1+r_j)=A_j and no buffer keep all residuals zero and A_j^2 w_j^2=9
while the true initial distance stays1. Their constant relative defect8
does not contradict (21): they are not the balanced identical-child reset
operation and do not satisfy its retained sign condition (5).

## 8. What this closes and what is still missing

This package gives a full legal, real, arbitrary-depth two-child construction,
an exact interaction/normalization law, and a necessary old-vertex cost
that makes its relative surplus O(1/depth). It retains a growing collection
of modified blocks, nonzero post-step residuals, values and the global
closest-minimizer distance. It is a substantive improvement over a serial
lift's geometric dilution, but it still has no fixed asymptotic gap.

Leaving this obstruction requires retaining signed boundary data rather than
forcing all four lists (5), changing the cross-correlation operation, using
nonidentical/unequal children outside the class of section4, or changing the
core interface itself. A transverse correlation change alone cannot repair
the particular planar bridge normal recorded in the separate round18 draft.
None of these remaining mechanisms is proved here. A failed lower gluing
rule gives neither an all-h lower theorem nor an improving full upper
certificate. The original target, with every horizon and every nonnegative
schedule compared to the exact B_n, remains open.
